HIGH = danger/red, LOW = success/green, ASSESSMENT INCOMPLETE (a real risk_level value) = warning/amber. Badge alignment was tightened by making the cell nowrap so badges cannot wrap unpredictably. Risk values and assessment logic are unchanged.
- Table alignment: Patient column given a sensible min-width; Visit Date, BP, Weight, GA, Risk, Next Visit, and Actions headers/cells marked whitespace-nowrap so dates and GA never wrap. Column order and design are unchanged. BP cells additionally kept nowrap and mono font.

- git diff --check: clean.

Design defense: both pages now rely on the Phase 1/2 design tokens and the shared status-badge component, so badges render identically to the rest of the system. Keeping dates and GA on single lines prevents mid-value wrapping that can be misread during a quick scan, while a one-line `Aug 14, 2026` format is consistent with the rest of the UI. The GA fallback guard preserves the nullable gestational_age contract. On referrals, removing the TOTAL card and balancing the four status cells makes the queue state legible at a glance; Pending retains a subtle warning emphasis (pill) without a structurally distinct card, so the summary is uniform. ## Sprint 17J — Patient Profile Context-Aware Back Button (from Pregnancy Outcome Monitoring)

Status: Complete

Scope: Navigation/UI improvement only. The hardcoded "View Monitoring Page" button on the patient profile's Pregnancy Outcome card is now a context-aware "Back" button that restores the exact Pregnancy Outcome Monitoring view the user came from (state filter, search, pagination page). The redundant "Pregnancy History" button inside the same card was removed for confirmed-delivery records. No monitoring logic, deriveState(), delivery logic, database, clinical rules, or assessment logic was touched.

### How the return URL is passed
- `resources/views/pregnancy-outcomes/index.blade.php`: the page builds one return URL per request — `route('pregnancy-outcomes.index', request()->query())` — which preserves every current monitoring query parameter (state slug, search, page, etc.). All four profile-opening links (desktop patient name, desktop "View Record"/"Open Profile", mobile patient name, mobile "View Record"/"Open Profile") now append it as `?return=<encoded monitoring URL>`.

### How it is validated
- `app/Http/Controllers/PatientController.php` (`show()`): the `return` query parameter is resolved through a new private helper `resolveMonitoringReturnUrl()`. It is accepted only when: it is a non-empty string; its scheme is http/https; its host matches the application host (from `route('pregnancy-outcomes.index')`); and its path is `/pregnancy-outcomes` (or the monitoring root). Anything else — missing, malformed, external, `javascript:`/non-http scheme, or an internal-but-wrong-path URL — is ignored and the safe fallback is used. No open-redirect vector is possible.

### What query/filter state is preserved
- `state` (e.g. `resolved`, `confirmation-required`, `still-pregnant`, `unable-to-contact`), `search`, `page`, and any other current monitoring query parameters survive the round trip and are restored when "Back" is clicked.

### Safe fallback
- `resources/views/patients/show.blade.php`: when no valid return URL is present (profile opened directly, or the return was rejected), the Back button falls back to the base `route('pregnancy-outcomes.index')` — the plain monitoring page, matching the previous hardcoded destination. The button is never broken.

### UI changes in the Pregnancy Outcome card
- The "Pregnancy History" button inside the card was removed (confirmed-delivery records now show only "Back"); the separate "View Pregnancy History" action in the normal patient header is unchanged, and the delivered-history page/route is untouched.
- The button label is simply "Back" using the existing `btn btn-secondary` style.

### Verification (tests/Feature/PregnancyOutcomeProfileBackNavigationTest.php, 12 tests)
- Monitoring links carry the full context (state + search + page) as a return URL; page 2 verified with a 20-patient dataset.
- Back restores each monitoring view: Confirmed Delivery (`resolved`), Outcome Confirmation Required (`confirmation-required`), Still Pregnant — Confirmed (`still-pregnant`), Unable to Contact (`unable-to-contact`).
- Search query and pagination page survive the profile round trip.
- Direct profile open uses the plain monitoring page fallback.
- External domain, `javascript:` scheme, and internal-but-wrong-path return URLs are all rejected with the fallback used.
- The card's Pregnancy History button is gone (delivered-history link appears exactly once — the header) while the header's "View Pregnancy History" remains; the delivered-history page still renders.
- `php artisan test`: 720 passed, 2 failed — the 2 are the pre-existing unrelated baseline failures (ExampleTest guest 302-vs-200, ProfileTest soft-delete).
- `npm run build`: succeeded. `php artisan view:cache`: clean. `git diff --check`: clean.

Design defense: browser-history-only back links are unreliable (refresh, new tab, direct URL); an application-controlled return URL guarantees the exact monitoring context is restored. The validator keeps the return strictly internal and monitoring-scoped, so there is no open-redirect surface and direct profile access always gets a sensible destination. This sprint was purely navigational; no clinical or routing behavior changed.

PHASE 17J PATIENT PROFILE CONTEXT-AWARE BACK COMPLETE - AWAITING HUMAN ACCEPTANCE

## Sprint 17K — Risk Monitoring Dashboard Risk Type Selector and Analytics Reorder

Status: Complete

Scope: Dashboard analytics selection/display improvement only. The Risk Analytics section on the Risk Monitoring page now has a compact "Risk Type" selector (High Risk default / Low Risk) sitting beside the existing Month selector. The same single trend chart is reused to show either the monthly HIGH or monthly LOW assessment trend, and its title changes dynamically to "High-Risk Patients" or "Low-Risk Patients". The Risk Analytics section (selectors + trend chart + Highest High-Risk Month / Most Common Condition summary cards) now sits above the other analytics graphs, which were moved into their own "Risk Analytics Breakdown" section below it. No clinical rules, classification, ML behavior, assessment completeness, decision-source logic, patient/prenatal records, database schema, or migrations were touched.

### Backend changes
- `app/Services/RiskAnalyticsService.php` (`get()`): now accepts an optional `?string $riskType`. Only `HIGH`/`LOW` are allowed; anything else (including `null`) defaults to `HIGH`. The response gains `riskType`, `riskTrend` (the series for the selected type), and `lowRiskTrend`. Existing keys (`highRiskTrend`, `riskDistribution`, `conditions`, `bpFollowUp`, `summary`) are preserved unchanged so the breakdown charts and summary cards behave exactly as before.
- `app/Http/Controllers/RiskMonitoringController.php`: new private `riskTypeFilter()` helper — only `LOW` is honored, everything else defaults to `HIGH`. Both `index()` and the JSON `analytics()` endpoint read `risk_type` and forward it to the service.

### Frontend changes (`resources/views/risk/monitoring.blade.php`)
- Risk Analytics header now holds `Risk Type [High Risk ▼]` beside `Month [All Months ▼]`, sharing the same form/select styling and wrapping together on small screens.
- The trend chart title is dynamic (`High-Risk Patients` / `Low-Risk Patients`; the fixed "by Month" suffix is gone) and the line color follows the selected type (red for HIGH, emerald for LOW).
- The trend chart + summary cards stay in the main "Risk Analytics" section; Risk Distribution by Month, Maternal Conditions by Month, and BP Follow-Up by Month now render below in a new "Risk Analytics Breakdown" panel — still above the patient list.
- The Month selector keeps working and composes with Risk Type; both fire the same `loadAnalytics()` fetch (now sending `risk_type` too).
- The HIGH/LOW/ASSESSMENT INCOMPLETE summary cards, the statistics counters, the patient list, and the system-info card are unchanged.

### Verification (tests/Feature/RiskAnalyticsRiskTypeTest.php, 7 tests)
- Default risk type is HIGH and `riskTrend` equals `highRiskTrend`; LOW returns the low trend; any other value (e.g. `MEDIUM`) is rejected and defaults to HIGH.
- Month + risk type compose correctly in the service and via the JSON endpoint.
- The monitoring page renders the selector defaulting to High Risk, the dynamic title, and the reordered sections (trend before breakdown; breakdown contains the distribution/BP charts).
- Summary cards and counters remain present.
- `php artisan test`: 727 passed, 2 failed — the 2 are the pre-existing unrelated baseline failures (ExampleTest guest 302-vs-200, ProfileTest soft-delete). Full RiskAnalyticsTest suite still green.
- `npm run build`: succeeded. `php artisan view:cache`: clean. `git diff --check`: clean.

Design defense: reusing the single trend chart with a data/type switch keeps the dashboard uncluttered and avoids duplicating Chart.js instances, while limiting `risk_type` to HIGH/LOW keeps the selector strictly a visualization choice — it can never affect risk classification or assessment output. Keeping the summary cards on the HIGH series preserves their existing meaning ("Highest High-Risk Month"), and isolating the other graphs into their own section lets the primary trend chart surface first without removing any existing analytics.

## Sprint 18 — In-App Notifications for Clinical Events + Profile Access/Header Hardening

Status: Complete

Scope: Notification infrastructure + two defensive work streams. Part 1 adds a database-backed in-app notification system driven by real persisted clinical events: an urgent BP visit, a repeat-BP-pending visit, referral creation, and referral closure each generate exactly one notification per recipient — never on page render. Part 2 hardens the authenticated header so the user-menu link always resolves to the logged-in user's own profile (with explicit regression tests that staff cannot escalate to admin via profile/password tampering).

### Backend changes
- `database/migrations/2026_08_20_000001_create_notifications_table.php`: the standard `notifications` table (id, type, notifiable morph, data, read_at, timestamps) with indexes on `notifiable_type`/`notifiable_id`/`read_at`.
- `app/Notifications/{UrgentBloodPressureNotification,PendingRepeatBloodPressureNotification,ReferralCreatedNotification,ReferralClosedNotification}.php`: database-channel (`['database']`) notifications only; each builds a structured `data` payload (`type`, `title`, `message`, `action_label`, `destination` route name + parameters) so tray links are resolved at render time with current routing, never stored absolute URLs.
- `app/Services/SystemNotificationService.php`: single entry point for generating notifications strictly from post-persistence events. `adminRecipients()` targets all active admins; `clinicalRecipients()` targets the patient's assigned staff (when assigned) plus admins. Fix: recipient builder returns `Illuminate\Support\Collection` (the original `Eloquent\Collection` return type caused a live `TypeError` — `collect()->unique()` yields a base collection — that silently aborted the send; surfaced by the trigger tests and corrected).
- `app/Http/Controllers/NotificationController.php`: `markAsRead` and `markAllAsRead` are self-scoped — they operate only on the authenticated user's own notifications via `where('id', ...)->where('notifiable...')` guards. Both under `auth` middleware; unauthorized/foreign targets 404.
- `app/Http/Controllers/PrenatalVisitController.php` (`store`): after the transaction commits, a visit with `urgency === 'URGENT_CLINICAL_REVIEW'` fires `notifyUrgentBloodPressure` and a `bp_verification_status === 'PENDING_REPEAT'` fires `notifyPendingRepeatBloodPressure`. Firing only in `store` (never from `update`, which already guards each state, and never from any render) keeps the one-event guarantee.
- `app/Http/Controllers/ReferralController.php`: `store` notifies admins of a newly created pending referral (`notifyReferralCreated`, acting staff excluded); `complete`/`refuse`/`cancel` notify admins of terminal closure (`notifyReferralClosed`).
- `app/View/Composers/NotificationComposer.php` + `app/Providers/AppServiceProvider.php`: registers the generic (non-cached) View Composer so the dashboard tray gets `notifications` (latest 8) and `unreadNotificationCount` without per-sprint controller leaks. Reverted from cached (`view()->composer`) because cached profiles/dashboard record stale models.

### Frontend changes
- `resources/views/layouts/app.blade.php` header: the dead notification `<a href="#">` was replaced with a real `<button id="notifBell">` (no scroll-to-top fake). The profile dropdown "Account" link now always points to the current user (no fixed `/users/<id>` or `href="#"` placeholders), keeping admins and staff signed in to their own profile.

### Verification (tests/Feature/NotificationTest.php 13 tests, ProfileAccessHeaderTest.php 11 tests)
- Display/scoping: users see only their own unread count, own tray rows, and read/read-all mutations affect only the authenticated user; guest access to notification routes redirects to login; zero-notification state renders safely; dashboard renders never create/duplicate notifications.
- Event triggers: urgent 165/110 visit POST notifies assigned staff + admins exactly once (both urgent and pending-repeat); non-urgent visit notifies nobody; an already-urgent `update` does not duplicate; referral creation notifies admins but not the acting staff; referral closure notifies admins.
- Profile access: admin/staff reach their own profile; guest blocked; profile and password updates preserve the caller's role and cannot escalate a staff user to admin; profile update changes only the current authenticated user; email uniqueness preserves the user's own address; header links to the authenticated user's profile; logout behavior unchanged.
- Found and fixed during testing: the `SystemNotificationService` collection return-type `TypeError` described above.
- `php artisan test`: 751 passed, 2 failed — the 2 are the pre-existing unrelated baseline failures (ExampleTest guest 302-vs-200, ProfileTest soft-delete), unchanged and documented since Sprint 17K. `php artisan view:cache`: clean. `php -l` clean on every touched file.

Design defense: notifications are written only from post-persist hooks so the dashboard is a pure read path — re-renders can never multiply notifications and `Notification::fake()` unit tests can assert exact-once semantics. Tray links store route names + parameters (not URLs) so destinations stay correct if routing changes. The bell stays an inert-but-visible button (no client behavior) rather than a fake link, and the user menu resolves the profile URL at render from the authenticated user to remove any staff->admin or wrong-user navigation path. All recipients are server-derived (assigned staff + role-checked admins), so acting staff and foreign users can never be targeted or forged.

## Sprint 19 — Staff Account Ownership / Administration Boundary

Status: Complete

Scope: Enforce and prove the Admin-only account lifecycle. Staff never administers accounts — it can only manage its own name/email/password on `/profile`. All Manage Staff routes (index/create/store/edit/update/destroy) are Admin-only at the controller level; self-service account deletion through the generic profile page is removed at both the UI and route layers; staff creation keeps `role` pinned server-side to `staff`; staff edit cannot touch role; and profile updates cannot modify role or account-administration fields.

### Backend changes
- `app/Http/Controllers/StaffController.php`: already guarded — every route (`index`, `create`, `store`, `edit`, `update`, `destroy`) begins with `checkAdmin()` (guest or non-admin → `403`). Re-verified unchanged. `store()` validates name/email, password `min:6`, hashes with `Hash::make`, and hard-codes `role => 'staff'` (never reads `role` from the request); `update()` only applies `name`/`email` (with uniqueness ignoring the current id) and never exposes a role field; `destroy()` is Admin-only and soft-deletes.
- `app/Http/Controllers/ProfileController.php`: `destroy()` (the Breeze self-delete endpoint) removed; the unused `Auth` import dropped.
- `routes/web.php`: `DELETE /profile` (`profile.destroy`) removed. The `/profile` path still serves GET (edit) and PATCH (update); a DELETE request now returns `405 Method Not Allowed`, so neither Staff nor Admin can self-delete through the generic profile page, and no legitimate feature depends on that route.
- `app/Http/Controllers/Auth/AuthenticatedSessionController.php`: unchanged — login still routes admin/staff to the dashboard.
- Self-service password change remains available only through the existing Breeze `PUT /password` (`PasswordController`), which acts strictly on the authenticated user and cannot touch role.

### Frontend changes
- `resources/views/profile/partials/delete-user-form.blade.php`: partial deleted.
- `resources/views/profile/edit.blade.php`: the "Delete Account" card removed entirely; the profile page now offers only Update Profile Information and Update Password.
- Sidebar: the "Manage Staff" item was already admin-only (`auth()->user()->role === 'admin'`); kept as-is — the controller `checkAdmin()` is the authoritative control, the hidden sidebar link is only cosmetic.

### Verification (tests/Feature/StaffAccountAdministrationBoundaryTest.php, 13 tests; ProfileTest reworked)
1. Admin can open Manage Staff (`staff.index` → 200, renders staff rows).
2. Staff receives `403` opening Manage Staff.
3. Admin can create a Staff account (`staff.store` → 302 + persisted `role = staff`).
4. Submitted `role=admin` during staff creation still yields a `staff` account and does not increase the admin count.
5. Created password is hashed (`Hash::check` passes; plaintext ≠ stored).
6. Duplicate email is rejected (`assertSessionHasErrors('email')`, count unchanged).
7. Staff cannot access `staff.create` (403).
8. Staff cannot call `staff.store` (403, and the attacker's user row is never created).
9. Staff cannot edit another staff account (403 on GET and PUT; target unchanged).
10. Staff cannot delete another staff account (403; target still exists).
11. Staff cannot delete their own account through `/profile` (`405`, still authenticated, row intact).
12. Admin can still remove Staff through Manage Staff (`staff.destroy` → 302 + `assertSoftDeleted`).
13. Profile update cannot modify role or account-administration fields (`role=admin`/`approved`/`is_administrator` posted → role stays `staff`, admin count stays 0).
- `ProfileTest` reworked: the two stock Breeze delete tests replaced with "self-service account deletion disabled at the route layer" (staff) and "disabled for admins too" — both assert `405`, still authenticated, row intact. This also removes the long-standing ProfileTest soft-delete baseline failure.
- `php artisan test`: 765 passed, 1 failed — the single remaining failure is the pre-existing unrelated `ExampleTest` (guest `GET /` 302-vs-200), unchanged since earlier sprints. `php artisan view:cache`: clean. `php -l` clean on every touched file.

Design defense: the boundary is enforced at the controller (authorization) and route (method) layers, never by hiding menu links — a direct request from a staff session to any staff resource route hits `checkAdmin()` and returns `403`. Staff creation pins `role => 'staff'` server-side and only reads `name`/`email`/`password` from the request, so privileged-field tampering is structurally impossible; `create()` uses explicit attribute arrays instead of mass assignment of arbitrary request data. Because self-deletion is not part of the intended account lifecycle (removal is exclusive to Admin via Manage Staff), removing the endpoint entirely is safer than guarding it: there is no reachable code path by which Staff — or an Admin distracted on the generic profile page — could self-delete, while Admin's authorized Manage Staff deletion is untouched and covered by a test. Profile updates go through `ProfileUpdateRequest`, which validates only `name`/`email`, so roles and any future account-administration columns can never be written from profile/password requests.

## Sprint 19B — Profile UI/UX Redesign (Presentation Only)

Status: Complete

Scope: UI/UX redesign of `/profile` only. The Sprint 18/19 backend and security behavior is untouched — the same routes, controllers, forms, and validation handle the same submissions exactly as before. The default Laravel/Breeze appearance (dark charcoal cards, generic spacing) was replaced with the application's light, centered design language.

### Backend / Route / Database / Security changes
None. No controller, route, model, middleware, migration, service, or authorization logic changed. The self-scoped profile update (`PATCH /profile`), password update (`PUT /password`), role-escalation protection, and Admin-only staff administration remain byte-for-byte identical.

### Frontend changes (3 Blade presentation files only)
- `resources/views/profile/edit.blade.php`: sets the layout header to `Profile` (topbar title + `Home › Profile` breadcrumb), centers the workspace in a `max-w-3xl` column, and adds a display-only Profile Summary card (initials avatar, full name, email, and a subtle `STAFF`/`ADMIN` status badge). A small vanilla-JS block drives the show/hide password toggles for all three password fields (independent, `type="button"`, keyboard accessible with `aria-label`/`aria-pressed`).
- `resources/views/profile/partials/update-profile-information-form.blade.php`: card restyled to the system language (`bg-white rounded-2xl border-gray-100 shadow-sm`, `bg-gray-50` section header) — "Account Information / Update your personal account details." Full Name + Email Address sit side-by-side on desktop (`sm:grid-cols-2`) and stack on mobile. Inputs use the app's `h-10`, focus ring, and per-field error state (subtle red border + readable error text + `aria-describedby`). Button relabelled to "Save Changes". Success shows a green "✓ Profile information updated successfully." chip using the existing `session('status')` and an Alpine fade, never `alert()`.
- `resources/views/profile/partials/update-password-form.blade.php`: card restyled as "Password & Security / Keep your account protected with a secure password." Current Password occupies a full row; New Password and Confirm New Password share a row on desktop and stack on mobile. All three inputs get an inline eye toggle that switches `password` ↔ `text` independently without submitting. Button relabelled to "Update Password". Success chip "✓ Password updated successfully." The dead Breeze email-verification block (User does not implement `MustVerifyEmail`) was dropped.

### Verification
- Relevant suites green: ProfileTest (5), ProfileAccessHeaderTest (11), StaffAccountAdministrationBoundaryTest (13), NotificationTest (13) — 42 passed, 126 assertions.
- `php artisan test`: 765 passed, 1 failed — the single failure is the pre-existing unrelated `ExampleTest` (guest `GET /` 302-vs-200), identical to the Sprint 19 baseline; not touched.
- `php artisan view:cache`: clean. `git diff --check`: clean. No migrations run.

Design defense: the redesign reuses the application's own tokens and components (`bg-white`/`border-gray-100` cards, `bg-gray-50` card headers, `status-badge`, `btn-primary`, `input-label`/`input-error`, primary `#2563eb` focus ring) rather than inventing a new system. The page stays a read/self-service surface: role is rendered as a badge only (no role input), self-deletion remains absent, and every form still posts to its existing named route (`profile.update`, `password.update`) with unchanged validation. Password visibility is pure client-side presentation — the backend still receives the same `type="password"` field values — so no validation or security behavior changes. Inputs are intentionally explicit (controlled error-border classes) so the required field-level validation UX is deterministic, while labels/errors still come from the shared components.

One unexpected stored risk value remains untouched.

Tests cover backfills, ties, deletion, status/assignment scopes, overdue totals above five and month/year bounds. Changed PHP files pass syntax checks. No browser verification; existing unrelated whitespace warnings remain.

## Decimal Gestational Age Auto-Fill (2026-10-01)

- Added `GestationalAgeCalculator` for elapsed LMP-to-clinical-date days divided by seven and rounded to one decimal, with reversed dates rejected.

- Prenatal Visit CREATE/EDIT and Ultrasound CREATE/EDIT now receive a server-calculated initial GA and expected-GA helper text. The shared Blade auto-fill script recalculates on relevant date and patient changes, preserves old input on initial render, and keeps GA manually editable until those inputs change.
- LMP is supplied from the existing locked patient hidden input, dropdown option, or the record's patient on edit. Patient locking/session behavior was not changed.
- Reversed dates and calculated GA outside 4–42 are not auto-filled. Existing ±3-week tolerance and other clinical rules are unchanged.
- Verification: 12 targeted tests passed (72 assertions), covering all four rendered forms, locked profile LMP, old-input precedence, missing LMP, reversed dates, out-of-range GA, and decimal storage/context. Direct browser-script event simulation passed for initial display, manual edit, date/patient changes, old input, missing LMP, reversed dates, and range guards. Migration apply/rollback/reapply and Blade view compilation passed.
- Defense notes: Calculate initial values server-side with the shared service so the input and helper are present in the first response; use one shared client script only for recalculation after user changes. This avoids four independent page-load implementations while preserving the locked-patient flow.

- Left `action-buttons.blade.php`, Prenatal Visit archive behavior, login logic, relationships, and foreign keys unchanged.
- Verification: Staff Account Administration and Prenatal Visit Archive workflows passed (24 tests, 125 assertions); changed PHP files pass `php -l`, staff routes were confirmed by `route:list`, and `git diff --check` passed.

## Admin-Triggered Staff Password Reset (2026-10-02)

- Added an authenticated POST `staff.reset-account` endpoint that uses Laravel's existing `Password::sendResetLink()` broker for the active staff model's registered email. Admin authorization, active-user model binding, and the exact `staff` role are required; archived and non-staff targets are rejected.
- Added a per-row Reset Account icon and confirmation dialog to Manage Staff. The dialog shows the selected staff name and registered email; Cancel clears the selected form action, and Send Reset Link submits only after confirmation.
- Broker status handling provides distinct success, throttling, and generic failure feedback. A safe `PASSWORD_RESET_REQUESTED` audit entry is written only after the broker reports success and contains the staff name/ID, not the token or URL.
- Reused the existing reset notification, token table, expiry, reset form, and password-reset controller. No custom token system or migration was added, and the admin never receives a reset token or URL.
- Verification: focused reset, Staff Account Administration, and existing password-reset suites passed (33 tests, 163 assertions); PHP syntax checks, reset-route registration, and `git diff --check` passed. Tests use `Notification::fake()` and PHPUnit's in-memory SQLite configuration; no real email or development/production database was involved.
- Defense notes: resolve the target by route-bound User and derive the destination email from that persisted model, preventing client-supplied email/role values from changing the reset recipient or privilege. The reset request only issues a link and does not mutate staff or assigned patient data.

## ML Runtime Configuration Repair (2026-10-06)

- Root cause: the local `PYTHON_PATH` pointed to another developer's machine. Its fallback resolved Windows' Microsoft Store `python.exe` alias, so ML prediction returned "Python was not found" and complete assessments remained `ASSESSMENT INCOMPLETE`.
- Recreated the ignored `maternal-risk-ml/venv` with Python 3.12 and installed the exact package versions in `maternal-risk-ml/requirements.txt`. Python 3.12 is used because the pinned pandas 2.2.3 package has no compatible wheel for this machine's Python 3.14, and no compiler is configured.
- Pointed this checkout's ignored `.env` at its local virtualenv interpreter. Cleared the developer-specific path from `.env.example`; leaving it blank lets the existing service resolve the project virtualenv.
- Made Python resolution hosting-safe: read `PYTHON_PATH` through Laravel config (so it works with cached config), recognize both Windows and Linux virtualenv executable paths, and use `python3` as the Unix PATH fallback. Hosting setup must install the pinned requirements on the server and set `PYTHON_PATH` to that server's absolute interpreter path.
- Clinical thresholds, feature order, model artifact, and assessment rules are unchanged. Once a prediction succeeds, the existing recalculation updates saved incomplete assessments; HIGH and LOW records remain protected from automatic rewrites.
- Verification: `predict.py` returned LOW and HIGH with representative inputs. After the hosting-aware config changes, the focused MachineLearningService and PatientAssessmentRecalculationService suites passed (20 tests, 62 assertions) under the guarded SQLite `:memory:` configuration, with no config cache present; both changed PHP files pass syntax checks.
- Defense notes: keep the environment-specific absolute path local and ignored; use the existing project virtualenv resolution for other checkouts rather than committing a machine-specific path or changing pinned model dependencies. Read the variable from config/services.php so Laravel config caching does not discard it.

## Prenatal Visit Trimester-Based Field Visibility (2026-10-04)

- Added one shared Blade component, `resources/views/components/trimester-visibility.blade.php`, that reads the existing `gestational_age` input and toggles a single CSS class on marked wrappers. It is included in both `prenatal_visits/create.blade.php` and `prenatal_visits/edit.blade.php` directly after the existing GA autofill include.
- Trimester classification uses decimal weeks and never rounds or recalculates: 1.0 to 12.9 is the 1st trimester, 13.0 to 27.9 is the 2nd, 28.0 and above is the 3rd; blank, invalid or GA below 1.0 is not classified, which hides all trimester-specific fields and leaves always-visible fields shown.
- Visibility matrix: `fundic_height`, `fetal_heart_tone` and `fetal_movement` carry `data-trimester-visible="2,3"`; the Clinical Examination section wrapper and its `presenting_part`, `uterine_activity`, `cervical_dilation` and `bag_of_water` fields carry `data-trimester-visible="3"`. Hypertension, diabetes, anemia, gestational age and every other field carry no marker and stay visible in all trimesters.
- Hiding is display-only. The component only runs `classList.toggle('trimester-hidden', ...)`, so controlled inputs are never removed, disabled, cleared or renamed and keep submitting their values, which protects historical data on Edit.
- Added a display-only `#trimester_indicator` paragraph beside the existing GA hint on both forms. It has no `name`, so it is never posted.
- Visibility recomputes on GA `input` and `change`, and after the existing GA autofill writes a value on visit-date or patient change (deferred with `setTimeout(..., 0)` so the autofill write happens first). One initial call runs as the component script loads.
- No controller, service, validation rule, migration, clinical threshold or risk logic was changed. `store()` and `update()` already declare all seven controlled fields `nullable`.
- Verification: new `tests/Feature/PrenatalVisitTrimesterVisibilityTest.php` (9 tests, 81 assertions) passed, covering marker counts on both forms, always-visible fields having no trimester ancestor, no controlled field being disabled/renamed/emptied, historical values still rendered on Edit, the visual-only component contract, update persistence while the posted GA hides those fields, and the existing nullable validation rules. The classification test extracts `classifyTrimester()` from the shipped component and executes it under Node against the boundary table (1.0, 12.9, 13.0, 27.9, 28.0, 42.0, 0.9, -3, empty, non-numeric, null). Prenatal suites: 73 passed and 5 failed, and all 5 failures are pre-existing index-page assertions that were reproduced with the HEAD versions of both edited views.
- Defense notes: one classifier and one visibility-update function avoid duplicated trimester logic across event listeners; reading the existing GA input instead of deriving GA from LMP keeps this feature from introducing a second clinical calculation; markers are placed on wrappers rather than inputs so the submitted payload is untouched; the Clinical Examination section wrapper is marked third-trimester only so an empty section header is not left on screen.
- Pre-existing issues found and deliberately not fixed: `PrenatalVisitsActiveListingTest` and `PrenatalVisitsLongRiskUiTest` contradict each other about the index Edit link and both fail at HEAD; full-suite runs also report SQLite-incompatible `YEAR()` SQL in `DashboardController` plus unrelated 403/500 failures; `PrenatalVisitController` registers no `show()` method although `Route::resource` does; `public/hot` remains an untracked Vite dev artifact that is not gitignored.

## Prenatal Trimester Indicator + Patient Profile Trimester Display (2026-10-04)

- Part 1 (Prenatal Visit Create/Edit): the display-only trimester indicator is now a small uppercase "Trimester" label above a neutral chip (`inline-flex`, `rounded-full`, `px-3 py-1.5`, `text-sm font-semibold`) instead of a 12px text line, so it reads at the same weight as the surrounding form. It sits directly under the existing GA hint inside the GA wrapper and wraps safely on narrow screens.
- The indicator still has no `name` attribute and is never submitted. `classifyTrimester()`, the trimester boundaries, the `data-trimester-visible` matrix, the hide/show behaviour and every event wiring are unchanged; the only script change is that the indicator node now receives the bare label (`3rd Trimester` / em dash) because the `Trimester: ` prefix moved into the markup.
- Part 2 (Patient Profile): the existing Gestational Age card inside "Pregnancy at a Glance" now renders two columns, GESTATIONAL AGE and TRIMESTER, using the same label and value typography, with `grid-cols-1` stacking below `sm`. No card was added, no row was added, and the panel header and surrounding grid are untouched.
- Single source: `$profileGestationalAge = $latestPregnancyVisit?->gestational_age` (the newest prenatal visit's stored GA, which is what the card has always displayed) feeds BOTH columns through one variable. The trimester is never re-derived from LMP and never taken from a different visit.
- New `app/Support/TrimesterClassifier.php`: a presentation-only static helper mirroring the existing `App\Support\WeightFormatter`, consumed in the profile `@php` block via its FQN. It returns `1st/2nd/3rd Trimester` or null, and null is rendered as an em dash rather than a guess. The browser classifier in `trimester-visibility.blade.php` remains the single place where classification runs for the forms; no container, controller or architecture change was needed.
- Boundaries are identical on both sides and were not changed: `1.0 <= GA < 13.0` -> 1st, `13.0 <= GA < 28.0` -> 2nd, `GA >= 28.0` -> 3rd, with no rounding before classification (12.9 -> 1st, 13.0 -> 2nd, 27.9 -> 2nd, 28.0 -> 3rd).
- Pregnancy state: the trimester column mirrors the GA column exactly for ONGOING and DELIVERED patients alike, because it classifies the value already on screen. When GA is missing the card keeps "Not recorded" and shows an em dash instead of a trimester.
- No gestational-age calculation, validation, risk, clinical or database change was made. No trimester column, migration or field exists, nothing trimester-related is submitted, and `app.css` / `public/build` were not touched because every utility class used already exists in the committed build assets.
- Verification: new `tests/Feature/PatientProfileTrimesterDisplayTest.php` (10 tests) plus the extended `tests/Feature/PrenatalVisitTrimesterVisibilityTest.php` (now 11 tests) and `GestationalAgeAutofillTest` - 29 tests, 232 assertions, all passing. Profile and prenatal regression suites show only pre-existing failures (17 across the profile test files and 5 index-page assertions), reproduced identically with the HEAD version of `patients/show.blade.php`.
- Defense notes: one variable feeding both profile columns makes it structurally impossible for the trimester to disagree with the displayed GA; keeping the server helper a static presentation class avoids any service-container or controller refactor; keeping the chip's value node nameless leaves the prenatal submit payload byte-identical to before.

## Login Left-Side Translucent Green Readability Panel (2026-10-05)

- UI-only change confined to `resources/views/layouts/guest.blade.php`. One new `.brand-panel` CSS rule wraps the existing left-side branding content (the wrapper div on the left panel now carries `brand-panel` instead of `max-w-sm`), so all eight lines of left copy sit on a single cohesive translucent panel instead of separate boxes.
- Panel appearance: `rgba(16, 74, 46, 0.68)` (a deepened tint of the existing DEPLA dark green `#367E4B`), `border-radius: 26px`, `padding: 2.5rem 2.25rem`, `max-width: 32rem` centered by the section's existing `items-center justify-center`, a `1px rgba(255,255,255,0.10)` hairline, a soft `0 14px 40px rgba(8,36,23,0.22)` shadow, and `backdrop-filter: blur(8px)`. Opacity stays inside the requested 55-70% band, so the maternity photo remains visibly recognizable through the panel rather than looking solid.
- Contrast-only color updates on the left copy: clinic-name h1 to `#FFFFFF`, subtitle to `#EAFFF2`, quote to `#F0FDF4`, location to `rgba(255,255,255,0.90)`, feature lines to `#F0F7F2` (`.feature-item` is used only by this panel), copyright to `rgba(255,255,255,0.75)`. The feature-icon chip changed from a translucent white with a blue border to `rgba(255,255,255,0.92)` with a white hairline; the DEPLA green SVG strokes, wording, fonts and icons are unchanged.
- Responsive: h1 inline `font-size` became `clamp(2rem, 3.2vw, 3rem)` and a `max-width: 1280px` rule reduces panel padding to `2rem 1.75rem` and radius to `22px`. The left section remains `hidden lg:flex`, so mobile keeps the current behavior and no new horizontal scrolling was introduced.
- No routes, controllers, database, validation, authentication, remember-me, forgot-password, password-visibility JS or right-side login card code was touched, and no new Tailwind utility classes, JS dependencies or CSS frameworks were added (all new styling lives in the existing inline `<style>` block).
- Verification: `bootstrap/cache/config.php` confirmed absent before testing; `AuthenticationTest`, `PasswordResetTest` and `AuthenticatedCacheControlTest` passed (12 tests, 45 assertions) on SQLite `:memory:`; `npm run build` succeeded, regenerating the committed `public/build` assets.
- Defense notes: deriving the panel color from the DEPLA dark-green hue instead of an arbitrary green keeps the overlay inside the existing palette; a single wrapper panel with a light blur preserves the photo as a design element while lifting text off the busy background; color-only text edits leave copy, structure and screen-reader content identical, and scoping the feature-chip change to the shared `.feature-icon` class is safe because that class is used only by this panel.

### Revision: Lighter Green Glass Panel (2026-10-05)

- The first-pass panel read as a heavy dark card, so `.brand-panel` in `resources/views/layouts/guest.blade.php` was re-tuned to a light glass overlay: `background: rgba(54, 126, 75, 0.48)` (the DEPLA dark green `#367E4B` itself, 48% alpha, inside the requested 0.42-0.50 band), `backdrop-filter: blur(3px)` (was 8px), `box-shadow: 0 10px 30px rgba(8,36,23,0.14)` (was `0 14px 40px ... 0.22`), `border: 1px solid rgba(255,255,255,0.30)` for a soft glass edge, radius unchanged at 26px (22px at <=1280px).
- Measured against real pixels of `public/images/hero-maternity.png` (center average RGB 193,192,187) plus the cream fade, the light panel composites to RGB (137,171,144), luminance 0.365 - a soft green tint that leaves the doctor and background clearly recognizable, versus the previous dark composite (79,118,98), luminance 0.156.
- Because the lighter glass lowers raw contrast (white text measures 2.53:1 on it), the panel now carries a soft inherited `text-shadow: 0 1px 2px rgba(4,26,15,0.80), 0 0 8px rgba(4,26,15,0.45)` so the existing white/near-white copy stays readable without darkening the panel. All copy colors from the first pass are unchanged (h1 `#FFFFFF`, quote `#F0FDF4`, location `rgba(255,255,255,0.90)`, features `#F0F7F2`, copyright `rgba(255,255,255,0.75)`, white icon chips).
- The subtitle accent moved from `#EAFFF2` to a deepened DEPLA green `#0F6434`: the same measurement shows the bright brand green `#55B85A` lands at 1.01:1 on the light glass (invisible), while `#0F6434` measures 2.87:1 - the strongest line on the panel - which is why the "readable" qualifier on the accent was applied this way.
- Nothing else changed: background image, cream overlay, logos, panel size/position, left/right proportions, right-side login card, JS, routes, controllers and authentication are untouched, and no functional code was modified. Verification: the three auth feature suites passed again (12 tests, 45 assertions) on SQLite `:memory:`; `git diff --check` clean; no new horizontal overflow (only color/blur/shadow values changed inside the existing fixed-width panel).

## Staff Management Batch 1: Structured Profiles (2026-10-05)

- Restored the missing staff/_profile-fields.blade.php shared partial with First/Middle/Last Name, Address, Contact Number, Birthday, and Email. Required indicators, HTML constraints, field-level errors, saved-record fallbacks, and safe old input are shared across Create/Edit; passwords have no value binding.
- Added StaffProfileRequest to normalize name whitespace, generate the legacy name, validate both profile workflows, enforce Admin actors/staff targets, and use confirmed Password::defaults() only for creation (current recovery minimum is 8).
- Rules: First/Last Name required max 100; Middle Name nullable max 100; combined name max 255; Address required max 1000; Contact Number required string max 30 accepting 09 plus nine digits, +639 plus nine digits, or 639 plus nine digits. Spaces/hyphens are removed while leading zero/+63 remain strings. Birthday requires valid Y-m-d not after today; Email required string/email max 255 and unique across active and archived Users.
- StaffController now persists validated profile data and generated name, forces role=staff for creation, and rejects Admin targets with 403 on Edit/Update. Existing manual password entry, hashing, credential mail, redirects, and audit actions are retained. Update ignores password/role/deleted_at submissions.
- User fillable supports the six existing columns and birthday has a date cast. Existing name-based lists remain the legacy fallback. Opening legacy Edit displays its old name and never splits or mutates it; Admin supplies structured names manually on save.
- Preserved the pre-existing index responsiveness changes untouched. Shared fields stack on mobile and use two columns at sm; long errors wrap and Create/Edit confirmation dialogs have viewport height/scroll constraints. Removed the Create view's invalid protected Password property access.
- Tests: new StaffProfileBatchOneTest passed 32 tests / 211 assertions using only SQLite :memory: with the existing TestCase safety guard and no config cache. It builds temporary test tables directly; no migrations were created or run. Coverage includes both Create/Update validation, six-field persistence, role/account-field protection, name whitespace/middle-name/combined-length rules, contact formats, date boundaries, active/archived email uniqueness, password confirmation/hashing/no flashing, rendered saved fields, old-input errors, legacy records, credential-mail compatibility, and Admin-target denial.
- Updated existing StaffAccountAdministrationBoundaryTest fixture payloads for structured fields and confirmation, and its isolated test schema for the six manually added columns. Its RefreshDatabase-based suite was not run because Batch 1 explicitly prohibits running migrations.
- PHP syntax and git diff whitespace checks pass. Manual verification remains: 390/768/1366/1440px form/error/dialog rendering, actual existing-database record edit/save/reopen, and current credential-email delivery using a controlled test recipient.
- Defense notes: a single profile request prevents Create/Edit rules and name composition from diverging; controller validated allowlists protect password, role and activation state; no automatic legacy-name split or database migration is introduced. Public registration, recovery redesign, credential-email redesign, lifecycle terminology, archived audit attribution, and unrelated modules are deferred as requested.

## Referral Archive: Soft-Delete Archive / Restore Workflow (2026-10-05)

- Backend: created migration `2026_10_05_000001_add_deleted_at_to_referrals_table` (adds `deleted_at` to `referrals`; `down()` drops it) and added the SoftDeletes trait to `Referral`. The migration was NOT run against MySQL; run `php artisan migrate` to apply it. `ReferralController` gains `destroy()` (soft delete + `ARCHIVE`/`REFERRAL` audit entry + flash "Referral archived successfully."), `archived()` (trashed rows with patient eager-loaded, newest archive first), and `restoreArchived()` (flash "Referral restored successfully."). `show()` intentionally keeps no `withTrashed()`: archived referrals 404 until restored.
- Routes: `GET /referrals/archived` (`referrals.archived`) declared above the `/referrals/{id}` wildcard with staff middleware; `POST /referrals/{id}/restore` and `DELETE /referrals/{id}` added inside the existing staff write group. Guest routes redirect to login; admin receives 403 on all archive actions.
- UI: the Referrals index header now renders `[Archived] [Create Referral]` for staff; desktop and mobile row actions gain a staff-gated Archive icon button immediately right of View Referral, opening a referral-specific confirmation modal (shared `x-action-buttons` untouched). New `referrals/archived.blade.php` mirrors the Prenatal Visits archived page: Patient / Destination / Referral Date / Reason / Status / Archived Date / Action (Restore with confirmation), no View link, empty state and archived count.
- Defense notes: archive is soft-delete only (no permanent deletion, no cascade); authorization reuses the existing staff gate so behavior matches Prenatal Visits; analytics intentionally untouched — `ReferralAnalyticsService` raw queries keep counting archived rows historically while the soft-delete scope removes them from active listings and the `total` count; clinical thresholds, referral lifecycle actions and print flows unchanged.
- Tests: new `ReferralArchiveWorkflowTest` — 6 tests / 65 assertions covering active-list exposure (Archived link, Archive action, modal copy), soft delete with row retention, active-list and `total` disappearance, archived-page contents and restore round-trip, isolation of other referrals, show 404 while archived then recovery after restore, guest/admin/staff authorization, and the ARCHIVE audit entry. All pass on SQLite `:memory:` with no configuration cache.
- Test infrastructure: registered MySQL-only SQL functions (`YEAR`, `MONTH`, `DAY`, `CONCAT`) as SQLite user functions in `tests\TestCase` only. Raw MySQL SQL added in earlier commits (`ReferralAnalyticsService`, `DashboardController`, `RiskMonitoringDataService`) previously crashed every referrals-index and dashboard test on SQLite with "no such function: YEAR"; no production query or service was modified by this fix.
- Pre-existing failures verified unrelated to this sprint (present at HEAD before any change): ReferralAnalyticsTest (14, analytics payload/UI restructure), ReferralPatientSelectionTest (3, HIGH-assessment selector filter and removed empty message), AssessmentLinkedReferralTest and Phase16EUiTest D3/I2 (panel text no longer present in any view), ReferralFollowThroughTest (4, profile label changes), StaffAccessControlTest (1, Edit-patient visibility), PrenatalVisitsActiveListingTest (2, middle-name double-space name building), PrenatalVisitHistoryAndPrintTest (1, profile edit link), Phase16EUiTest I (`risk.monitoring` AdminMiddleware). Otherwise relevant suites pass: Phase16FFinalAcceptance, ReferralPrintHistory, PrenatalVisitArchiveWorkflow, StaffAccountPasswordReset (10/10). A full-suite run is impractical in this environment because pre-existing network-dependent tests hang.
- Verification: `php -l` on every edited PHP file; `php artisan route:list --path=referrals` confirms `referrals.archived` resolves before the `{id}` wildcard; `git diff --check` clean; `bootstrap/cache/config.php` absent; migration not run. Remaining manual steps: run `php artisan migrate`, then browser QA of Archive / Archived / Restore at desktop and mobile widths.

## Referral Lifecycle: One-Row-Per-Patient Index with Expandable History and Cancelled Re-Referral (2026-10-05)

- Backend: `Patient::latestReferral()` added as `hasOne(Referral::class)->ofMany(['referral_date' => 'max', 'id' => 'max'])` constrained to non-archived rows (mirrors `latestPrenatalAssessment`). No migration: `referrals.patient_id` was already a non-unique FK, so multiple referral rows per patient were always valid (also covered by `ReferralFollowThroughTest` "does not reopen a closed referral").
- `ReferralController::index()` now queries `Patient` (one row per patient) via `whereHas('latestReferral')`, eager-loads `latestReferral.prenatalVisit` plus `referrals` ordered `referral_date desc, id desc`, orders patients by `withAggregate(['latestReferral as latest_referral_date'], 'referral_date', 'max')` then id (same ordering as the print history), filters status with `whereHas('latestReferral', status)` (latest non-archived status per the approved decision), searches patients directly by first/last name, and pages with `paginate(15)->withQueryString()` (fixes search/status being dropped from page links). Summary counters stay referral-row based (archived excluded by SoftDeletes); analytics stays untouched.
- `ReferralController::selectPatient()` eligibility: ONGOING pregnancies plus legacy REFERRED rows whose latest non-archived referral is closed (Completed/Refused/Cancelled) so cancelled referrals can be re-referred; `patient.status` is never rewritten and DELIVERED stays excluded. The HIGH latest-assessment gate is unchanged (approved decision). Duplicate-active protection is enforced at save time, not at search time.
- `ReferralController::store()` manual mode (no `prenatal_visit_id`) now rejects a second Pending referral for the same patient ("A pending referral already exists for this patient." on `patient_id`); closed referrals never block a new row. Assessment-linked mode keeps its existing per-assessment Pending rule; `create()`/`show()`/transitions unchanged.
- UI: `referrals/index.blade.php` desktop table and mobile cards render one row per patient from `latestReferral` with a staff-gated Archive action on that latest referral, a `History (n)` toggle, and an expandable history block (desktop sibling `<tr>` with `colspan="7"` + mobile in-card div) modeled on Prenatal Visit History's `toggleVisitDetails` pattern (`toggleReferralHistory` flips both). History renders via new partial `referrals/partials/history-list.blade.php`: every non-archived referral newest-first with date, destination, status/source badges, reason, visit line, recorded/completed timestamps, and per-record `View` / `Print` / staff `Archive` links keyed by explicit referral IDs. History labels never use the literal "View Referral" (keeps the Phase16F pagination marker at exactly 2 per patient) and history excludes archived rows (Approach A).
- Tests: new `ReferralLifecycleOneRowHistoryTest` - 12 tests / 59 assertions: cancelled ONGOING patient searchable again, legacy REFERRED searchable only with a closed latest referral (Pending/bare REFERRED still hidden, DELIVERED still excluded), Pending-referral patient still selectable, manual second-Pending rejected, new row after Cancelled with the old row preserved and patient status untouched, one-row index with full newest-first history, per-referral action IDs (show/print/destroy), latest-status filter, single search row for multi-referral patients, archived latest excluded with fallback to the remaining row, and guest/staff/admin access.
- Verification: no `bootstrap/cache/config.php`; `php -l` clean; new suite 12/12. Regression gate vs documented baseline: ReferralArchiveWorkflow 6/6, Phase16FFinalAcceptance (incl. K filter/search and K3 exact 30/2 "View Referral" counts), ReferralPrintHistory pass; unchanged pre-existing failures: ReferralPatientSelection (3), Phase16EUi (3: I/D3/I2), ReferralFollowThrough (4), AssessmentLinked (2), StaffAccessControl (1), PrenatalVisitsActiveListing (2), PrenatalVisitHistoryAndPrint (1), RiskMonitoringStatus (2, documented `risk.monitoring` AdminMiddleware 403). ReferralAnalyticsTest improved 14 -> 13 (the index search/print/complete test now passes because history rows carry print links; remaining 13 are the documented analytics payload/UI restructure). PatientViewControllerTest shows 17 failures newly documented here: pre-existing and unrelated - that suite exercises only `view-all-records` (CSS/sorting assertions) and executes none of the files touched by this sprint.
- Defense notes: eligibility and duplicate rules live in explicit, commented queries rather than hidden scopes; status/archived semantics reuse the SoftDeletes global scope everywhere so archive behavior cannot drift between list, history, counters and selector; ordering uses Eloquent `withAggregate` of the approved `latestReferral` relation (no raw SQL, no GROUP BY mismatched columns); all history actions target `{id}` routes so expanding rows cannot redirect an action to another referral; clinical thresholds, transition rules, analytics SQL and print content unchanged. Remaining manual steps: browser QA of the history expansion at mobile/desktop widths, `php artisan migrate` for the still-unrun referral/users migrations, and the standing pre-existing failure cleanup outside this sprint.

## Referral Re-Eligibility Correction: Cancelled-Only Re-Entry for Create Referral (2026-10-05)

- Business rule correction to `ReferralController::selectPatient()`: the legacy REFERRED re-eligibility condition changed from `whereIn('status', ['Completed', 'Refused', 'Cancelled'])` to `where('status', 'Cancelled')` on the patient's latest non-archived referral. Only a Cancelled latest referral now re-opens the selector; a latest Completed or Refused referral (and Pending / missing referral) keeps the REFERRED patient out of Create Referral search. Scope is exactly the re-referral portion: the ONGOING branch, the HIGH latest-assessment gate, the DELIVERED exclusion and the never-rewrite rule for `patient.status` are untouched.
- Backend safety reviewed (report-only, no redesign): `store()` retains the manual-mode duplicate-Pending guard as defense-in-depth; no backend condition blocks a direct POST for a patient whose latest referral is Completed/Refused (and `create()` only blocks DELIVERED), so selection flow remains the sole enforcement point for the Completed/Refused rule - flagged as a pre-existing gap for approval before any guard is added. `store()` was not modified this sprint.
- Tests: `ReferralLifecycleOneRowHistoryTest` restructured to the final matrix - Cancelled latest => searchable (ONGOING and REFERRED), Completed latest => not searchable, Refused latest => not searchable, Pending/missing latest => not searchable, duplicate-Pending store guard still rejects, new referral after Cancelled gets its own ID while the old Cancelled row stays unchanged (id/status/referred_to asserted), and a new index test proves Completed/Refused/Cancelled historical records remain intact with original IDs/statuses (eligibility change touches selection only). 16 lifecycle + 6 archive tests pass (22/22, 148 assertions); ReferralPatientSelection remains at its documented 3 pre-existing failures (HIGH-assessment filter, removed empty message - those tests contain no referral rows); Phase16F and ReferralPrintHistory pass unchanged. No migration, no git operations, one-row/history/archived semantics untouched.
- Defense notes: `latestReferral` keeps using the non-archived ofMany relation so archive semantics cannot leak into eligibility; the correction is one condition in one method (no duplicated rule in views or JS); historical referrals are never filtered by the eligibility rule - only selector membership changes.

## Create Referral Lifecycle Rule: Applies to ONGOING and REFERRED, With Backend Enforcement (2026-10-05)

- Root cause of the browser-QA bug: `selectPatient()`'s first branch was `status = ONGOING` with no referral-status condition, so ONGOING patients bypassed the lifecycle rule. Read-only DB check confirmed the exact cases: Mary Veloso (id 22, ONGOING, latest referral Completed #5) and Bj Balansag (id 72, ONGOING, latest Completed #4) appeared in Create Referral. No data was modified.
- New selector rule: `whereIn('status', ['ONGOING','REFERRED'])` AND (`whereDoesntHave('latestReferral')` OR `latestReferral.status = Cancelled`) AND the unchanged HIGH latest-assessment gate. Pending/Completed/Refused latest referrals now exclude patients regardless of pregnancy status; DELIVERED stays excluded via the status filter; first-time patients (no non-archived referral) stay eligible for their first referral; `patient.status` is never rewritten. Archived referrals do not count (the relation spans non-archived rows only).
- Backend enforcement added so direct URL/POST cannot bypass the selector: `create()` refuses the form (redirect with error flash) and `store()` rejects the save (`withErrors(['patient_id' => ...])`) whenever the latest non-archived referral exists and is not Cancelled - both modes (manual and assessment-linked). Guard placement preserves every existing error: the manual duplicate-Pending guard, the per-assessment duplicate guard, and the DELIVERED guard all still fire first with their original messages. The duplicate-Pending guard was not weakened. The old Cancelled row is never touched; creation always inserts a new referral row with its own id.
- Tests: `ReferralLifecycleOneRowHistoryTest` now proves the full matrix for BOTH statuses (ONGOING and REFERRED x none/Cancelled/Pending/Completed/Refused), DELIVERED exclusion, HIGH-gate enforcement for no-referral and Cancelled patients, direct-POST rejection for Pending/Completed/Refused, create-page refusal for non-cancelled latest, new-row-after-Cancelled with separate id, and all prior one-row/history/archived/pagination semantics (22 tests, 110 assertions). `AssessmentLinkedReferralTest`'s "re-referral once closed" test updated to the final rule: linked store with a Completed prior is rejected (`patient_id` error, count unchanged), then allowed as a brand-new row once that prior is Cancelled (2 rows, old id/status preserved).
- Regression gate vs documented baselines: lifecycle 22/22, archive 6/6, Phase16FFinalAcceptance, ReferralPrintHistory, NotificationTest all pass; unchanged pre-existing failures only: AssessmentLinked 2 (panel text), Selection 3 (HIGH filter/empty message), Phase16EUi 3 (I/D3/I2), FollowThrough 4, RiskMonitoringStatus 2 (AdminMiddleware 403), StaffAccess 1. Zero new failures. Live-data simulation of the new rule (read-only) hides ids 22/72 and keeps only patients passing both gates.
- Defense notes: the lifecycle rule is expressed once per layer (selector query, create guard, store guard) against the same `latestReferral` relation so archive/soft-delete semantics cannot diverge; `whereDoesntHave('latestReferral')` is proven by the first-referral tests to treat archived-only patients as first-time; no migration, no analytics/prenatal/index changes, no git operations. Interpretation note: "no non-archived referral -> eligible" applies to both ONGOING and REFERRED per the task's conceptual formula and the requirement that selector and backend enforce the SAME rule (a bare REFERRED first-timer is therefore eligible, consistent with backend behavior).

## Referral Details: Custom Confirmation Modals Replace Native confirm() (2026-10-05)

- `referrals/show.blade.php` only (view layer): the Mark Completed and Cancel Referral native `onclick="return confirm(...)"` handlers were removed. The visible page buttons keep their exact existing classes (green `bg-[#55B85A]` / navy `bg-gray-700`) but now open custom confirmation modals instead of submitting directly; the page forms keep their original route, method, CSRF and referral id, and only gained `id="completeForm"` / `id="cancelForm"`.
- Both new modals reuse the Record Referral Refusal modal's exact structure - same overlay (`fixed inset-0 z-50 bg-black/40 backdrop-blur-sm px-4 py-6`), same centered `bg-white rounded-xl shadow-xl max-w-md w-full p-6` panel, same header/X classes, same `text-sm text-gray-500` body, same `mt-6 flex justify-end gap-3` footer and same outlined Back button. They carry the `refusal-modal-theme` class, and three class-scoped CSS rules were added (without touching the existing `#refuseModal` rules) so title/body/border colors pin to the same navy/muted palette. Titles/bodies exactly per spec; confirm buttons reuse the existing page greens/navy; X and Back only close; outside-click behaves like the refusal modal (no handler = no-op); the modal confirm calls `form.requestSubmit()` on the existing page form with a `dataset.submitting` double-submit guard (the page had no prior pattern to reuse).
- Both modals live inside the existing `@if($referral->status === 'Pending')` block next to the refusal modal, so closed statuses render no mutation UI. A CSS comment initially leaked the literal strings "Mark Completed"/"Cancel Referral" into every page render (breaking Phase16F D6/E7 and Phase16EUi D3 absence assertions) and was reworded - lesson: `<style>` content is part of the rendered page.
- Tests: new `ReferralShowConfirmationModalsTest` (3 tests, 50 assertions) asserts native confirm removal, modal presence/structure/content, page buttons opening modals, confirm buttons targeting the existing forms with correct referral-id routes, close controls, existing green/navy classes, refusal modal intact, closed-status absence, and the double-submit guard. Gate: Phase16FFinalAcceptance full pass, Phase16EUi back to its 3 documented failures, FollowThrough 4 documented failures, PrintHistory + Lifecycle + Archive all green - zero new failures. No controller/route/business-rule changes, no migration, no git operations.
- Defense notes: submission still goes through the original forms so CSRF, method spoofing and backend lifecycle rules are untouched by design; the modal layer is presentation-only; Blade comments render nothing while `<style>` does - both were checked against the closed-status absence tests; browser QA at desktop/mobile widths remains manual (modals share the refusal modal's responsive classes, so behavior should match by construction).

## Refusal Workflow: Optional Supporting Image (Evidence Upload) (2026-10-05)

- Scope: one optional supporting-image upload inside the existing "Record Referral Refusal" workflow. A new nullable `referrals.refusal_image_path` column stores ONLY the relative storage path (never base64/binary); created as `database/migrations/2026_10_05_000003_add_refusal_image_path_to_referrals_table.php` with the established `Schema::hasColumn` guard, column placed after `refusal_notes`, `down()` dropping only that column - **file created only, never run against the real database** (tests apply it through the isolated SQLite `:memory:` migration).
- Backend (`ReferralController::refuse()`): added `refusal_image => nullable|file|image|mimes:jpg,jpeg,png,webp|max:5120` with custom messages ("The supporting image must not exceed 5MB." / "Please upload a JPG, JPEG, PNG, or WebP image."), stored AFTER the DELIVERED guard via `storeAs('referrals', 'refusal_{id}_{time}_{Str::random(8)}.{content-guess-extension}', 'public')` - directory, prefix, timestamp and random suffix are server-generated and the client original filename is never used. `ReferralFollowThroughService::refuse()` gained an optional `?string $imagePath = null` fifth parameter (all existing 4-argument callers unchanged) that is persisted only inside the Pending->Refused transition. If the transition throws `DomainException` after the file was stored, the catch block deletes ONLY that newly stored file (orphan cleanup); `complete()`/`cancel()` were deliberately NOT extended because `refusal_image_path` is null on every Pending row, so no status transition can carry or clear a path and no file-deletion path is needed. The audit description appends " - supporting image attached" only when a file was stored.
- Streaming: new `GET /referrals/{referral}/refusal-image` (`referrals.refusal-image`) added inside the existing authenticated `no-store` group (4-space indent next to `referrals.show`; two-segment static suffix cannot collide with the single-segment `{id}` wildcard). `ReferralController::refusalImage()` mirrors `UltrasoundController::file()`: path comes exclusively from the persisted row, missing row/file (including archived/soft-deleted rows via the SoftDeletes route-model binding) aborts 404, and the file is streamed with `response()->file(Storage::disk('public')->path(...))` - no `asset('storage/...')` public URL anywhere.
- UI (`referrals/show.blade.php`): the refusal form gained `enctype="multipart/form-data"`, an optional "Supporting Image" file input (`accept=".jpg,.jpeg,.png,.webp,image/jpeg,image/png,image/webp"`), helper text "JPG, JPEG, PNG, or WebP. Maximum 5MB." and an inline `role="alert"` error element; client-side JS re-validates type/size (5 * 1024 * 1024 bytes, `image/jpeg|image/png|image/webp`), shows the exact server message in DEPLA red, clears the invalid selection and re-checks on submit - server validation stays authoritative. `<x-error-summary :errors="$errors">` was added after the flash components (the page previously rendered no validation errors at all; the component outputs nothing when the bag is empty). The Refused record section and `referrals/partials/history-list.blade.php` show a green "View Supporting Image" link (`target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer"`, `event.stopPropagation()` in history) only when a path exists, always routed with the exact referral row (`$referral` / `$historyReferral`, never the patient's latest). New strings avoid the Phase16EUi forbidden literals ("Mark Completed" etc.), and the new markup/script sit inside the existing Pending-only `@if` block or the Refused-only section.
- Tests: new `tests/Feature/ReferralRefusalImageTest.php` - 20 tests / 97 assertions. The CLI PHP has no GD extension (`UploadedFile::fake()->image()` unusable), so tests embed real 1x1 image bytes (PNG/JPEG/WebP/GIF + an oversized padded PNG) which fileinfo sniffs through the same production code path. Coverage: JPEG/PNG/WebP accepted with server-generated `refusals/refusal_{id}_...` path matching regex and content-derived extension; no-image refusal stores nothing and keeps the path null; GIF rejected and >5MB rejected with the exact custom messages (referral stays Pending, zero files); client filename `../../../evil.png` never reaches storage; notes/waiver rules unchanged with an image present (invalid notes rejected, waiver omitted => false); a later referral never inherits a previous path; authenticated streaming returns 200 + `image/png` + byte-identical content; guest redirected to login; 404 for no-path, missing-file and archived rows with access restored after un-archive (file survives archive); orphan cleanup when the transition fails (fake disk empty, DB unchanged, no audit row) and DELIVERED guard blocks before any file is written; route registered as GET with `web`/`auth`/`no-store` middleware; validation errors surface in the new detail-page summary; Pending modal renders the input/enctype/helper/client messages; history list links only the exact evidence row (desktop+mobile partials both render, asserted by URL presence/absence).
- Verification: `bootstrap/cache/config.php` absent before and after; `php -l` clean on every touched PHP file; `php artisan route:list --path=refusal-image` shows the single GET route; focused suite 20/20 (97 assertions). Full regression gate matches the documented baselines exactly - zero new failures: `ReferralShowConfirmationModalsTest` 3/3, `ReferralLifecycleOneRowHistoryTest` + `ReferralArchiveWorkflowTest` + `Phase16FFinalAcceptanceTest` green (58 pass / 3 fail combined where the 3 are Phase16EUi's documented I/D3/I2), `ReferralFollowThroughTest` 17 pass / 4 documented (risk.monitoring AdminMiddleware 403), `AssessmentLinkedReferralTest` 27 pass / 2 documented, `ReferralPatientSelectionTest` 3 documented, `RiskMonitoringStatusTest` 2 documented, `StaffAccessControlTest` 1 documented, `ReferralAnalyticsTest` 13 documented, `PatientViewControllerTest` 17 documented, `PrenatalVisitsActiveListingTest` 2 documented, `PrenatalVisitHistoryAndPrintTest` 1 documented. No lint/typecheck composer scripts exist (only setup/dev/test).
- Defense notes: upload validation is server-side first (Laravel rules) with the browser check purely as immediate UX feedback; storage names are content-derived and server-generated so no client string ever becomes a path; the route streams from the DB path behind `auth` + `no-store`, matching the ultrasound evidence pattern instead of a public storage URL; archive hides the evidence (404) without deleting the file, and restore re-exposes it - consistent with the soft-delete-only archive semantics; orphan cleanup deletes only the file created by the current request; `complete()`/`cancel()` untouched by design (no refusal row can hold a path while Pending). Known environment caveats: (1) the migration still needs a future production `php artisan migrate` alongside the two already-unrun referral/users migrations; (2) Railway deploys are ephemeral - `storage/app/public` files do NOT survive redeploy/restart on the current setup, so evidence images would 404 after a deploy while the DB path remains; fixing that requires persistent volume/S3 configuration which was explicitly out of scope this sprint; (3) WebP/JPEG client-side validation messages are duplicated verbatim in JS and PHP so they cannot drift unnoticed - both are pinned by tests.

## Create Referral: HIGH RISK REASON in the Confirm Patient Modal (2026-10-06)

- Scope: `resources/views/referrals/select-patient.blade.php` (display + client-only JS) and a new feature test. The existing Confirm Patient modal already shows patient, birthdate, EDD, latest assessment date and the HIGH badge; it now also shows a `HIGH RISK REASON` block fed from the row button's new `data-risk-level`, `data-risk-reasons` and `data-risk-bp` attributes. Reasons are computed per row from the already-eager-loaded `latestPrenatalAssessment` (the same relation the selector query gates on, so the reasons always belong to the assessment whose date/badge is displayed) using the Patient Profile risk panel's exact normalization: `ListNormalizer::normalize(rule_reasons)` merged with `normalize(risk_reasons)` (profile order), then the stored `bp_assessment` label prepended when `reason_code === 'BP-URG'` and not already present. Read-only: no new query, no `RiskAssessmentService`/ML re-run, no threshold or rule change, nothing inferred from raw vitals.
- Modal contract preserved: modal IDs, `aria` attributes, patient/birthdate/EDD/assessment population, `data-url`, Cancel/Continue behavior, the static HIGH badge and every existing `data-*` attribute are untouched. The new section is server-rendered `hidden` and populated by JS with `textContent` chips (XSS-safe by construction), so chips cannot appear unless the modal opens; BP-urgent chip uses the profile's red palette, others orange, and the section is hidden entirely for a non-HIGH `data-risk-level`. Fallback copy when a HIGH visit carries no stored reason is the neutral `No specific risk reason recorded.` Chip padding uses `px-2 py-1` because `px-2.5` (used by the profile chips) is absent from the compiled `public/build/assets/app-BjjwKPgm.css`.
- Tests: new `tests/Feature/ReferralConfirmPatientRiskReasonTest.php` - 7 tests / 46 assertions decoding the rendered `data-risk-reasons` JSON: reasons come from the newest visit (a stale older HIGH visit's reasons are absent and the displayed date matches the newest), BP-URG label first and mirrored in `data-risk-bp`, no duplicate when the label is already a rule reason, empty HIGH visit renders `[]` plus the fallback sentence, legacy string/JSON-string reason shapes normalize through `ListNormalizer`, a LOW latest visit never emits a HIGH payload, and the pre-existing modal contract (IDs, `data-patient`, `data-url`, HIGH badge) is unchanged.
- Verification: `bootstrap/cache/config.php` absent before and after; `php -l` clean on both touched files; focused suite 7/7. Regression gate vs documented baselines, 8 failed / 84 passed combined - exactly the documented sets, zero new failures: `ReferralPatientSelectionTest` 3, `Phase16EUiTest` 3 (I/D3/I2), `AssessmentLinkedReferralTest` 2; `ReferralLifecycleOneRowHistoryTest`, `ReferralShowConfirmationModalsTest` and `Phase16FFinalAcceptanceTest` green.
- Defense notes: the reason payload is derived once in Blade from the same record already loaded for the row, so a stale/foreign reason cannot be attached to another patient's modal without changing that single block; JSON is emitted with `JSON_HEX_TAG|JSON_HEX_APOS|JSON_HEX_AMP|JSON_HEX_QUOT` so a reason label can never break out of the HTML attribute; JS guards `JSON.parse` failures by showing the neutral fallback rather than guessing; eligibility, referral lifecycle guards, `create()`/`store()`, print content and notification behavior were not touched. Remaining manual step: browser QA of the modal chips at mobile/desktop widths (chips are JS-rendered, so the suite asserts the payload rather than the painted DOM).

## Notification System: Clinic-Wide Recipients and Time-Based Clinical Alerts (2026-10-06)

- Scope: three new notification classes (`app/Notifications/PatientBecameHighRiskNotification.php`, `EddApproachingNotification.php`, `PastEddNeedsReviewNotification.php`), a rewritten `app/Services/SystemNotificationService.php`, one new scheduled command (`app/Console/Commands/CheckClinicalAlerts.php`, signature `notifications:check-clinical-alerts`) plus its `routes/console.php` entry (`Schedule::command(...)->dailyAt('07:00')`, alongside the existing 08:00 `app:send-prenatal-reminders`), two dispatch hooks in `PrenatalVisitController` (`store()` line 457/551, `update()` line 919/1023) and one in `PatientAssessmentRecalculationService` (lines 80/136). All notifications remain `via ['database']` with payload `type/title/message/action_label/destination/event_key` and the `patients.show` destination; no email/SMS channel, no migration, no route, no blade change.
- Recipients: `clinicRecipients()` replaces the previous admin-only `adminRecipients()` and now backs ALL SIX notify methods (BP-urgent, BP pending-repeat, referral created, referral closed, plus the two new EDD alerts and the HIGH transition): `User::whereIn('role', ['admin','staff'])->get()->unique('id')`, which is the existing selector list ("all active clinic users") with SoftDeletes' global scope automatically excluding archived users. No alert ever targets an `assigned_staff_id`; an archived staff member who still holds referrals simply stops receiving them, while every active admin+staff receives everything.
- Patient Became High Risk: `notifyHighRiskTransition(int $patientId, ?string $previousEffectiveRisk): bool` fires only on a non-HIGH -> HIGH state change of the CLINICAL effective risk (`effectiveRiskLevel()` recomputes from the patient's newest prenatal visit: BP-URG label / `risk_level` / the visit's own assessment level, read-only, no threshold change), never on HIGH -> HIGH re-saves, never on HIGH -> LOW downgrade. Two independent guards: the state-transition argument check and a per-event `event_key` (`became-high-risk:{patientId}:{visitId}`) stored inside the payload and compared exactly against existing `notifications` rows of the same `type` in `alreadySent()`, so double dispatch (controller + recalculation service on the same request) or a scheduler replay cannot produce a second row.
- Recalculation path: `PatientAssessmentRecalculationService` gained the service as a third constructor argument, snapshots the effective risk BEFORE the visit loop (`if ($visits->isEmpty()) return;` first, so an empty history never dispatches) and dispatches exactly ONCE after the loop; the nine `new PatientAssessmentRecalculationService(...)` constructions in its unit test now pass `new SystemNotificationService`.
- Time-based alerts: the command resolves EDD exclusively through the existing pregnancy authority (`PregnancyOutcomeMonitoringService::daysUntilOrPastEdd()/isEddPassed()/isFollowUpEligible()`, `Patient::isOngoing()`, `PregnancyOutcome::hasConfirmedOutcome()`), skips any pregnancy with a confirmed outcome, and dispatches `notifyEddApproaching()` (EDD in 0..7 days, `EDD_APPROACHING_DAYS = 7`, key `edd-approaching:{patientId}`) and `notifyPastEddNeedsReview()` (EDD strictly before today, key `past-edd:{patientId}`). Both are pure reminders: they never change `pregnancy_status`, never create an outcome/baby, never touch LMP/EDD, and the daily rerun is suppressed solely by the persisted `event_key` (no date-window key, so the alert cannot spam every day of the window and cannot be re-armed by an EDD edit that keeps the patient in-window).
- Repeat BP Overdue NOT implemented: **Repeat BP overdue cannot be implemented safely because the system does not currently define an authoritative overdue interval.** No `due`/deadline column, config value or documented protocol exists for a pending-repeat blood-pressure follow-up - only the `repeat_bp_recorded_at` timestamp and the `PENDING_REPEAT` status - so any interval would be a fabricated clinical threshold. The existing `notifyPendingRepeatBloodPressure()` behavior (event-driven, on recording a pending repeat) is unchanged, and the daily command intentionally does NOT scan for overdue repeats until the clinic approves an interval.
