- Error-key migration: because the clinic-hours rules now actually fire at the request layer, the field-keyed messages (`appointment_time`/`confirmed_time`) surface before the service-level DomainException (`walk_in`/`appointment` keys). Four existing test assertions were updated to the field keys (confirm checkup `confirmed_time`, confirm counseling `confirmed_time`, walk-in 17:00 `appointment_time`, past-sprint clinic-hours test `appointment_time`); the illegal-transition tests at lines 283-323 keep the `appointment` key (unrelated service DomainException path, no clinic-hours involvement).
- Tests: three new regression tests appended - (1) the rendered walk-in factory script contains `const CLINIC_TODAY = "` (real quote) and no `&quot;` variants anywhere in the script text; (2) the walk-in modal markup renders the patient search input, debounced Alpine bindings, `<template x-for>`, `name="patient_id"`, `:value="walkIn.patientId"`, `clearPatient()`, and the full button label with both `x-text`/`:disabled` attribute contents; (3) clinic-hours FormRequests reject 07:59/17:00 with the exact field-level messages for both `appointment_time` and `confirmed_time`, while in-window 08:00/16:59 and delivery 07:59/17:00 remain accepted (24/7 preserved).
- Verification: preflight green before every run (`bootstrap/cache/config.php` absent; phpunit.xml forces SQLite `:memory:` with `force="true"`); `php -l` clean on both FormRequests and the test file; one early iteration of the fix introduced a literal `{{ }}` inside a JS comment - Blade compiled it to `echo e();` with zero arguments and every calendar GET 500'd; the comment was reworded and the focused suite re-run clean, which is a useful lesson: Blade compiles `{{ }}` anywhere in the file, including JS comments. Focused suite `Phase3B2BAppointmentManagementTest` = **42 passed / 295 assertions** (39 prior + 3 new). Full suite = **1399 passed / 122 failed** vs the 1396/122 baseline - the +3 passed are exactly the new tests and the 122 failures are the identical pre-existing legacy set (incl. the known `StaffAccessControlTest` line 76) - zero regressions.
- Defense notes: the fix is emit-JSON-unescaped-with-HEX-flags, the same pattern the file's details payload already used safely - no new library, no client-side JSON parsing change, no Alpine restructuring; HEX flags prevent a `</script>` breakout from any interpolated value, so the hardening is strictly stronger than the escaped original; the clinic-hours fix moves no logic into the request layer (the service remains the authoritative write-boundary check, still proven by the direct-service-call tests) - it only makes the already-declared rules actually execute as the author intended; the two split rules reuse the existing custom message arrays, so no user-visible wording changed; both fixes are additive/hardening and touch no clinical threshold, transition rule, or patient-data write path.
- Residual risks: (1) HTTP tests still never execute Alpine - the new tests assert the rendered markup/script content, not live typing/search/select behavior, so a manual browser check of the walk-in flow (type -> debounce -> select -> button label swap -> create) remains recommended before release; (2) the past-sprint residual risks (stale `clinicToday`/`clinicNow` page-load anchors, date-only delivery rules, frozen-time hermeticity) are unchanged and remain documented in the prior entry; (3) the pre-existing ~122 legacy suite failures remain out of scope.

## DEPLA Phase 3B.3: Patient Portal Online Appointment Requests (2026-10-10)

- Scope: patient-facing ONLINE REQUEST module inside the existing Patient Portal. New files: `app/Http/Controllers/PatientAppointmentController.php`, `app/Http/Requests/PatientAppointmentStoreRequest.php`, `resources/views/patient/appointments/index.blade.php`, `resources/views/patient/appointments/create.blade.php`, `resources/views/patient/appointments/partials/card.blade.php`, `tests/Feature/Phase3B3PatientAppointmentsTest.php`. Modified: `app/Services/AppointmentManagementService.php` (one NEW method `createOnlineRequest()` only), `routes/web.php` (three portal routes), `resources/views/patient/layout.blade.php` (My Appointments nav item), `resources/views/patient/dashboard.blade.php` (placeholder card -> functional link + banner/docblock text), `tests/Feature/Phase3APatientPortalDashboardTest.php` (placeholder expectation -> link expectation), `docs/IMPLEMENTATION_PROGRESS.md`. NO new migration, NO schema change, NO second appointment table, NO Admin/Staff calendar redesign, NO clinical threshold change, NO `.env`/`config/database.php`/Railway/local-MySQL change, NO package install, NO `public/hot` change, NO git commit/push. The unresolved walk-in UI regression reported in the previous sprint was re-checked and remains unresolved (the Admin/Staff walk-in patient-search Alpine bindings are still dead in the rendered markup); per scope it was NOT modified here.
- Navigation: the Patient Portal layout gained a "My Appointments" item (calendar icon, active state via `request()->routeIs('patient.appointments.*')`) between Dashboard and My Profile, reusing the existing DEPLA portal palette and POST+CSRF logout. The dashboard's "Appointments - Coming Soon" placeholder article became a functional link card (same green gradient icon treatment as My Profile); the welcome banner now says only medical records are coming soon. Admin/Staff sidebar and clinic navigation were not touched.
- My Appointments page (`GET /patient/appointments`, route name `patient.appointments.index`): responsive, DEPLA portal theme, three sections - Pending Requests (each card shows "Awaiting clinic confirmation"), Upcoming Confirmed Appointments (shows the clinic-confirmed schedule in green when present), and Appointment History (bounded `paginate(10)->withQueryString()`; pending/upcoming use `limit(20)`). Each card shows type, booking source (Online/Walk-in), preferred schedule, confirmed schedule when available, status badge, date requested (created_at in Manila), and decline/cancellation reasons when recorded. Completed and past-confirmed appointments live in history. No staff-only audit details (reviewer name, reviewed_at, internal ids) are exposed. Empty states per section.
- Request form (`GET /patient/appointments/create`): appointment type radios (Checkup / Counseling / Delivery Request), preferred date (`min` = clinic today), preferred time (required + `08:00`-`16:59` hints for checkup/counseling; optional for delivery via a small Alpine `requiresTime` getter), CSRF, `old()` preservation, per-field error text, a general error banner for write-boundary DomainExceptions, and a client-side double-submit guard (`submitting` disables the button). Always-visible note: "Submitting an appointment request does not guarantee confirmation." Delivery type reveals: "Delivery requests do not reserve a bed... contact or visit the clinic directly rather than waiting for online confirmation." (no invented hotline). A page-local `[x-cloak]` rule keeps delivery-only UI hidden on first paint (the compiled stylesheet ships no such rule). No patient_id/status/source/confirmed/reviewed field exists anywhere in the form.
- Store workflow (`POST /patient/appointments`, route name `patient.appointments.store`): validate via `PatientAppointmentStoreRequest` (authorize = role patient; type in TYPES; requested_date required Y-m-d; requested_time required+clinic-hours for checkup/counseling, nullable for delivery; `pastScheduleIssue()` scoped to requested_* for field-level past errors) -> resolve patient from `auth()->user()->patientAccount` (never input) -> `AppointmentManagementService::createOnlineRequest()` re-validates the schedule at the write boundary inside `DB::transaction`, assigns patient_id/source=online/status=pending with confirmed_*/reviewed_* NULL -> redirect to My Appointments with the exact success message "Your appointment request has been submitted. Please wait for confirmation from the clinic." Duplicate DomainException -> `back()->withInput()->withErrors(['appointment' => ...])`. No notification, no email, no audit entry (not in scope; future phase may add one).
- Schedule logic reuse: `createOnlineRequest()` calls the SAME private `validateSchedule()` the staff confirm/walk-in path uses (checkup/counseling 08:00-inclusive to 17:00-exclusive Mon-Sun with required time; delivery 24/7 optional time; `assertNotPastSchedule()` Asia/Manila full-local compare re-read inside the transaction). Clinic hours rules in the FormRequest come from the existing split-array `AppointmentConfirmRequest::clinicHoursRules()` (a piped string inside an array is silently ignored by Laravel's parser - the previous sprint's finding). The global application timezone stays UTC; only these rules are Manila-anchored, matching the 3B.2A calendar policy.
- Duplicate policy (provisional): inside the transaction, the same patient may not hold another ACTIVE (pending or confirmed) request with identical type + requested date + requested time. Completed/cancelled/declined history never blocks a new request; there is no one-per-day rule; different patients may hold the same schedule; one patient may hold different types/times on one date. HONEST LIMITATION: this is a read-before-insert existence check inside a transaction, NOT a database unique constraint - a hard concurrency guarantee would need a schema change (unique index), which this phase deliberately does not introduce (per the brief: STOP and report rather than migrate). Two truly simultaneous identical inserts could still race on MySQL; sequential double-clicks and any request starting after the first commit are caught.
- Access control: all three routes carry the existing portal chain `['auth', 'patient-portal-account', 'must-change-password', 'no-store']` (identical ordering to dashboard/My Profile). Controller repeats the patient-role 403 (portal middleware passes clinic roles through) and the same `resolveOwnPatient()` 403 wording. No ID appears in any URL; `?patient_id=` is ignored; `/patient/appointments/{id}` 404s. Forged patient_id/user_id/status/source/confirmed_*/reviewed_*/decline/cancellation fields posted with a valid request are never read. Unlinked and archived accounts are refused before any appointment data loads. Patients cannot view another patient's rows, confirm/decline/cancel/complete, create walk-ins, edit confirmed schedules, reach the clinic calendar, or reach the staff patient-search endpoint.
- Admin/Staff integration: requests are ordinary rows in the existing `appointments` table (source=online, status=pending), so the 3B.2A calendar shows them on the requested date with the correct patient, Online source and Pending status, and the 3B.2B Confirm/Decline workflow applies unchanged - proven by a test that submits a patient request, sees it on the staff day view (patient last name, Pending, Online), and confirms it through the existing endpoint. No second table, no separate approval mechanism.
- Tests: new `tests/Feature/Phase3B3PatientAppointmentsTest.php` - 41 tests / 264 assertions, all green, covering all 30 required scenarios plus: authorization matrix (guest redirect; admin/staff 403 on all three routes with zero rows created; unlinked/archived 403 with friendly message; flagged patient redirected to mandatory password change; soft-deleted user treated as guest), identity isolation (forged patient_id ignored, query-param patient_id ignored, no ID-based URL exists, foreign patient's rows absent), schedule rules (past date; today's past time rejected / future time allowed / equal-minute boundary allowed; checkup 07:59/17:00 rejected and 16:59 accepted; counseling 07:30 rejected and 08:00 accepted; checkup/counseling require a time; delivery date-only accepted; delivery 23:30 and 02:15 accepted; invalid type/date rejected; old input preserved), duplicates (exact pending duplicate refused with `appointment` error and old input preserved; different time/type not blocked; declined history re-allowed; confirmed counts as active blocker; different patient identical schedule allowed; three same-day appointments of different types allowed), display (confirmed schedule with month-name date and staff name absent; decline and cancellation reasons; completed in history with no awaiting-phrase; pending with awaiting-phrase and "Booked via Online"; three empty states; form shows guidance and never renders patient_id/status/source/confirmed/reviewer inputs), side effects (delivery request changes no other table - prenatal_visits/medical_histories/birth_plans/ultrasounds/pregnancy_outcomes/babies/referrals/users/patients/audit_logs all snapshot-compared; no bed/admitted_at/discharged_at/occupancy/room column exists; zero notifications and zero audit rows; failed validations create no partial records; exactly one appointments table exists and the schema is unmutated), and calendar reflection (submit -> staff day view shows it -> existing confirm endpoint works). Frozen-time tests use `Carbon::setTestNow` in Asia/Manila with an `afterEach` cleanup.
- Verification: preflight green before every run (`bootstrap/cache/config.php` absent; phpunit.xml forces SQLite `:memory:` with `force="true"`; `tests/TestCase.php` isolation guard active); `php -l` clean on all new/edited PHP files; `php artisan view:cache` compiles every Blade cleanly (cleared after). One PowerShell `Set-Content -Encoding UTF8` BOM broke a controller's `php -l` mid-sprint and was stripped immediately (all files verified BOM-free). Focused Phase 3B.3 suite 41/41. Phase 3A portal suite re-run 30/30 after updating the one intentionally-replaced placeholder assertion. Full suite **1440 passed / 122 failed** vs the pre-change baseline 1399/122 - the +41 passed are exactly the new tests and the 122 failures are the identical pre-existing legacy set (incl. the known `StaffAccessControlTest` line 76) - zero regressions. No `optimize`/`config:cache`/`optimize:clear`/migrate/seed/db commands; no package install; no git operations.
- Defense notes: identity is resolved by the same `patientAccount` hasOne link and 403 wording as the rest of the portal, so a forged id can never reach another patient's rows; server-owned columns (patient_id, source, status, confirmed_*, reviewed_*, reasons) are assigned exclusively inside the service and are structurally unreachable from the request (no field, no `$request->only`, no mass assignment); schedule rules are literally the staff service's `validateSchedule()`, so the two surfaces cannot drift and a future threshold change lands once; the duplicate check runs at the write boundary inside the transaction (not at form render) and its read-before-insert limitation is documented rather than overstated; delivery messaging states the no-bed/visit-directly guidance without inventing a hotline; history is the only paginated query and pending/upcoming are hard-capped, so a long-lived patient cannot render an unbounded page; no notification/audit write means a patient submission cannot spam the notification tray or fabricate staff-attributed audit history. Remaining manual step: browser QA of the form (radio focus states, date/time pickers, Alpine delivery-note toggle, disabled submit during posting) at 390/768/1366/1440px - HTTP tests assert rendered markup, not painted interaction.

## DEPLA Phase 3B.3 hotfix: Appointment Details modal cloak + inline confirm errors + single Confirmed placeholder (2026-07-10)

- Scope: two reported Admin/Staff Appointments Calendar bugs. BUG 1 — clicking Confirm "refreshed" without confirming: the four management forms (CONFIRM/DECLINE/CANCEL/COMPLETE, calendar.blade.php) and the details container carried `x-cloak` but the project ships NO `[x-cloak]{display:none}` rule (absent from built CSS and resources/css), so on first paint every form rendered VISIBLE with an empty `:action` (`selected` is null until a chip is clicked) and a real submit button — the invisible overlay's `firstFocusable().focus()` (modal.blade.php x-init) could land on that stray button, submitting an empty PATCH-shaped POST to the day-view URL, which never reaches `PATCH /appointments/{id}/confirm`. The row genuinely stayed Pending (no stale data). BUG 2 — the Confirmed field rendered "Not yet confirmed · Not yet confirmed": detailsMap() emitted the same placeholder for BOTH `confirmedDateLabel` and `confirmedTimeLabel` when unconfirmed, and the view concatenated them with an unconditional `·` separator. Files changed (3): `resources/views/appointments/calendar.blade.php` (added `[x-cloak] { display: none !important; }` to the page <style> block — NO change to any x-cloak/x-show binding, no global CSS, no package; added inline `@error('appointment')` banner at the top of the CONFIRM form, `@error('confirmed_date')` under the date input, `@error('confirmed_time')` under BOTH the checkup/counseling time input and the delivery time input — same pattern as the walk-in modal; replaced the two-span Confirmed concatenation with ONE `dd` driven by a ternary that returns "Not yet confirmed" when both labels are null and `date · time` otherwise); `app/Http/Controllers/AppointmentCalendarController.php` (detailsMap now emits `null` for confirmedDateLabel/confirmedTimeLabel when unconfirmed instead of the duplicate string, and "Not provided" only when a confirmed date exists without a time — the ternary owns the placeholder wording; reviewedAtLabel fallback changed "Not provided" -> "Not yet reviewed" so Reviewed By / Reviewed Date read consistently while a REAL reviewer name/date is never hidden); `tests/Feature/Phase3B2BAppointmentManagementTest.php` (+3 regression tests). NOT modified (per safety): AppointmentManagementService, all appointment FormRequests, routes, Patient Portal, migrations, .env/config, public/hot, global CSS.
- Behaviour preserved: modal still reopens after a failed confirm via `errorAppointmentId` (AppointmentConfirmRequest::failedValidation + the controller's DomainException catch were untouched); schedule validation is NOT weakened — the FormRequest rules, clinicHoursRules() and the service's in-transaction assertNotPastSchedule() are byte-identical; old input binding on the date/time inputs is unchanged; the details JSON payload keys are unchanged (only two values became null, which the new ternary consumes). Delivery date-only confirmations show "Oct 11, 2026" alone (time label "Not provided" is suppressed by the join filter); fully confirmed rows show "Oct 11, 2026 · 10:00"; the Requested field still shows its own "Not provided" placeholder for date-only requests (unchanged, single placeholder).
- Tests: 3 new focused markup/HTTP tests in Phase3B2BAppointmentManagementTest — (1) the cloak CSS rule is present and all four forms keep x-cloak + CSRF/_method/confirmUrl survive; (2) a past confirmed_date, an out-of-hours confirmed_time and a stale-transition DomainException each flash the right error bag, and the flashed message renders INSIDE the confirm-form region (asserted by substring slicing between the confirm and decline form markers), not only in the occluded page flash; (3) the details payload carries null/null for a pending row, "Not provided" for a date-only confirmed row, "Nov 5, 2026 · 10:00" for a fully confirmed row, exactly ONE "Not yet confirmed" string in the document, and consistent "Not yet reviewed" reviewer placeholders. Focused suite 45 passed / 324 assertions (was 42/295, +3 tests). Phase3B2A calendar 23 passed. Full suite 1443 passed / 122 failed vs the pre-change baseline 1440/122 — the +3 are exactly the new tests and the 122 failures are the identical pre-existing legacy set (RiskAnalytics/ReferralAnalytics/Explainability/PatientViewController/PregnancyOutcome/StaffAccessControlTest:76 etc., none appointment-related) — zero regressions. Preflight before every run: bootstrap/cache/config.php absent, phpunit.xml forces SQLite :memory: with force="true", TestCase isolation guard active; no optimize/config:cache/migrate/seed; no package install; no git operations; MySQL/Railway untouched.
- Defense notes: the cloak fix is additive CSS only — it cannot alter Alpine behaviour that already worked, and it removes the pre-init submit target that caused the phantom "refresh"; inline @error mirrors the walk-in modal's established pattern so a rejected confirm now explains itself inside the open modal (the page flash remains as a fallback for the non-modal case); the placeholder fix moved the wording decision into ONE ternary in the view fed by null-aware controller labels, so the duplicate string can never reappear from the payload side, and a reviewer who actually reviewed still shows their real name/date (no information is hidden). Honest limitation: HTTP tests assert rendered markup, not painted browser behaviour — the exact first-paint focus target that triggered the phantom submit is a browser-runtime detail; if the reported symptom persists after this fix, capture DevTools evidence (Network entry for the click, Elements snapshot of the forms before Alpine init).

## DEPLA Phase 3B.3 hotfix: Confirm-time duplicate-input submission bug (2026-07-10)

- Bug: Admin/Staff -> Appointments -> Pending online appointment -> Confirm. The confirm form opened correctly and the Confirmed Time field visibly showed a valid time (e.g. 04:56 PM), but submitting returned the inline error "An appointment time is required for checkup and counseling appointments." and the appointment stayed Pending across repeated attempts. Root cause (proven from the rendered markup, not assumed): resources/views/appointments/calendar.blade.php rendered TWO <input type="time" name="confirmed_time"> controls inside the same confirm form - one for checkup/counseling (required) and one for delivery (optional). Alpine x-show only toggles visibility via CSS (display:none); it does NOT remove the control from the form, so BOTH inputs were submitted on every confirm. PHP's request parsing keeps the LAST value for a repeated key, and Laravel's ConvertEmptyStringsToNull turns "" into null. The hidden delivery input always came after the visible checkup/counseling input and submitted an empty value, so the server received confirmed_time = null even though the user saw a valid time - tripping the FormRequest's 'required' rule while the appointment remained Pending. The visible "04:56 PM" was only the browser's 12-hour display of the input's own value; the value was never lost in the DOM, it was CLOBBERED on submission by the trailing duplicate empty key. Fix (minimal, one file): resources/views/appointments/calendar.blade.php - added x-bind:disabled to each confirmed_time input, bound to the OPPOSITE of that input's own x-show condition (checkup input disabled when type === 'delivery_request'; delivery input disabled when type !== 'delivery_request'). A disabled control is excluded from form submission and from constraint validation, so exactly ONE confirmed_time reaches the server - the visible/active one the user actually edited. No change to name attributes, required attributes, :value bindings, x-show conditions, AppointmentConfirmRequest rules, clinic-hours rules, past-schedule (Manila) rules, AppointmentManagementService, routes, statuses, or the Patient Portal. Validation was NOT weakened: a missing/empty confirmed_time for checkup/counseling is still refused by the same 'required' rule, out-of-hours, invalid-format and past-schedule refusals are unchanged, and delivery requests may still be confirmed date-only with a null time.
- Behavior preserved: Confirmed Time remains required for Checkup and Counseling (FormRequest 'required' on confirmed_time for non-delivery types, unchanged); Delivery Request may still have no confirmed time (nullable, unchanged); the active input still submits the browser's 24-hour HH:MM value (the :value binding and type="time" step="60" are unchanged, and the fix only excludes the INACTIVE control - it never rewrites the active value); Philippines timezone anchoring, clinic hours 08:00 inclusive to before 17:00, and past-date/time rejection are byte-for-byte untouched; no auto-confirm without successful validation (the service still re-validates under lockForUpdate and refuses illegal/stale transitions); inline @error('appointment'/'confirmed_date'/'confirmed_time') display inside the reopened modal is preserved.
- Tests: added 4 focused regression tests to tests/Feature/Phase3B2BAppointmentManagementTest.php: (1) markup contract - the rendered confirm form contains exactly two name="confirmed_time" controls, each carrying its x-bind:disabled binding, with required kept only on the checkup/counseling control; (2) HTTP reproduction of the duplicate-key clobber - a single well-formed confirmed_time succeeds, and the legacy duplicate-empty-key payload (pre-fix browser behaviour) is still correctly refused as a missing time, proving validation was not weakened; (3) counseling confirmation with a valid in-hours time succeeds, delivery confirmation with a null time succeeds (date-only), and after a staff confirmation the Patient Portal My Appointments page (fresh request as the linked portal user) shows the Confirmed status and the "Clinic-confirmed schedule:" row; (4) invalid time format (e.g. '4:56 PM'), today's past time, and out-of-hours 17:00 are all rejected with the appointment kept Pending, while a valid in-window HH:MM time flips Pending -> Confirmed and stores the time. Focused suite Phase3B2BAppointmentManagementTest: 49 passed / 356 assertions (was 45/324, +4 new tests). Regression gates: Phase3B2AAppointmentsCalendarTest 23 passed / 85 assertions; Phase3B3PatientAppointmentsTest 41 passed / 264 assertions. Full suite: 1447 passed / 122 failed vs the pre-change baseline 1443/122 - the +4 passed are exactly the new tests and the 122 failures are the identical pre-existing legacy set (none appointment-related) - zero regressions. Preflight before every run: bootstrap/cache/config.php absent, phpunit.xml forces SQLite :memory: with force="true", TestCase isolation guard active; php -l clean on the test file; php artisan view:cache compiled the Blade cleanly (cleared after). No migrations/seeds/optimize/config:cache; no package installs; no git operations; MySQL/Railway untouched; public/hot untouched.
- Defense notes: the fix is a pure submission-scoping change - it cannot alter any value the user typed, only which control is allowed to carry that value to the server, so there is no path by which a visible valid time can again be overwritten by an invisible empty one; because the inactive control is disabled (not merely hidden), it is excluded from BOTH submission and browser constraint validation, which also removes the pre-fix risk of the hidden required checkup input blocking a date-only delivery submission; the duplicate-key HTTP test documents the exact historical failure mode so a future regression that drops the disabled binding (or reintroduces a second same-name control without one) is caught by an assertion rather than a clinician's screenshot; the Patient Portal reflection test proves the confirmed schedule crosses the staff->patient surface on a fresh request, closing the loop the original bug report described. Honest limitation: HTTP tests assert the rendered markup contract and the request/response behaviour, not painted browser behaviour - the disabled binding is applied by Alpine at runtime, so if the symptom ever reappears the DevTools check is the Network tab's form data for the confirm PATCH (it must contain exactly ONE confirmed_time key with the visible HH:MM value).

## DEPLA Phase 3B.4: Appointments Calendar default status filter is All Statuses (2026-07-10)

- Requirement: In Admin/Staff -> Appointments Calendar, opening /appointments without a status query parameter should default to All Statuses and display appointments across Pending, Confirmed, Declined, Cancelled and Completed, while Month/Year/Day views, the service-type filter, explicit status selection, and date/view navigation all keep working. Fix (minimal, one controller method): app/Http/Controllers/AppointmentCalendarController.php resolveStatusFilter() changed the default from 'active' to 'all' for BOTH the absent-parameter case (query('status', 'all')) and the invalid-value fallback (': 'all'). Previously an absent or unrecognized status hid Cancelled/Declined history by default. The Active (Pending, Confirmed, Completed) option remains available and, when explicitly chosen, still narrows the list exactly as before. resources/views/appointments/calendar.blade.php: moved the All Statuses <option> above Active in the #filter-status select so it is both the computed default and the first visible option; the per-status options and their -driven selected logic are unchanged. No change to ACTIVE_STATUSES, STATUS_LABELS, baseQuery(), the date/type resolution helpers, AppointmentManagementService, routes, statuses, or the Patient Portal. Calendar display-date rules (cancelled rows showing confirmed_date ?? requested_date) and the appointment detail panel are untouched; they are independent of which statuses the query returns.
- Behavior preserved: an explicit specific status (e.g. status=pending) is applied normally and is never overridden by the new default (requirement 7); the service-type filter composes with the status filter exactly as before because the two resolve independently; Month/Year/Day views all read the same , so the change applies uniformly across views without touching view resolution; Prev/Next/date links carry the status via the existing  query builder, so the selected status survives navigation (requirement 6); invalid values (e.g. status=not_a_status) now fall back to All Statuses instead of Active (requirement 8) - a safe, superset fallback that can only widen, never hide, results.
- Tests: added one new test and rewrote two that encoded the old default. NEW Phase3B2A test 'falls back to all statuses for an invalid status parameter' posts status=not_a_status and asserts cancelled + declined history rows are visible. REWROTE Phase3B2A 'hides cancelled appointments by default and shows them via the status filter' -> 'shows cancelled appointments by default and still honors an explicit status filter' (now asserts the no-parameter request shows both cancelled and pending rows, status=pending narrows to pending only, and status=active hides cancelled). REWROTE the Phase3B2B test's mid-section comment/assertion (default now shows the cancelled row; status=active hides it; status=cancelled shows it). Results: Phase3B2AAppointmentsCalendarTest 24 passed / 93 assertions (was 23/85, +1 new test); Phase3B2BAppointmentManagementTest 49 passed / 359 assertions (unchanged count, updated assertions). Full suite: 1448 passed / 122 failed vs the pre-change baseline 1447/122 - the +1 passed is exactly the new fallback test and the 122 failures are the identical pre-existing legacy set - zero regressions. Preflight before every run: bootstrap/cache/config.php absent; phpunit.xml forces SQLite :memory: with force="true"; TestCase isolation guard active; php -l clean on all three changed files. No migrations/seeds/optimize/config:cache; no package installs; no git operations; MySQL/Railway untouched; appointment records and schema untouched.
- Defense notes: the default was changed in the single resolver that every view (month/year/day) already funnels through, so there is one authoritative fallback path rather than per-view special cases - a future status cannot appear selected-but-ignored. Falling back invalid values to the full superset ('all') rather than to Active is deliberate: a typo can only show MORE rows, never silently hide a cancelled/declined record a clinician was looking for, which is the safer failure mode for an audit-style view. The select's option order was moved to match the computed default so the rendered control agrees with the query behavior (what you see selected is what the request without parameters actually queried). Because the status filter is carried in every Prev/Next/date link via the existing  builder, the new default does not break deep links - explicit status=... URLs continue to resolve to that exact status, and only absent/invalid ones widen to All. Honest limitation: these are HTTP tests asserting rendered rows for the default/invalid/explicit cases; the Month/Year/Day parity and date-navigation status carry-over are verified by the pre-existing Phase3B2A navigation tests (which pass explicit status and confirm it is preserved) rather than by a new browser-level test.

## DEPLA Phase 3B.4: Appointment Notifications (Admin, Staff, Patient) (2026-07-10)

- Requirement: database-backed appointment notifications on the existing Laravel notification infrastructure - new ONLINE requests notify active Admin+Staff; confirmed/declined/cancelled/completed transitions notify the patient's linked, active Patient Portal account. No new tables, no email/SMS/push/WebSocket/queues/reminders. Inspection-first (12-item architecture map) confirmed: SystemNotificationService already sends database-only notifications to clinicRecipients() (all active admin+staff, archived excluded by User SoftDeletes), the notifications table (2026_08_20 migration) is a single shared Laravel-standard table (uuid id, type, morph notifiable, text data, read_at), existing payload contract is type/title/message/action_label/destination{route,parameters} (+event_key for dedupe), the clinic tray bell+dropdown lives in layouts.app via NotificationComposer (bound in AppServiceProvider), clinic notification routes sit inside the ['auth','clinic','no-store'] group with ownership-scoped mark-as-read (404 on foreign id), and every existing caller notifies AFTER its DB::transaction commits (send-after-commit is the house pattern; no afterCommit/queue infra exists). No architectural incompatibility found - the existing subsystem was extended, not replaced.
- New notification classes (5, all database-only via(), following ReferralCreatedNotification/PatientBecameHighRiskNotification conventions): app/Notifications/AppointmentRequestedNotification.php (clinic; type APPOINTMENT_REQUESTED; day-view deep link appointments.index?view=day&date={requested}&status=all - no patient identifier in the URL; message 'New appointment request: {Type} appointment submitted for {F j, Y}.'), AppointmentConfirmedNotification.php (patient; type APPOINTMENT_CONFIRMED; message uses the ACTUAL confirmed schedule in Philippine 12-hour display via a private formatClock() helper - 'Your {Type} appointment has been confirmed for {F j, Y} at {g:i A}.' - and omits the time clause for date-only delivery requests), AppointmentDeclinedNotification.php / AppointmentCancelledNotification.php / AppointmentCompletedNotification.php (patient; the detailed decline/cancellation reason is deliberately EXCLUDED and stays in the authorized My Appointments page). Every payload carries a stable event_key (appointment-requested:{id} / appointment-{status}:{id}) for the ledger dedupe, and destination.route = patient.appointments.index for the three patient-facing events. Appointment model gained an additive TYPE_LABELS const + getTypeLabelAttribute() accessor (mirrors Patient::getFormattedAddressAttribute precedent) used by the message builders.
- Service (app/Services/SystemNotificationService.php): two new public methods. notifyAppointmentRequested(Appointment): bool - refuses non-online source (walk-in guard), event_key appointment-requested:{id}, sendOnceTo(clinicRecipients(), ...) so one row per active clinic recipient. notifyAppointmentStatusChanged(Appointment, string ): bool - resolves the single recipient through activePortalUserFor() (patients.user_id -> users.id, User SoftDeletes global scope excludes archived accounts, role must be 'patient', null-safe when unlinked; no account is ever created), then sendOnceTo(collect([]), ...) with a match map over confirmed/declined/cancelled/completed (any other status no-ops). sendOnceTo() is a new private generalization that takes an arbitrary recipient collection + empty-guard; the existing private sendOnce() now delegates to it, so the three pre-existing event-keyed clinical paths (high-risk, EDD, past-EDD) are behaviorally unchanged.
- Dispatch wiring (send-after-commit, matching the prenatal/referral convention): PatientAppointmentController::store() now captures the Appointment returned by createOnlineRequest() and calls notifyAppointmentRequested() AFTER the service transaction has committed (DomainException path returns before the call - failed/duplicate submissions produce zero notifications). AppointmentManagementController injects SystemNotificationService and calls notifyAppointmentStatusChanged() AFTER each successful confirm/decline/cancel/complete service call (the return values are now captured); walkInStore() intentionally dispatches NOTHING (clinic staff already see the row they just created - no new-request notification for walk-ins). The AppointmentManagementService itself stays notification-free. Existing audit logging is untouched (audits remain INSIDE each transaction).
- Patient Portal surface (new): routes GET /patient/notifications (patient.notifications.index), POST /patient/notifications/{id}/read (patient.notifications.read), POST /patient/notifications/mark-all-read (patient.notifications.markAllRead) - all on the identical portal middleware chain ['auth','patient-portal-account','must-change-password','no-store']. New app/Http/Controllers/PatientNotificationController.php: index() paginates the AUTHENTICATED user's own notifications (paginate(10)->withQueryString(), newest first) + unread count; markAsRead() scopes through ->notifications()->where('id',...)->firstOrFail() (foreign id = 404, same model as the clinic controller); markAllAsRead() updates unreadNotifications() (inherently self-scoped); portalUserOrAbort() 403s non-patient roles (same in-controller pattern as PatientAppointmentController). New app/View/Composers/PatientNotificationComposer.php bound to patient.layout in AppServiceProvider (mirrors the clinic NotificationComposer): latest 5 + unread count for patient-role users only (clinic roles/guests get an empty state so the bell never leaks clinic-tray data). patient/layout.blade.php gained a Notifications nav item with a bell icon and an unread-count badge (aria-label, 99+ cap). New resources/views/patient/notifications/index.blade.php follows the portal's established conventions (rounded-2xl cards, uppercase section headings, per-section empty states, manual Previous/Next pagination with Showing X-Y of Z): unread items get a 3px green left border + unread dot + per-row POST 'Mark as read' button; items with a server-built destination route render an 'action_label' link; a 'Mark all as read' button appears whenever unreadCount > 0; timestamps render in Asia/Manila via ->setTimezone('Asia/Manila')->format('M j, Y g:i A') WITHOUT changing the global app timezone (config stays UTC).
- Tests: new tests/Feature/Phase3B4AppointmentNotificationsTest.php - 21 tests / 168 assertions with phase3b4* prefixed global helpers (phase3b4User/Patient/Link/Eligible/Appointment/ConfirmedAppointment/PostOnline/ActAsPatch/WalkIn/FreezeManila/Rows/RowFor, afterEach clears Carbon::setTestNow). Covers all 27 required scenarios: online request notifies active admin AND staff with the exact payload (type/title/message/event_key/deep link); archived staff excluded + submitting patient never self-notified; walk-in creation sends zero new-request notifications; invalid (past) submission and duplicate-rejected submission add nothing; confirmed appointment notifies the linked patient with the actual schedule and the '10:00 AM' format; date-only delivery confirmation omits the time clause; declined/cancelled/completed each notify with their exact messages and event keys (3 patient rows for 3 transitions); no linked account and archived (soft-deleted) portal account both no-op while the transition still succeeds; invalid transitions (double-confirm, decline-after-confirm) and FormRequest rejections (out-of-hours) produce zero notifications; event-key ledger makes a repeated service dispatch a no-op and a re-confirm attempt adds no second row; audit logs remain intact alongside the new notification rows; the existing clinic tray (dashboard bell) shows the appointment notification and consumes it via the existing notifications.read route; patient list shows only own notifications (cross-patient isolation by distinct appointment types); unread badge/empty-state/Manila-timestamp rendering; single mark-as-read works for the owner, a foreign notification id is 404, and mark-all leaves a third party's row unread; guests redirected, clinic roles 403 on portal routes, unlinked and archived patients 403, patients 403 on clinic notification routes; destinations built only from authorized named routes with no patient identifier in the calendar deep link; existing clinical-alert paths (notifyEddApproaching) still function; a mocked notification-transport failure returns 500 but the appointment stays CONFIRMED with its audit row (transaction already committed); Mail::fake()+Queue::fake() prove no email/SMS/queued jobs are generated and all five classes are database-only via() (checked via ReflectionClass::newInstanceWithoutConstructor). Updated tests/Feature/Phase3B3PatientAppointmentsTest.php: the 'sends no notification and writes no audit entry' test became 'writes no audit entry for a patient submission and notifies active clinic users' (audit still 0; notifications = 2 for one admin + one staff recipient), and its file-header docblock no longer claims zero notifications.
- Results: focused Phase3B4AppointmentNotificationsTest 21 passed / 168 assertions. Regression gates: Phase3B3 41 passed / 264 assertions; Phase3B2B 49 passed / 359; Phase3B2A 24 passed / 93; NotificationTest 13 passed / 45; ClinicalAlertNotificationTest 21 passed / 75; ClinicRouteAuthorizationTest 7 passed / 97 - all unchanged. Full suite: 1469 passed / 122 failed vs the pre-change baseline 1448/122 - the +21 passed are exactly the new tests and the 122 failures are the identical pre-existing legacy set (none appointment/notification-related) - zero regressions. Preflight before every run: bootstrap/cache/config.php absent; phpunit.xml forces SQLite :memory: with force='true'; TestCase isolation guard active; php -l clean on all 18 changed/new files; php artisan view:cache compiled all Blade cleanly (cleared after). No migrations/seeds/optimize/config:cache; no package installs; no git operations; MySQL/Railway untouched; public/hot untouched; no new DB columns or tables.
- Defense notes: notifications are dispatched ONLY after the owning DB::transaction has returned (commit already happened) - the exact send-after-commit convention every existing prenatal/referral caller already follows - so a failed or rolled-back transition can never leave an orphan notification; the DomainException catch blocks in both controllers sit BEFORE the notification call, so refused transitions (double-confirm, out-of-hours, duplicate online request) short-circuit with zero rows. Duplicate delivery has two independent layers: the service's lockForUpdate+assertStatus state-transition guards (each transition is once-only per appointment) plus a stable event_key ledger checked by sendOnceTo() against the notifications table - so even a repeated service-level dispatch is a no-op (proven by the test asserting the second notifyAppointmentRequested() returns false). A notification delivery failure cannot corrupt appointment state because the state change and its audit row are already committed inside the transaction; the failure surfaces as a 500 after commit and the test proves the appointment is still CONFIRMED with its audit intact. Recipient resolution is deliberately fail-closed: no linked patient row, a soft-deleted (archived) portal account, or a mis-linked non-patient role all resolve to no recipient and safely no-op - no portal account is ever auto-created. Privacy: the clinic-facing payload carries only appointment type + requested date (no patient name, contact number or clinical data) and the calendar deep link contains no patient identifier (view/date/status query params only, mirroring the existing calendar URL conventions); patient-facing messages contain no data the recipient does not already own, and the decline/cancellation reason is intentionally kept out of the notification row. Role isolation is enforced three ways: the portal middleware chain on the new routes, the in-controller portalUserOrAbort() role check (403 for clinic roles), and self-scoped queries through the authenticated user's own notifications()/unreadNotifications() relations (a foreign notification id is a 404, never a 403-with-hint); the existing clinic routes keep their ['auth','clinic'] boundary so patients remain 403 there. Honest limitation: the patient bell updates on full page render (same as the existing clinic composer - no polling/AJAX was added, per the no-new-infrastructure constraint), so a notification created in another tab appears after the next navigation; and the Phase3B.4 tests are HTTP/DB-level (asserting rows, payloads, status codes and rendered markup) rather than browser-level click-throughs of the Alpine bell toggle.

- Scope: re-inspected and re-verified the completed Phase 3B.4 work (the two feature entries above document the implementation in full) and closed one remaining coverage gap. This closes the only untested calendar requirement (status preservation across date/view navigation).

Regression gates unchanged: Phase3B3 41/264, Phase3B2B 49/359, NotificationTest 13/45, ClinicalAlertNotificationTest 21/75, ClinicRouteAuthorizationTest 7/97.
