## DEPLA Phase 3D.2A Step 1: appointment_change_requests schema + model (2026-10-12)

- Scope: implementation step 1 of the user-approved Phase 3D.2A cancellation design (schema + model only; NO runtime wiring). Four design decisions were explicitly approved before any code was written: (1) date-only cutoff interpretation - cancellation/reschedule requests on date-only (delivery) schedules are allowed through the END of the scheduled Manila day and refused from the next day, matching the existing pastScheduleIssue date-only treatment; (2) pending-request auto-resolution - when Admin/Staff cancel or complete an appointment that still has a pending change request, the service will auto-close the request in the SAME transaction (cancellation request -> approved with an explanatory review note; reschedule request -> declined), preserving the invariant that a pending request exists only while its appointment is confirmed; (3) reschedule reasons are optional (cancellation reasons remain required, per the approved workflow rules); (4) NO database-level duplicate constraint - duplicate/pending-request concurrency is prevented by lock-first ordering (every operation locks the appointment row via lockForUpdate before reading/inserting request rows), because a unique(appointment_id, status) would wrongly cap a lifetime at one approved request and MySQL does not support partial unique indexes.
- New additive migration database/migrations/2026_10_12_000001_create_appointment_change_requests_table.php: appointment_id FK RESTRICT (request history never destroyed); type string(16) cancellation|reschedule; proposed_date date nullable + proposed_time time nullable (NULL for cancellation; time nullable so date-only delivery reschedules stay legal); reason text nullable (required-for-cancellation is enforced in the FormRequest/service, not the schema); status string(16) default pending (pending|approved|declined); reviewed_by FK users SET NULL + reviewed_at timestamp + review_note text nullable; timestamps (created_at IS the requested timestamp - no duplicate column, deliberate); indexes on appointment_id, status and [appointment_id, status]. NO soft deletes (resolved requests are immutable low-volume history; a deleted_at scope would complicate the one-pending invariant). Rollback refuses to drop a populated table (same history-preservation guard as the appointments and pregnancy_outcomes migrations). The FILE is created only - execution against local MySQL or Railway still requires separate explicit approval; automated tests exercise it on SQLite :memory: only.
- New model app/Models/AppointmentChangeRequest.php: data holder only (no cutoff/one-pending/approve logic - those belong to AppointmentManagementService under the appointment row lock). Constants TYPE_CANCELLATION/TYPE_RESCHEDULE + TYPE_LABELS, STATUS_PENDING/APPROVED/DECLINED + STATUS_LABELS; fillable matches the schema; casts proposed_date->date and reviewed_at->datetime (time stays a string, matching Appointment); relations appointment() and reviewer() (reviewed_by, SET NULL, deliberately separate from Appointment::reviewer() so approval never overwrites the original confirmer provenance); getTypeLabelAttribute()/getStatusLabelAttribute() accessors for calendar/portal/notification display.
- One deliberate test update: tests/Feature/Phase3B3PatientAppointmentsTest.php schema-snapshot guard (was "does not create a second appointment table...") renamed to "does not add unexpected appointment tables or mutate the appointments schema" and now asserts the EXACT set {appointment_change_requests, appointments} after a patient submission - the guard stays tight (any unknown third table fails) while acknowledging the approved additive table. The patient submission itself and all other assertions in the file are unchanged (41 passed / 263 assertions after the update).
- Verification (chunked fresh-process runs - see environment finding below): appointment-domain gates ALL GREEN - Phase3B1+3B2A+3B2B+3B3+3B4+NotificationTest+ClinicalAlertNotificationTest+ClinicRouteAuthorizationTest run: 198 passed / 1 failed (the snapshot guard, found, fixed, then Phase3B3 re-run 41/41 green); C2A (Phase2*+3A+3B*) 297 passed / 0 failed; Unit+Auth+SchemaContract+PregnancyOutcomeMigration+ReferralMigrationRollbackSafety 426 passed / 1 legacy (AssessmentContextTest 20.0-vs-20 float/int, pre-existing, pure value object, unrelated); Feature A-P chunk 288 passed / 59 legacy; Feature P-U chunk 262 passed / 38 legacy - failures confined to the known legacy set (ExplainabilitySprint7, PatientViewController, ReferralAnalytics, PregnancyOutcomePhase17E, etc.), none appointment-related. Preflight before every run: bootstrap/cache/config.php absent; phpunit.xml forces APP_ENV=testing / DB_CONNECTION=sqlite / DB_DATABASE=:memory: with force='true'; TestCase isolation guard active; php -l clean on both new files; BOM check clean.
- Environment finding (pre-existing, NOT caused by this change): a MONOLITHIC php artisan test full-suite run now hangs context-dependently on this machine inside legacy-failing UI test files - output stops immediately after a failure dump (first observed after Phase16EUiTest I2, hang in test J; reproduced again after PregnancyOutcomePhase17ETest AFK, hang in test AFL), and the affected tests pass when run standalone or as whole files (J: 1.18s pass; AFL: 2.07s legacy-fail; whole Phase16EUiTest file: 3 failed / 24 passed in 2.50s). Rigorous pre-existence proof: the hanging C2B file set (PregnancyOutcome*+PrenatalVisit*+Profile*) was re-run with the two Phase 3D.2A files TEMPORARILY MOVED OUT of the project - it hung at the identical stop point with a near-byte-identical output dump (897664 vs 897646 bytes), then the files were restored and verified present. The hang therefore predates and is independent of this change; full-suite coverage was completed via fresh-process chunked runs of the identical file set (all 81 top-level Feature files + Unit/Auth subsets), which is the workaround to use until the environment hang is investigated separately.
- Defense notes: NO runtime wiring in this step - no routes, no controller methods, no service methods, no Blade changes, no notifications, no audit actions - so there is zero behavioral surface for clinical workflows; the appointments table and every existing column/status/transition are untouched; the new table exists but nothing writes to it yet. No migration was executed against MySQL; no local MySQL or Railway changes; no .env or configuration changes; no package installs; no git operations; public/hot untouched. Patient safety: the schema encodes no clinical thresholds and makes no scheduling decisions - cutoff, ownership, source and approval rules will be implemented (with re-checks under lock) in Phase 3D.2B, and the lock-first concurrency foundation chosen here is what those rules will rely on.
## DEPLA Phase 3D.2B: Patient Cancellation (direct pending + confirmed request) (2026-10-12)

- Requirement: patient-side cancellation for ONLINE appointments only, using the Phase 3D.2A schema (appointment_change_requests, no new migrations/columns). PENDING online appointments: the owning patient cancels DIRECTLY (reason 3-1000 required; pending->cancelled in one lockForUpdate transaction; row kept as history; reviewed_* untouched; in-transaction audit PATIENT_CANCELLED_PENDING; clinic notified after commit; no change-request row; no patient self-notification). CONFIRMED online appointments: the owning patient submits an approval-gated cancellation REQUEST (reason 3-1000 required; ONE appointment_change_requests row type=cancellation status=pending created under the appointment row lock; appointment STAYS confirmed with confirmed_date/time byte-identical; in-transaction audit PATIENT_REQUESTED_CANCELLATION; clinic notified after commit; no automatic cancel). Eligibility: online source only, own patientAccount link only, pending/confirmed only, before the approved Manila cutoff (timed = strictly before the start minute; date-only = through the end of the scheduled day), one pending change request per appointment across all types, duplicate/concurrent submissions refused by the lock-first re-check. Staff approve/decline and rescheduling deliberately NOT implemented (later steps); walk-ins keep the existing staff workflow untouched.
- Service (app/Services/AppointmentManagementService.php): two new public methods, both in ONE DB::transaction with lockAppointment() first. cancelByPatient(Appointment, Patient, User, reason): asserts patient-owned + online source (assertPatientOwnedOnline, new private), status=pending (existing assertStatus), cutoff (new assertNotScheduledStartPassed -> public static scheduledStartPassedIssue, symmetric with pastScheduleIssue: Manila-anchored, re-read inside the tx; timed uses lte(startOfMinute) so the start minute itself is refused; date-only allows the whole scheduled day; message wording names the passed schedule and current Philippine time), then stores cancellation_reason and audits PATIENT_CANCELLED_PENDING with the patient portal user as actor. requestAppointmentCancellation(Appointment, Patient, User, reason): same ownership/source/status(confirmed)/cutoff asserts, then locks+checks for any pending change request (any type) and refuses with a DomainException, then inserts the request row (proposed_* NULL) and audits PATIENT_REQUESTED_CANCELLATION in the same transaction. normalizeReason (3-1000) is re-applied at the write boundary for defence in depth. Appointment reviewed_by/reviewed_at are never touched by either patient path; the appointments table gains no columns.
- Notifications (send-after-commit, event_key ledger dedupe via the existing sendOnceTo): SystemNotificationService gained notifyPatientCancelledPendingAppointment(Appointment) (online-source guard; event_key appointment-patient-cancelled:{id}) and notifyAppointmentCancellationRequested(AppointmentChangeRequest) (type+pending guard; event_key appointment-cancel-requested:{requestId}); two new database-only classes AppointmentPatientCancelledNotification (APPOINTMENT_PATIENT_CANCELLED; message carries type label + requested date only; day-view deep link appointments.index?view=day&date={requested}&status=all) and AppointmentCancellationRequestedNotification (APPOINTMENT_CANCELLATION_REQUESTED; type label + confirmed-date fallback requested; no patient name/contact/clinical data and no patient reason in either payload). The patient is never notified about their own action; both dispatches happen only after the service transaction returns, and the DomainException catch paths dispatch nothing.
- Portal endpoints (routes/web.php, same ['auth','patient-portal-account','must-change-password','no-store'] chain): POST /patient/appointments/{appointmentId}/cancel -> PatientAppointmentController::cancel and POST /patient/appointments/{appointmentId}/cancellation-requests -> cancellationRequest. Both resolve the appointment through the OWN patient's relation (foreign id OR missing id = 404) and forward ONLY the validated reason into the service; DomainException surfaces as back()->withInput()->withErrors(['reason']). New shared FormRequest PatientAppointmentCancelRequest (authorize patient role; reason required|string|min:3|max:1000 - identical to the staff AppointmentCancelRequest rules so the surfaces cannot drift). Index() now eager-loads the new Appointment::pendingChangeRequest() HasOne (status=pending, across both change types, latest('id') tie-break) on the upcoming and history queries; grouping and History pagination are otherwise byte-for-byte unchanged.
- Portal card UI (resources/views/patient/appointments/partials/card.blade.php): eligibility computed SERVER-SIDE in the partial from the same public scheduledStartPassedIssue the service enforces. Eligible PENDING online cards render a pure-HTML <details> Cancel Appointment disclosure with a CSRF POST form (reason textarea 3-1000, inline @error('reason')); eligible CONFIRMED online cards render a Request Cancellation disclosure with copy stating the appointment stays confirmed until review; a confirmed card with a pending change request renders an amber "Cancellation request pending - awaiting clinic review" badge plus the patient's own reason and NO action forms. Walk-ins, terminal statuses, past-cutoff rows and non-online sources stay fully read-only exactly as before; no staff-only details (reviewer, ids) are rendered. No JavaScript/Alpine dependency was added (details/summary disclosure).
- Tests: new tests/Feature/Phase3D2BPatientCancellationTest.php - 25 passed / 167 assertions with phase3d2b* prefixed helpers (Freeze/Eligible/PendingAppointment/ConfirmedAppointment/CancelPost/RequestPost/notification-row helpers; afterEach clears Carbon::setTestNow). Covers: direct cancel success (status/reason/audit actor+module/2 clinic notifications with exact event_key/payload privacy/no patient self-notification); reason validation refusals with zero side effects; walk-in refusal both paths; cutoff at the start minute refused and one minute before allowed; date-only delivery cancellable on its scheduled day; terminal-status and wrong-path refusals (confirmed must use the request path); repeated submit -> one audit, no duplicate notifications; event-key ledger makes a repeated service dispatch a no-op; confirmed request success (appointment confirmed schedule byte-identical, request row shape, audit, clinic notifications with privacy); request reason validation; walk-in/pending/past-cutoff request refusals; one-pending-per-appointment (second refused, new one allowed after resolution); pending-review badge + hidden actions in the portal card; staff cancel still fully works while a patient request is pending (existing staff functionality preserved; request row left for the review step); foreign appointment 404 on both endpoints; forged patient_id/status/source in the body ignored; guest redirect/login, clinic-role 403, unlinked 403, archived-patient-record 403 (house convention); the cutoff helper matrix (equal minute, yesterday, next minute, future day, date-only day boundary); and card eligibility rendering for eligible/walk-in/past/terminal cards.
- Regression gates (fresh process, preflight confirmed before every run - bootstrap/cache/config.php absent, phpunit.xml forces SQLite :memory: with force='true', TestCase isolation guard active): Phase3B1+3B2A+3B2B+3B3+3B4+3D2B+3A+2D+NotificationTest+ClinicalAlertNotificationTest+ClinicRouteAuthorizationTest combined run: 278 passed / 1682 assertions - ALL GREEN, including the unchanged staff management suites (3B2B 49), portal suites (3A, 2D), notification suites (3B4 21, NotificationTest 13, ClinicalAlert 21) and route authorization (7). The pre-existing monolithic full-suite hang was not re-encountered because the focused gates complete in ~24s in one process; the chunked-run workaround from Phase 3D.2A remains available if a wider sweep is requested. php -l clean and BOM-free on all 9 changed/new PHP files; Blade view:cache compiled all templates cleanly (cleared after).
- Defense notes: every patient mutation re-derives ALL server-owned facts (ownership via own-relation query + in-tx re-assert, online source, status, cutoff, one-pending) inside a single lockForUpdate transaction - the client can submit only a reason, so a forged patient_id/status/source/confirmed schedule is ignored by construction (proven by the forged-payload test); the direct-cancel and request paths share one FormRequest and one cutoff helper with the service, so the surfaces cannot drift, and the staff cancel/decline/confirm/complete transitions are byte-for-byte untouched (regression-proven); failed operations write no audit and dispatch no notification because both live strictly inside/after the committed transaction respectively, and the event_key ledger makes even a repeated post-commit dispatch a no-op. Walk-in exclusion is enforced twice (route-level patient scoping cannot see other patients' rows; the service refuses non-online sources with a message pointing to the clinic). Privacy: clinic notification payloads carry appointment type + schedule date only - never patient name, contact, clinical data or the patient's reason - and deep links contain no patient identifier; the reason stays on the appointment/request row and audit log, visible only to authorized clinic users. Honest limitations: (1) auto-resolution of a still-pending change request when staff later cancel/complete the appointment is deliberately deferred to the staff-review/hardening step (this step's test documents the deferral - staff cancel succeeds and the request row remains for the review step to resolve); (2) staff approve/decline UI/actions do not exist yet, so a pending request currently has no reviewer path - the next step adds it; (3) the card forms repopulate old('reason') into both disclosures after a validation redirect (cosmetic only; one form is used per submission); (4) HTTP/DB-level tests assert rendered markup and behaviour, not painted-browser click-throughs of the details/summary disclosure.
## DEPLA Phase 3D.2C: Admin/Staff Review of Patient Cancellation Requests (2026-10-12)

- Requirement: staff-side review of patient-submitted cancellation requests on confirmed ONLINE appointments, using the Phase 3D.2A schema (no new migrations/columns). APPROVE: one DB::transaction, appointment row locked FIRST then the request row (same lock-first ordering as requestAppointmentCancellation, so the two paths serialize); revalidate request type=cancellation, status=pending, appointment status=confirmed, source=online and the approved Manila cutoff; appointment -> cancelled with the PATIENT's original reason copied into cancellation_reason (the reviewer never re-types it); request -> approved with reviewed_by/reviewed_at and an OPTIONAL staff note (nullable, 3-1000 when present) on review_note; the appointment's original reviewed_by/reviewed_at (the confirmer) are NEVER overwritten; in-transaction audit CANCELLATION_REQUEST_APPROVED; after commit the patient's linked active portal account receives exactly ONE notification (AppointmentCancellationApprovedNotification, event_key appointment-cancellation-approved:{requestId}) - deliberately NOT also the generic status-changed notification, so approval never double-notifies. DECLINE: same locks and revalidation EXCEPT no scheduled-start cutoff (declining changes nothing on the appointment, so a stale request can always be cleaned up as explained in the service docblock); appointment stays confirmed with its schedule byte-identical; request -> declined with a REQUIRED explanation (3-1000); audit CANCELLATION_REQUEST_DECLINED; one patient notification (AppointmentCancellationDeclinedNotification) whose payload deliberately EXCLUDES the explanation (portal card only, Phase 3B.4 reasons-never-in-notifications convention). AUTO-RESOLUTION (Phase 3D.2A decision 2, now implemented): cancel() and complete() call autoResolvePendingChangeRequests() INSIDE their existing transaction after the appointment mutation and its audit - pending cancellation + staff cancel -> auto-approved; pending cancellation + staff complete and any pending reschedule -> auto-declined; reviewed_* and an explanatory review_note are stamped exactly like a manual review; one extra audit row per request (CANCELLATION_REQUEST_AUTO_APPROVED / CHANGE_REQUEST_AUTO_DECLINED); NO extra patient notification from auto-resolution (the appointment's own status notification for the cancel/complete already covers the outcome - proven by test).
- Service (app/Services/AppointmentManagementService.php): two new public methods approveCancellationRequest()/declineCancellationRequest() plus three new private internals (lockChangeRequest - re-reads the request under lock, ALWAYS after lockAppointment so lock order is uniform; assertOnlineSource - structural walk-in refusal even for directly-inserted fixture rows; autoResolvePendingChangeRequests - the matrix above). assertStatus() gained two match arms (approve_cancellation / decline_cancellation) for readable refusal wording. cancel() and complete() gained their single autoResolvePendingChangeRequests() call; every other byte of the four existing staff transitions is untouched (regression-proven by the unchanged Phase3B2B suite). Class docblock documents the full 3D.2C contract including the deliberate no-cutoff-on-decline and no-extra-notification-on-auto-resolution decisions.
- Controller/routes/requests: new app/Http/Controllers/AppointmentChangeRequestController.php (thin, mirrors AppointmentManagementController: service call in try/catch DomainException; on failure back()->with('errorRequestId')->with('errorAppointmentId')->withErrors(['change_request']) so the calendar's existing errorAppointmentId auto-open reopens the details modal with the error; on success post-commit notification then back()->with('success')). Two new PATCH routes in the existing ['auth','clinic','no-store'] group: /appointment-change-requests/{changeRequest}/approve|decline (named appointment-change-requests.approve/decline; implicit model binding gives 404 for invalid ids; PATCH only, no GET mutation). Two new FormRequests: AppointmentChangeRequestApproveRequest (admin|staff authorize; review_note nullable|string|min:3|max:1000) and AppointmentChangeRequestDeclineRequest (review_note REQUIRED 3-1000).
- Notifications (send-after-commit, event_key ledger dedupe via the existing sendOnceTo): SystemNotificationService gained notifyAppointmentCancellationApproved()/notifyAppointmentCancellationDeclined() (type+status guards, single active-portal-user recipient via activePortalUserFor, event keys appointment-cancellation-approved:{id} / appointment-cancellation-declined:{id}); two new database-only classes AppointmentCancellationApprovedNotification (message names the type label + confirmed-date-fallback schedule and the cancelled outcome) and AppointmentCancellationDeclinedNotification (message says the request was not approved and the appointment remains confirmed - explanation excluded). Payload privacy matches the Phase3B4 contract: no patient name/contact/clinical data, no patient reason, no staff explanation.
- Calendar UI: AppointmentCalendarController eventsGroupedByEffectiveDate batch-loads pending cancellation-request ids (no N+1) and eventPayload carries hasPendingCancellationRequest; detailsMap batch-loads pending cancellation requests and merges a cancellationRequest block ({id, reason, requestedAtLabel in Manila g:i A, approveUrl, declineUrl} or null). calendar.blade.php: month chips render an amber "!" marker with aria/title when the flag is set; the day row renders an orange "Cancellation Request" pill; the legend gains a "Cancellation request pending" entry; the details modal gains an orange Pending Cancellation Request panel (submitted-at, patient reason, approve/decline consequence copy) shown only while cancellationRequest is non-null; the confirmed-state action area gains Approve Cancellation / Decline Cancellation Request buttons (the existing Cancel/Mark Completed buttons remain - using them auto-resolves the request in-tx); two new Alpine action forms (approveRequest: optional staff note, shows patient reason + consequence copy; declineRequest: required explanation textarea) following the existing confirm/decline/cancel/complete form conventions (x-show + CSRF + _method PATCH + @error('change_request') banner + Back button).
- Patient portal: Appointment::latestCancellationRequest() HasOne (type=cancellation, latest('id')); PatientAppointmentController::index eager-loads it alongside pendingChangeRequest on upcoming+history; card.blade.php renders the review OUTCOME (never shown while pending, which the existing badge owns): declined request on a still-confirmed card shows a red banner with the clinic's explanation + "Your appointment stays confirmed"; approved request on a cancelled card shows a grey "approved / cancelled as you requested" banner. No staff-only details (reviewer, ids) are rendered.
- Tests: new tests/Feature/Phase3D2CAppointmentChangeRequestReviewTest.php - 25 passed / 136 assertions with phase3d2c* prefixed helpers (Freeze/Eligible/ConfirmedAppointment/PendingRequest via the real service path/FixtureRequest for rows the patient path cannot create/Patch/notification-row helpers; afterEach clears Carbon::setTestNow). Covers: approve success (status/reason copy/request stamping/original-reviewer preserved/audit/single patient notification with exact type+event_key/no status-changed double); empty optional note; twice-refused with no duplicate state/audit/notification; cutoff refusal at the start minute (request stays pending, appointment confirmed); walk-in source refusal; non-cancellation type refusal; decline success (appointment untouched/schedule byte-identical/required explanation stored/audit); decline validation refusals (422 on empty and too-short); decline notification privacy (explanation not in payload); stale decline after approve; authorization matrix (patient 403 both endpoints, guest redirect to login FIRST in the test before any actingAs, invalid id 404); stale approve after staff direct cancel (auto-approved in-tx, only the cancelled-status patient notification exists, auto-approval audit row) and after staff complete (auto-declined, completed-status notification only); reschedule-type fixture auto-declined on staff cancel; staff cancel with no pending request writes zero auto-resolution audit rows (regression); calendar day-details block with review URLs; block still present under status=confirmed filter; month chip marker + day badge rendering; flag removed after review ("cancellationRequest":null); portal declined-explanation card; portal approved-outcome card; event-key dedupe for both notifications (second dispatch false, one row); schema contract (appointment_change_requests columns exact, appointments 16 columns exact, no delivery_admissions/bed_occupancies). One deliberate 3D.2B test update: 'preserves staff cancellation of a confirmed appointment while a patient request is pending' now asserts the request is auto-APPROVED by the staff cancel (the 3D.2B test previously asserted the deferred pending state, which 3D.2C supersedes; the appointment-preservation assertions are unchanged).
- Verification (fresh process, preflight confirmed before every run - bootstrap/cache/config.php absent, phpunit.xml forces SQLite :memory: with force='true', TestCase isolation guard active): Phase3D2C 25/25 green standalone; regression gate in three fresh-process chunks: (1) Phase3B1+3B2A+3B2B 96 passed; (2) Phase3B3+3B4+3D2B 87 passed (after the one documented 3D.2B assertion update); (3) NotificationTest+ClinicalAlertNotificationTest+ClinicRouteAuthorizationTest 41 passed. Combined 249 passed / 0 failed across the appointment domain and its notification/route-authorization dependents. php -l clean on all 10 changed/new PHP files; Blade templates compile (calendar and card partials rendered by the passing HTTP tests).
- Defense notes: every review re-derives ALL server-owned facts (request type/status, appointment status/source, cutoff for approve) inside a single lockForUpdate transaction with the appointment locked before the request - a stale double-click, a concurrent staff cancel/complete or a directly-forged request row can never produce an inconsistent outcome (each is refused with a DomainException and zero writes); the decline path deliberately skips the cutoff because it mutates nothing on the appointment, so cleanup of a stale pending request is always possible; auto-resolution runs strictly inside the caller's existing transaction so a cancelled appointment can never be observed with a still-pending request after commit, and it writes its own audit rows without altering the pre-existing staff-action audit rows. Notification discipline: exactly ONE patient notification per review outcome, sent only after commit, ledger-deduped by event_key; auto-resolution sends none (the status notification covers it - proven by the exact-count tests); decline explanations never leave the authorized portal card. Privacy: staff review notes stay on the request row + audit log; calendar payload carries the patient's reason only to the authorized clinic boundary (same as every other detail field); portal outcome banners render no staff-only identifiers. Honest limitations: (1) reschedule REQUESTS and staff rescheduling are Phase 3D.3 - this step only auto-declines stray reschedule-type fixtures and refuses them on approve/decline; (2) the calendar month/year views do not show the request block (only the chip flag) - review happens in the details modal, same as all other management actions; (3) HTTP/DB-level tests assert rendered markup and behaviour, not painted-browser click-throughs of the Alpine action forms; (4) the pre-existing monolithic full-suite hang (documented in Phase 3D.2A) was not re-encountered because the focused gates complete in ~14s in fresh processes.
## DEPLA Phase 3D.3B Step 1: Patient Portal Reschedule Request Submission - backend only (2026-10-12)

- Requirement: the owning patient proposes a NEW schedule for their own CONFIRMED ONLINE appointment, using the existing Phase 3D.2A schema (NO migrations/columns). Approved K-decisions applied: K1 (expired proposed schedule => refuse approval, request stays pending) belongs to the later approval step; K2 exact-duplicate guards are enforced at SUBMISSION (this step) and will be re-checked authoritatively at approval; K3 (notify active Admin/Staff on submission) is DEFERRED to a later step - documented in the controller/service docblocks and listed as a remaining item, deliberately NOT silently omitted (this step dispatches zero notifications, proven by exact-count tests); K4 (approval applies the patient's exact proposed schedule, no staff editing) and K5-consistent storage (delivery proposals ALWAYS date-only, proposed_time NULL) apply from here; K6 (calendar type-aware indicators) is a later UI step. Eligibility: own patientAccount link only, online source, confirmed status, before the approved Manila cutoff, and ONE pending change request per appointment across BOTH types. The appointment row is NEVER mutated by submission - confirmed_date, confirmed_time, status and reviewed_* stay byte-identical until staff review.
- Service (app/Services/AppointmentManagementService.php): new public requestAppointmentReschedule(Appointment, Patient, User, string $date, ?string $time, ?string $reason): AppointmentChangeRequest in ONE DB::transaction with the same lock-first discipline (lockAppointment BEFORE any request row read/write, so a concurrent reschedule vs cancellation serializes and the one-pending check is race-free). Order inside the tx: assertPatientOwnedOnline -> assertStatus(confirmed, 'request rescheduling for') -> assertNotScheduledStartPassed (same Manila cutoff as cancellation: timed strictly before start minute; date-only through end of scheduled day) -> pending change request lock-check across both types (same wording as the cancellation path) -> K5 force $time=null for TYPE_DELIVERY_REQUEST BEFORE validation -> shared validateSchedule (calendar checkdate, clinic hours for checkup/counseling, Manila future rule) -> collision guard 1 (proposal identical to the CURRENT confirmed schedule refused: confirmed schedule with confirmed_date fallback to requested; date-only compares null==null, so an identical date-only delivery proposal is refused too) -> new private assertNoConflictingActiveAppointment (collision guard 2: same patient + same type, status pending|confirmed, whereKeyNot the appointment being rescheduled, effective schedule = confirmed when a confirmed date exists else requested, exact date+time compare in PHP over the patient's same-type active rows - cannot be one indexed WHERE across the confirmed/requested fallback, clinic-scale volume, same honest read-before-insert limitation as createOnlineRequest) -> optional reason (null/blank stores NULL, else trim via normalizeReason 3-1000) -> insert one appointment_change_requests row (type=reschedule, proposed_date/time, status=pending) -> in-transaction audit PATIENT_REQUESTED_RESCHEDULE (confirmed->confirmed, description carries proposed schedule, actor and request id). Class docblock extended with the full 3D.3B Step 1 contract.
- FormRequest (app/Http/Requests/PatientAppointmentRescheduleRequest.php, NEW): authorize = patient role only. targetAppointment() resolves the appointment server-side through the authenticated account's OWN patientAccount->appointments() link (never from input); an unresolvable/foreign appointment or a clinic role leaves the time rule lenient so the controller's own-relation firstOrFail owns the 404/403. Rules: proposed_date required|string|date_format:Y-m-d; proposed_time = required+date_format:H:i+AppointmentConfirmRequest::clinicHoursRules() (spread as separate elements) ONLY when the target is checkup/counseling, nullable for delivery (K5) and for unresolvable targets; reason nullable|string|min:3|max:1000. withValidator adds the shared AppointmentManagementService::pastScheduleIssue on the PROPOSED fields (field-mapped errors, skipped when format rules already failed) using an EFFECTIVE time of null for delivery so the form can never refuse something the date-only service path accepts. One-pending, cutoff, identical-to-current and active-duplicate checks are deliberately NOT duplicated here - they stay service-authoritative under the lock.
- Controller/routes: PatientAppointmentController::rescheduleRequest() mirrors cancellationRequest exactly - portalUserOrAbort (patient role), resolveOwnPatient (friendly 403 for unlinked/archived, house convention), own-relation whereKey firstOrFail (foreign id = 404), service call with ONLY validated proposed_date/proposed_time/reason, DomainException -> back()->withInput()->withErrors(['appointment']) (distinct key from the cancellation path's 'reason'), success -> redirect patient.appointments.index with a success flash stating the appointment stays confirmed until review. Its docblock explicitly records the K3 deferral (clinic submission notice belongs to a later step together with approve/decline endpoints and outcome notifications). One new route in routes/web.php after the cancellation-request route: POST /patient/appointments/{appointmentId}/reschedule-requests, named patient.appointments.rescheduleRequest, same ['auth','patient-portal-account','must-change-password','no-store'] middleware chain (auth runs before FormRequest authorize, so guests still redirect to login).
- Notifications: NONE dispatched by design in this step (K3 deferred). No SystemNotificationService method, no notification class and no event key was added yet; the later step will add the clinic submission notification plus the APPROVED/DECLINED patient notifications and the staff approve/decline endpoints. UI: none in this step either - the Patient Portal reschedule form and the calendar type-aware indicators (K6) are deferred per the approved step scope; backend behavior is fully proven by HTTP tests before any UI is built.
- Tests: new tests/Feature/Phase3D3BPatientRescheduleTest.php - 29 passed / 152 assertions, phase3d3* prefixed helpers (User/Patient/Link/Eligible/ConfirmedAppointment/DeliveryAppointment/PendingAppointment/ReschedulePost/CancelRequestPost/Freeze; afterEach clears Carbon::setTestNow). Covers: submission atomicity (one pending type=reschedule row with exact proposed fields, appointment byte-identical including updated_at and original reviewer, zero audit collateral, PATIENT_REQUESTED_RESCHEDULE audit row with patient_id, redirect+success flash); explicit zero-notification contract for the deferred K3; reason optional+trimmed (blank/omitted -> NULL) and 2-char/1001-char refusals with zero rows; proposed_date required and format refusal; proposed_time required for checkup plus 07:00/17:00 out-of-hours refusals and 08:00/16:59 boundary acceptances; past proposed date refused; earlier-than-now time today refused with the equal minute allowed; delivery K5 (submitted time ignored -> proposed_time stored NULL, time omitted also NULL); date-only delivery cutoff (through the end of the scheduled day allowed, next day refused as 'appointment'); timed cutoff (start minute and later refused with no row/audit, one minute before allowed); guard 1 (identical checkup refused, same date different time allowed, identical date-only delivery refused); guard 2 (matching another confirmed same-type refused, another pending same-type refused via its requested schedule, matching a DIFFERENT type allowed, self excluded by whereKeyNot); one-pending BOTH directions (cancellation pending blocks reschedule, reschedule pending blocks cancellation with zero PATIENT_REQUESTED_CANCELLATION audit); walk-in refusal; non-confirmed (pending) refusal; foreign id 404; service-direct call passing a different patient throws DomainException with zero writes (ownership enforced below the controller too); authorization matrix (guest redirect to login, staff 403, admin 403, unlinked patient 403, archived patient record 403 - no rows, no audit); forged appointment_type/status/source/confirmed_*/patient_id in the body all ignored (row type stays reschedule, appointment and foreign patient untouched); sequential double submit refused (one row, one audit); and the schema contract (appointments 16 columns exact, appointment_change_requests 12 columns exact, no delivery_admissions/bed_occupancies - no Phase 3D migrations).
- Verification (preflight confirmed before every run - bootstrap/cache/config.php absent, phpunit.xml forces SQLite :memory: with force='true', TestCase isolation guard active): focused Phase3D3B 29/29 green; regression gates in fresh-process chunks: (1) Phase3B1+3B2A+3B2B+3D2B 121 passed / 742 assertions; (2) Phase3B3+3B4 62 passed / 431 assertions; (3) Phase3D2C 25 passed / 136 assertions; (4) NotificationTest+ClinicalAlertNotificationTest+ClinicRouteAuthorizationTest 41 passed / 217 assertions. Combined 278 passed / 0 failed. One transient defect was caught and fixed before the final gate: the first service edit accidentally consumed the opening lines of the approveCancellationRequest docblock (ParseError on class load, surfaced by the first test run as 28 failures); the docblock was restored, php -l clean on all 5 changed/new PHP files, then the full focused+gate sequence re-run green.
- Defense notes: submission is read-only toward the appointment - every server-owned fact (patient identity, appointment type, status/source, cutoff, one-pending, both collision guards) is re-derived inside the lockForUpdate transaction, so a forged body can only ever propose date/time/reason and nothing else (proven by the forged-payload and service-direct tests); guard 1 and guard 2 are both enforced at submission (K2) and will be RE-CHECKED at approval, where a proposal could have become stale (e.g. a second appointment created meanwhile); the identical-schedule refusal is service-level with appointment-level intent (the FormRequest only owns format/clinic-hours/past rules), keeping the patient surface and any future staff surface on one code path. Honest limitations: (1) no staff approve/decline for reschedule exists yet - a pending reschedule request currently has no reviewer path (next step: approve/decline endpoints applying K1/K2/K4 + K5 confirmed_time=null on approval); (2) K3 clinic submission notification is intentionally not yet implemented; (3) no Patient Portal form/card or calendar indicator yet - submission is proven at the HTTP layer first; (4) guard 2 is a read-before-insert check under the appointment lock, not a DB constraint (documented in the helper docblock); (5) the pre-existing monolithic full-suite hang was not re-encountered - focused gates complete in ~16s across fresh processes, and the chunked-run workaround from Phase 3D.2A remains in force.
## DEPLA Phase 3D.3B Step 2: Staff Reschedule Review Backend (2026-10-12)

- Requirement: Admin/Staff approval and decline of patient reschedule requests, backend only (no notifications, no new UI - both deferred to a later step with separate approval). Approved rules implemented exactly: only PENDING reschedule requests on CONFIRMED ONLINE appointments may be reviewed; approval applies the patient's EXACT proposed schedule (K4 - staff cannot edit the proposal during approval; extra posted schedule fields are ignored by the shared FormRequest); the original confirmed schedule stays unchanged until approval; decline keeps the appointment confirmed with its original schedule; approval revalidates BOTH the current appointment cutoff (Asia/Manila) AND the proposed schedule (format/clinic hours/future rule); decline requires an explanation (shared required FormRequest); EXPIRED proposals cannot be approved but may always be declined (decline has no cutoff by design - it mutates nothing, so stale requests can be cleaned up instead of stranded); exact duplicate active appointments are refused AT APPROVAL (the authoritative K2 re-check, since a conflicting appointment can appear between submission and review); delivery rescheduling is date-only - confirmed_time is written NULL even if a forged row carries a stray proposed_time; the appointment's original reviewed_by/reviewed_at (the confirmer) are preserved - reviewer provenance lives on the REQUEST row and in the audit.
- Service (app/Services/AppointmentManagementService.php): two new public methods mirroring the Phase 3D.2C cancellation review pair, each in ONE DB::transaction with lockAppointment() BEFORE lockChangeRequest() (identical lock order to every other review/auto-resolution path, so no deadlock and stale double-clicks serialize; the loser re-reads the already-changed state and is refused). approveRescheduleRequest(AppointmentChangeRequest, User, ?string note): in-transaction revalidation chain = type=reschedule -> status=pending (stale repeats refused with the status-named wording) -> assertOnlineSource -> assertStatus(confirmed, new 'approve_reschedule' match arm) -> assertNotScheduledStartPassed (current cutoff) -> K5 delivery force-null -> validateSchedule(proposed date/time: calendar, clinic hours, Manila past rule - the EXPIRED-proposal refusal) -> guard 1 (identical to current confirmed schedule refused) -> assertNoConflictingActiveAppointment (guard 2: another active same-patient+type row holding the exact proposed schedule refused; self excluded) -> then apply: confirmed_date/confirmed_time only (status and reviewed_* untouched), request -> approved with reviewed_by/reviewed_at/optional trimmed note, audit RESCHEDULE_REQUEST_APPROVED (confirmed->confirmed) carrying the OLD schedule (current confirmed, requested fallback), the NEW schedule and the staff note in the description. Every refusal throws BEFORE the first save - a failed approval writes nothing. declineRescheduleRequest(..., string note): same locks; type/reschedule + pending + online + confirmed rechecks (new 'decline_reschedule' match arm); NO cutoff and NO proposed-schedule validation (deliberate, documented in the docblock); required explanation via normalizeReason; request -> declined with provenance; appointment untouched; audit RESCHEDULE_REQUEST_DECLINED. assertStatus() gained the two new match arms; the class docblock gained a Step 2 contract paragraph (and Step 1's stale 'later steps' sentence was refreshed to point at Step 2).
- Controller (app/Http/Controllers/AppointmentChangeRequestController.php): the existing PATCH endpoints now TYPE-DISPATCH on the bound request's type: cancellation -> the original approveCancellationRequest/declineCancellationRequest + their existing post-commit patient notifications (behaviour byte-for-byte unchanged - regression-gated); reschedule -> approveRescheduleRequest/declineRescheduleRequest with NO notification (deliberately deferred, noted in-code as 'not omitted silently') and distinct success flashes; ANY OTHER type -> refused safely through the new shared private reviewError() helper (back() + errorRequestId + errorAppointmentId + errors('change_request') - the exact Phase 3D.2C refusal shape, never a 500). The FormRequests are reused verbatim (approve: optional 3-1000 review_note; decline: required 3-1000) and the routes are untouched - no route or FormRequest changes in this step. The class docblock documents the dispatch, the preserved cancellation notification contract and the reschedule notification deferral.
- Tests: new tests/Feature/Phase3D3B2StaffRescheduleReviewTest.php - 21 passed / 185 assertions with phase3d3b2* prefixed helpers (User/Patient/Link/Eligible/ConfirmedAppointment/DeliveryAppointment/RescheduleRequest via the REAL patient service path/FixtureRequest for rows the patient path cannot create/Patch/Freeze/NotificationCount/ExpectNoWrites refusal helper; afterEach clears Carbon::setTestNow). Covers: approval applies the exact proposed schedule (confirmed_date/time moved, requested_* and original reviewer preserved, request stamped with reviewer provenance, audit, ZERO notifications); empty optional note; K4 (staff-posted proposed_*/confirmed_*/status fields ignored - the request row's proposal wins); audit description carrying old AND new schedules with actor/patient/module; K5 delivery (date-only real path AND stray proposed_time='15:00' on a forged row both land confirmed_time NULL, proposal row itself not rewritten); review-time duplicate active appointment created BETWEEN submission and refusal (no writes); expired proposed schedule (isolated: current schedule still future); current appointment cutoff passed (isolated: proposal still future); identical-to-current proposal refused; decline keeps the original schedule + stamps + audit + zero notifications; decline requires an explanation (empty and 2-char refused, no writes); the expired-proposal pair (approve refused at a freeze where both schedules are past, then decline succeeds at the SAME freeze); stale reviews (approve twice, decline-after-approve, approve-after-decline, approve after staff cancel auto-resolution, decline after staff complete auto-resolution); cancelled/completed appointment fixtures refused on both endpoints with the request left pending; walk-in source refused on both endpoints; unknown type ('transport') rejected safely on both endpoints with zero writes and zero audits of any kind; and a cancellation-dispatch regression smoke test (cancellation-type request through the SAME endpoint still cancels + sends the original approved notification). One DELIBERATE Phase3D2C test update: 'approve refuses a non-cancellation request type' used a RESCHEDULE fixture under the pre-Step-2 contract; it now targets an UNRECOGNIZED type ('transport') so its safety property (type refusal with zero writes) survives while reschedule dispatch is covered by the new file; the 3D.2C header sentence was refreshed accordingly.
- Verification (preflight confirmed before every run - bootstrap/cache/config.php absent, phpunit.xml forces SQLite :memory: with force='true', TestCase isolation guard active): focused Phase3D3B2 21/21 green; regression gates in fresh-process chunks: (1) Phase3B1+3B2A+3B2B+3D2B 121 passed / 742 assertions; (2) Phase3B3+3B4 62 passed / 431; (3) Phase3D2C 25 passed / 136 (after the one documented update); (4) Phase3D3B Step 1 29 passed / 152; (5) NotificationTest+ClinicalAlertNotificationTest+ClinicRouteAuthorizationTest 41 passed / 217. Combined 299 passed / 0 failed. php -l clean on the changed service/controller (test file executed clean, no import warnings).
- Defense notes: every review re-derives ALL server-owned facts (type, pending status, online source, appointment status, current cutoff, proposed validity, both collision guards) INSIDE the transaction under the appointment-then-request lock order shared with request submission, cancellation review and auto-resolution - a stale double-click, a cancel/complete racing a review, a directly-forged request row (unknown type, walk-in, cancelled appointment, missing/identical/expired proposal) or a conflicting appointment created after submission can each only produce a DomainException session error with ZERO writes (proven per-case by the ExpectNoWrites-style assertions); K4 means the reviewer's POST body cannot influence the applied schedule - only the request row's proposal can (proven by the forged-input test); approval never overwrites the confirmer provenance and decline never touches the appointment row at all (single save of the request row); notifications are structurally impossible on this path in Step 2 (no service dispatch, no controller dispatch - exact-count tests pin it to zero). Honest limitations: (1) outcome notifications for approve/decline (and the Step 1 K3 clinic submission notice) remain deferred - pending a separate approval step; (2) no calendar/portal UI change in this step: the existing 3D.2C review UI posts to the same endpoints and now works for reschedule rows too (the details modal's Approve/Decline buttons reuse the same forms), but the modal copy still says 'cancellation request' in places - copy/indicator polish is the K6 UI step; (3) true multi-connection lock contention cannot be exercised in a single-process SQLite test run - the serialization argument rests on the shared, documented lock order (appointment before request) proven by structurally identical 3D.2C concurrency tests; (4) the pre-existing monolithic full-suite hang was not re-encountered - the focused gates complete in ~18s across fresh processes.
## DEPLA Phase 3D.3B Step 3: Reschedule Workflow Notifications (2026-10-12)

- Requirement: database notifications for the patient rescheduling workflow, dispatched only AFTER the owning transaction commits: (1) SUBMISSION -> every active Admin/Staff account; (2) APPROVAL -> the patient's linked active portal account with the newly confirmed schedule; (3) DECLINE -> the linked portal account saying the request was not approved and the original appointment remains confirmed. Privacy rules: no patient name/contact/reason/reviewer notes in any payload; the decline explanation never travels through the notification (portal card, later UI step); no generic duplicate status-changed notification on either review outcome; no notifications for failed validation, refused/stale reviews, repeat submissions or auto-resolution; no delivery to archived/unlinked accounts or to recipients outside their authorized surface (clinic calendar link for clinic rows, My Appointments link for patient rows). No Patient Portal or Calendar UI changes in this step (separate approval required).
- Notifications (3 new database-only classes, mirroring the Phase 3D.2B/3D.2C cancellation trio): app/Notifications/AppointmentRescheduleRequestedNotification (type APPOINTMENT_RESCHEDULE_REQUESTED; required message 'A patient has requested to reschedule an appointment.'; action 'View day' with the day-view deep link appointments.index?view=day&date={current confirmed schedule}&status=all - privacy-minimized payload, no identifiers); AppointmentRescheduleApprovedNotification (APPOINTMENT_RESCHEDULE_APPROVED; message names the NEWLY confirmed schedule from the appointment row post-approval - date plus time when present, date-only for delivery per K5; action 'View My Appointments' -> patient.appointments.index; no staff note in payload); AppointmentRescheduleDeclinedNotification (APPOINTMENT_RESCHEDULE_DECLINED; message says not approved + type label + existing confirmed schedule remains confirmed; destination patient.appointments.index; NO explanation in payload). All via ['database'] only, each carrying its event_key.
- Service (app/Services/SystemNotificationService.php): three new methods reusing the existing sendOnceTo ledger dedupe and recipient resolvers - notifyAppointmentRescheduleRequested (type+pending guard; clinicRecipients = all active admin+staff, archived excluded by SoftDeletes; event key appointment-reschedule-requested:{requestId}); notifyAppointmentRescheduleApproved / notifyAppointmentRescheduleDeclined (type+status guards; single recipient via activePortalUserFor - patient->user_id -> role=patient, archived/mis-linked refuses; event keys appointment-reschedule-approved:{requestId} / appointment-reschedule-declined:{requestId}). Docblocks record the single-notification-per-outcome contract (no generic status-changed sibling) and why auto-resolution never reaches these methods.
- Dispatch points (all strictly AFTER the service transaction returns): PatientAppointmentController::rescheduleRequest now captures the returned change request and calls notifyAppointmentRescheduleRequested (the Step 1/2 'K3 deferred' note was updated to 'now implemented'); AppointmentChangeRequestController reschedule branches call notifyAppointmentRescheduleApproved/Declined on \->fresh() (fresh reloads the committed status/schedule), replacing the 'deliberately deferred' comments; the class docblock now documents all notification paths. DomainException catch paths return before dispatch, so validation failures, stale reviews and refusals structurally cannot notify. Cancellation branches byte-for-byte untouched (regression-proven).
- Test updates (documented, deliberate): Phase3D3BPatientRescheduleTest - the 'zero notifications - K3 deferred' test was REWRITTEN as 'dispatches the K3 clinic submission notification to active staff after commit and never notifies the patient' (exact 1-row count for the single-staff fixture, type/event_key/message/title match, reason absent from payload, read_at null, submitting patient has 0 rows) and the atomicity test now expects 1 notification; header refreshed; count is 29 still. Phase3D3B2StaffRescheduleReviewTest - the three zero-notification assertions on SUCCESSFUL approve/decline/double-approve now expect exactly 1 row of the correct reschedule outcome type (the refused-approval test still asserts 0), header refreshed; still 21 tests.
- Tests: new tests/Feature/Phase3D3B3ReschedulingNotificationsTest.php - 16 passed / 158 assertions with phase3d3b3* prefixed helpers (World = admin+staff+portal+patient with appointment reviewer pinned to the staff so recipient counts are deterministic; ReschedulePost/CancelPost/Patch/ServiceRequest via the real service path/FixtureRequest/Rows/Payload/TotalRows/Freeze; afterEach clears Carbon::setTestNow). Covers: submission notifies exactly admin+staff with the required message, event key, day-view link params (view/day/2026-11-07/all), unread, patient not notified; archived staff and unrelated patient logins excluded; payload privacy (name/surname/email/contact/reason absent); zero notifications on validation failure AND service refusal with zero rows; repeat submission while pending adds no second notification; event-key dedupe on repeated service dispatch (second call false, row count unchanged); approval = exactly ONE row to the owning portal user with 'November 20, 2026 at 10:00' in the message, My Appointments destination, no reviewer note/name/email in payload, zero clinic recipients, zero generic status rows (total==1); delivery approval announces date-only (no ' at '); unlinked patient AND archived portal account both produce zero notifications while the review still succeeds; review-time duplicate refusal produces zero reschedule rows; stale double approve keeps exactly one row; repeated approval dispatch deduped by event key; cross-recipient isolation (other patient/admin/staff all 0); decline = one row to the owner, 'was not approved' + 'November 7, 2026' + 'remains confirmed', explanation absent from payload; auto-resolution (staff complete) sends no reschedule outcome rows while the COMPLETED status notification still fires; cancellation regression (submission still sends the 2 clinic cancellation-requested rows, review still sends the 1 patient cancellation-approved row, ZERO reschedule-typed rows, exact totals 2 then 3).
- Verification (preflight confirmed before every run - bootstrap/cache/config.php absent, phpunit.xml forces APP_ENV=testing/DB_CONNECTION=sqlite/DB_DATABASE=:memory: with force='true', TestCase isolation guard active): focused run Phase3D3B3+Step1+Step2 in one fresh process = 66 passed / 509 assertions; regression gates in separate fresh processes: (1) Phase3B1+3B2A+3B2B+3D2B 121 passed / 742 assertions; (2) Phase3B3+3B4 62 passed / 431; (3) Phase3D2C 25 passed / 136; (4) NotificationTest+ClinicalAlertNotificationTest+ClinicRouteAuthorizationTest 41 passed / 217. Combined 315 passed / 0 failed (299 prior baseline + 16 new). BOM check clean on all 9 changed/new PHP files; no lint script exists in composer.json (no pint/php-cs-fixer); no migration executed; no .env/config/package/git changes.
- Defense notes: notifications live entirely OUTSIDE the transactions - the service never touches SystemNotificationService, controllers dispatch only after a successful return, so a notification can never be rolled back by a later failure nor cause a commit (a dispatch failure would surface after the committed write, never inside it); the event_key ledger makes even a repeated post-commit dispatch a no-op at the DB layer, independent of the state guards (type+status checks refuse wrong-state rows before they are even keyed); recipients are re-resolved fresh at dispatch (active clinic users, active linked portal user), so an account archived between transaction and dispatch receives nothing; payload construction reads only type label + schedule + fixed text - there is no code path that could interpolate a reason, note or patient identifier into a reschedule payload (proven by the substring tests). Honest limitations: (1) no Patient Portal review-outcome card/banner yet - a declined request's explanation is still invisible to the patient outside the (not yet built) card, per the approved step scope; (2) no calendar indicators/copy polish (K6) - the details modal still shows cancellation-oriented copy for reschedule rows; (3) unread/read behavior is asserted at creation (read_at null) but tray read flows are existing NotificationTest coverage, unchanged; (4) the pre-existing monolithic full-suite hang was not re-encountered - gates complete in ~19s total across fresh processes.
## DEPLA Phase 3D.3B Step 4: Patient Portal Rescheduling UI (2026-10-12)

- Requirement: present the already-implemented and already-tested reschedule backend (Step 1 submission, Step 2 staff review, Step 3 notifications) on the Patient Portal My Appointments card: a Request Reschedule action on eligible cards (own + online + confirmed + before the scheduled-start cutoff + no other pending change request), while KEEPING Request Cancellation; the form posts proposed_date (required), proposed_time (required for checkup/counseling, NOT rendered for delivery requests per K5) and an optional 3-1000 reason to patient.appointments.rescheduleRequest with CSRF and a mandatory confirmation note ('Your current appointment will remain confirmed until the clinic approves your reschedule request.'); clinic hours enforced client-side (min 08:00, max 16:59) matching the server rule; NO patient_id/source/status/ownership fields may be posted (identity comes from the own-patient route resolution). Pending reschedule requests show a Reschedule Request Pending badge with the proposed schedule and optional reason while the status stays Confirmed and BOTH change forms are hidden; cancellation pending wording stays byte-identical; approved requests show a success banner with the NEW confirmed schedule; declined requests show the clinic's explanation with the original confirmed schedule kept; no staff-only review identifiers anywhere.
- Backend changes (minimal): app/Models/Appointment.php gained latestRescheduleRequest() (HasOne, type=reschedule, latest('id') - mirrors latestCancellationRequest, so outcome banners need no N+1); PatientAppointmentController::index eager-loads it alongside the two existing change-request relations on the upcoming and history queries only (the pending section never has change requests and stays untouched); rescheduleRequest()'s DomainException catch now also flashes errorAppointmentId so the refusal renders on the TARGETED card only instead of every card on the shared page. No service, FormRequest, route or schema change.
- UI (resources/views/patient/appointments/partials/card.blade.php, pure Blade - no JavaScript added, Alpine not required): the action chain restructured as pending-badge -> direct cancel -> (Request Cancellation disclosure + Request Reschedule disclosure) so one eligible confirmed card can offer both forms (the one-pending service rule guarantees only one can be submitted); the pending badge now branches on change type (cancellation wording preserved verbatim including 'Cancellation request pending - awaiting clinic review.', reschedule wording adds the proposed date/time and 'Your reason:'); two new outcome banners render ONLY when no request is pending and the card is still Confirmed (approved = new confirmed schedule, declined = review_note explanation + original schedule kept); the reschedule disclosure carries a hidden display-state marker (form=reschedule) used ONLY to gate rendering - old('form')==='reschedule' reopens the disclosure, keeps old proposed_date/proposed_time/reason on the reschedule form and forces BOTH cancellation textareas' old('reason') and @error('reason') blocks off (the two forms share the field name, so this prevents cross-form reason leakage); the scoped DomainException alert renders when ->has('appointment') and session('errorAppointmentId') matches this card's id.
- Tests: new tests/Feature/Phase3D3B4PatientRescheduleUiTest.php - 14 passed / 130 assertions with phase3d3b4* prefixed helpers (ReschedulePost posts the real form payload including the form=reschedule marker from the index referer; FormSegment helpers regex-extract a SINGLE card's rendered form so per-card assertions are possible; afterEach clears Carbon::setTestNow). Covers: eligibility matrix (eligible online confirmed renders exactly one Request Reschedule and its own route URL; walk-in/cutoff-passed/pending cards render none; terminal statuses render none; a pending cancellation request hides BOTH forms); delivery card form omits name="proposed_time" while counseling keeps it required; required note text, CSRF token, min/max clinic-hours attributes, and NO patient_id/status/source/appointment_type/confirmed_date/reviewed_by in the posted form; both change forms coexisting (one of each); pending reschedule badge shows proposed schedule + reason, status stays Confirmed (no 'Awaiting clinic confirmation'), no forms, no cancellation wording; cancellation pending wording intact with 'Your cancellation reason:' and no reschedule badge; approved banner shows 'Your reschedule request was approved.' + 'November 20, 2026' + '10:30' after the REAL staff approve endpoint; declined banner shows the explanation + original 'November 7, 2026 at 14:00' confirmed schedule after the REAL staff decline endpoint (appointment untouched, asserted in DB too); foreign patient name/schedule/route URL never rendered; section headings + history pagination ('of 11 history entries', page=2) unchanged; validation failure reopens the reschedule disclosure via the open attribute regex, preserves old input on the right form, renders the field error id, keeps the long reschedule reason out of the cancellation form (substr_count 1 page-wide) and the short-reason error rendered exactly once; double submit -> DomainException message rendered exactly once on the targeted card while the unrelated eligible card still offers its form.
- Verification (preflight confirmed before every run - bootstrap/cache/config.php absent, phpunit.xml forces APP_ENV=testing/DB_CONNECTION=sqlite/DB_DATABASE=:memory: with force='true', TestCase isolation guard active): focused Phase3D3B4 14 passed / 130 assertions; regression gates in separate fresh processes: (1) Phase3B1+3B2A+3B2B+3D2B 121 passed / 742 assertions; (2) Phase3B3+3B4 62 passed / 431; (3) Phase3D2C 25 passed / 136; (4) NotificationTest+ClinicalAlertNotificationTest+ClinicRouteAuthorizationTest 41 passed / 217; (5) Phase3D3B Step1 29 passed / 164; (6) Step2+Step3+Step4 together 51 passed / 475. Combined 329 passed / 0 failed (315 prior baseline + 14 new). php -l clean on the changed controller/model/test; php artisan view:cache compiled every Blade template cleanly (cleared after); BOM absent on the new test file; no lint script exists in composer.json; no migration executed; no .env/config/package/git changes; MySQL/Railway untouched; public/hot untouched.
- Defense notes: the display layer re-derives eligibility from the SAME public cutoff helper (AppointmentManagementService::scheduledStartPassedIssue) and relation state the service enforces under lock, so the form is only an affordance - a forged or stale post is still refused server-side (Step 1 tests); the only hidden field added is a display-state marker the backend never reads (validated keys are proposed_date/proposed_time/reason only), proven by the no-ownership-fields form-segment assertions; cross-form safety is explicit rather than incidental because both forms post 'reason' on one shared page (gated old input, gated @error, per-form textarea content asserted page-wide via substr_count); the DomainException flash id mirrors the calendar's existing errorAppointmentId pattern and keeps a multi-card page from showing a refusal on unrelated cards (asserted: message appears exactly once while a second eligible card keeps its form); outcome banners are guarded by no-pending + still-Confirmed so a stale banner can never contradict a newer pending request; relations are eager-loaded on the two sections that render them (no N+1), and the pending section deliberately skips them because pending appointments cannot have change requests. Honest limitations: (1) old input and the reopened disclosure are session-global, not card-scoped - a failed post repaints old values onto OTHER cards' reschedule forms too (the reason/cancellation leakage is blocked and asserted, but identical values appear on every reschedule form until the next full page load) - card-scoped old input would need a per-form namespace agreed with the client first; (2) no browser click-through was performed (no browser automation package may be installed) - open-attribute, constraint attributes and error anchoring are asserted at the markup level; (3) the calendar/K6 copy polish for reschedule rows is still a separate, unapproved step; (4) the pre-existing monolithic full-suite hang was not re-encountered - gates complete in ~20s total across fresh processes.

- Backend changes (minimal): app/Http/Controllers/AppointmentCalendarController.php - the ONE batched pending-change-request read per view now filters only on status (both types, still zero N+1) and maps appointment_id to type; eventPayload() adds hasPendingChangeRequest + pendingChangeRequestType while keeping hasPendingCancellationRequest byte-compatible (cancellation only) for Phase 3D.2C consumers; detailsMap() always emits BOTH cancellationRequest and rescheduleRequest keys (exactly one non-null - the one-pending-per-appointment invariant guarantees mutual exclusion), the reschedule block carrying currentDateLabel/currentTimeLabel/proposedDateLabel/proposedTimeLabel (null time labels for date-only delivery per K5 - the payload never invents a clock time), nullable reason, requestedAtLabel and the server-built approve/decline URLs; yearPayload() adds ONE guarded batched read (skipped when the year has no rows) feeding a per-month pendingChangeRequestCount. app/Http/Controllers/AppointmentChangeRequestController.php - reviewError() now also calls ->withInput() so a DomainException refusal keeps the staff member's typed review note (vendor confirmed: no-argument withInput() flashes the current request input). No route, service, FormRequest, model or schema change.

Combined 344 passed / 0 failed (329 prior baseline + 15 new). php -l clean on both changed controllers and the new test; php artisan view:cache compiled every Blade template cleanly (cleared after); BOM absent on the new test file; no lint script exists in composer.json; no migration executed; no .env/config/package/git changes; MySQL/Railway untouched; public/hot untouched.
- Defense notes: request-status visibility is strictly additive - chip/badge styling stays driven by the appointment row itself (asserted: payload status remains confirmed while pending, so a pending review can never repaint a status the patient still sees as confirmed); review URLs are server-built from the request id and the forms carry CSRF - the client never constructs an id, a status or a source, and K4/K5 remain backend-enforced; mutual exclusion of the two review panels is structural (exactly one non-null key by the one-pending invariant) rather than a UI guess, and the panels render from the same payload the forms post to; the reopen path keys on errorAppointmentId + the change_request error only - validation failures without an appointment id show the top flash and preserve input without a premature open (asserted both ways); withInput() on DomainException paths exists solely to keep the typed explanation and bypasses no validation; the batched reads are guarded by empty-collection checks and asserted at exactly two queries for a five-request day, so future per-row regressions fail loudly.  Honest limitations: (1) FormRequest validation failures do not flash errorAppointmentId, so a too-short review note shows the message in the top flash rather than reopening the modal (old input is preserved either way); (2) old('review_note') is session-global like the Step 4 portal pattern - a failed post repaints the note into all four textareas until the next page load; (3) no browser click-through was performed (no browser automation package may be installed) - Alpine behavior is asserted at markup/init level; (4) the pre-existing monolithic full-suite hang was not re-encountered - gates complete in ~20s total across fresh processes.

## DEPLA Phase 3D.4B: Pre-migration hardening - portal write throttling, cancellation error scoping, mass-assignment contract (2026-10-13)

- Requirement: close the three Medium audit findings recorded before any schema work, with NO migration, NO database access, NO package install and NO git operation. P1 - the four Patient Portal appointment write routes (store, cancel, cancellationRequest, rescheduleRequest) had no throttling. P2 - the shared My Appointments page renders many cards, and both cancellation forms post a field named `reason`, so a cancellation refusal repainted its error and old reason onto every card (and the controller's bare `withInput()` parked the whole request body in the session). P3 - `Appointment` and `AppointmentChangeRequest` carried schema-wide `$fillable` arrays, so a future `Model::create($request->all())` could re-parent an appointment, flip its status or pre-approve a change request.
- P1 - throttle middleware + named rate limiter. routes/web.php: the four patient POST routes each gained `throttle:patient-appointment-writes` appended to their existing `['auth','patient-portal-account','must-change-password','no-store']` chain, with a route-block comment documenting the policy. app/Providers/AppServiceProvider.php: `RateLimiter::for('patient-appointment-writes', ...)` registered from `boot()` via a new private `registerPatientAppointmentWriteRateLimiter()` - `Limit::perMinute(10)->by('patient-appointment-write:user:'.$user->getAuthIdentifier())`, with an IP-keyed fallback only when `$request->user()` is null. One SHARED bucket across all four endpoints bounds a patient's total write rate; the GET list/create pages, the Admin/Staff calendar and management routes and the notification read actions are deliberately outside the limiter. Keyed per authenticated user, never per IP, so two patients behind one NAT address cannot exhaust each other's budget. Verified framework behavior before coding: `throttle` is in the default middleware aliases (`Configuration\Middleware::defaultAliases`), and `middlewarePriority` plus `SortedMiddleware::priorityMapIndex` (which strips parameters for the lookup) place StartSession and `auth` before `throttle`, so the limiter always resolves the signed-in user from the session and a guest is refused by `auth` before any attempt is counted. `CACHE_STORE=array` in phpunit.xml resets the counters per test.
- P2 - card-scoped cancellation failures + old-input whitelist. app/Http/Controllers/PatientAppointmentController.php: `cancel()` and `cancellationRequest()` DomainException catches now return `back()->withInput($request->only(['reason']))->with('errorAppointmentId', $appointment->id)->with('errorForm', 'cancel'|'cancellation-request')->withErrors(['reason' => ...])`; `rescheduleRequest()`'s catch kept its `errorAppointmentId` + `appointment` key and its full `withInput()` was narrowed to the four fields the reschedule form actually posts (`proposed_date`, `proposed_time`, `reason`, `form`) so a forged POST cannot park a password in the session. app/Http/Requests/PatientAppointmentCancelRequest.php: `failedValidation()` is REPLACED (not wrapped) by a `redirect()->to($this->getRedirectUrl())->withInput(array_intersect_key($this->all(), ['reason' => true]))->withErrors($validator->errors(), $this->errorBag)` throw wrapped in `HttpResponseException`, plus a `flashCancellationFailureScope()` helper that flashes `errorAppointmentId` (from the route parameter, never from input) and `errorForm` derived from the route name; the replacement is required because the framework's `Handler::invalid()` calls `withInput(Arr::except($request->input(), $this->dontFlash))` on the WHOLE original request (vendor-verified: `FormRequestServiceProvider` does not rebind the container 'request' to the FormRequest), which would still park a forged `patient_id`/`status` in the session. resources/views/patient/appointments/partials/card.blade.php: new `@php` scope block computes `$hasCancellationCardError` (reason error AND `errorAppointmentId` equals this card AND `errorForm` in {cancel, cancellation-request}), the two form-specific flags `$directCancelReturn` / `$cancelRequestReturn`, and `$hasCancellationCardFallbackError` (the targeted form no longer renders - status moved on, cutoff passed, request already pending, or the patient posted the other endpoint's route); the direct-cancel and cancellation-request textareas now render `{{ $directCancelReturn ? old('reason') : '' }}` / `{{ $cancelRequestReturn ? old('reason') : '' }}` with their `@error('reason')` blocks gated on the same flags, and a new card-level alert (mirroring the existing scoped reschedule alert) prints the message once when the form it belongs to is not on screen, so a refusal is never silent. `$rescheduleReturn` and the reschedule form are untouched.
- P3 - mass-assignment contract. app/Models/Appointment.php `$fillable` narrowed to `['appointment_type','requested_date','requested_time']`; app/Models/AppointmentChangeRequest.php narrowed to `['proposed_date','proposed_time','reason']` (`review_note` deliberately excluded - staff-authored review output). Excluded from Appointment: `patient_id`, `source`, `status`, `confirmed_date`, `confirmed_time`, `reviewed_by`, `reviewed_at`, `decline_reason`, `cancellation_reason`; excluded from AppointmentChangeRequest: `appointment_id`, `type`, `status`, `reviewed_by`, `reviewed_at`, `review_note`. Repository-wide scan confirmed NO production code mass-assigns either model (the only `new Appointment()`/`new AppointmentChangeRequest()` sites are in AppointmentManagementService and assign column by column), no factory/seeders create appointments, no `Model::unguard()` and no `preventSilentlyDiscardingAttributes` anywhere - so behaviour is unchanged and the contract only protects future code. Test fixtures that seeded server-owned columns therefore switched from `::create(` to `::forceCreate(` (17 Appointment sites + 3 AppointmentChangeRequest sites across Phase3B1/3B2A/3B2B/3B3/3B4/3D2B/3D2C/3D3B/3D3B2/3D3B3/3D3B4/3D3B5), and the two instance-level `->update([...])` calls that touched non-fillable columns became `forceFill([...])->save()` (Phase3B3:624 status+decline_reason; Phase3B2A:166 status). `AppointmentChangeRequest::query()->update([...])` at Phase3D2B:442 is a query-builder update (bypasses fillable by design) and was left alone. Phase3B1's FK-refusal test now uses `forceCreate` so it still tests the foreign key rather than the NOT NULL column.
- Tests: new tests/Feature/Phase3D4BPreMigrationHardeningTest.php - 17 passed / 151 assertions with phase3d4b* prefixed helpers (Portal/Pending/Confirmed fixtures, CancelPost/RequestPost/ReschedulePost posting exactly what the forms post from the index referer, a textarea-value regex extractor, and SpendBudget burning the limiter with validation failures so no rows are created). P1: 429 after 10 attempts with the budget proven SHARED across all four endpoints; a second signed-in patient on the same IP still gets through; GET index/create stay 200 after the budget is spent; `patient.notifications.index` and the Admin/Staff `appointments.index` stay 200; an unauthenticated POST redirects to login and is never 429. P2: direct-cancel refusal painted exactly once with the typed reason only on the matching `cancel-reason-{id}` textarea (the sibling pending card's textarea empty); a direct-cancel refusal never lands on the sibling card's `cancel-request-reason-{id}`; a cancellation-request refusal scoped to its own form; a service DomainException on a confirmed card rendered once through the card-level fallback with the pending sibling's textarea empty; a reschedule reason failure sets NO `errorForm`, renders exactly once and leaves the cancellation textarea empty; old input whitelisted to `reason` on both the validation and DomainException paths of both cancellation endpoints, with `password`/`password_confirmation`/`patient_id`/`status`/`confirmed_date`/`reviewed_by` proven absent from `_old_input`. P3: `fill()` of every server-owned Appointment column leaves the model clean and the saved row unchanged; `fill()` of every ownership/review AppointmentChangeRequest column likewise; content and proposal fields still mass-assign.
- Verification (preflight confirmed before every run - bootstrap/cache/config.php absent, phpunit.xml forces APP_ENV=testing/DB_CONNECTION=sqlite/DB_DATABASE=:memory: with force='true', TestCase isolation guard active): baseline recorded BEFORE any edit in three fresh processes (96 + 87 + 120 = 303 passed, 0 failed); after the change the focused new file 17/17 and the same regression chunks re-run green - (1) Phase3B1+3B2A+3B2B 96 passed / 574 assertions; (2) Phase3B3+3B4+3D2B 87 passed / 599; (3) Phase3D2C+3D3B+3D3B2 75 passed / 487; (4) Phase3D3B3+3D3B4+3D3B5 45 passed / 419. Combined 320 passed / 0 failed (303 prior + 17 new) - zero regressions and no test tripped the new limiter. `php -l` clean on all 8 changed/new PHP files; Blade compiles (every card is rendered by the passing HTTP tests). No lint script exists in composer.json (no pint/php-cs-fixer); no migration executed; no `.env`/config/`public/hot`/package/git changes; MySQL/Railway untouched.
- Defense notes: P1 uses the named-limiter form rather than `throttle:10,1` precisely because the numeric shorthand keys on the request IP and the per-user policy is the whole point; the limiter is registered in the existing AppServiceProvider (no new provider, no new cache driver, no new table), and because `auth` outranks `throttle` in the framework priority list a guest can neither consume a shared bucket nor leak a per-user one.
