P2's scope keys are derived from the ROUTE (parameter + name), which the server fully controls, so a client cannot aim a refusal at another patient's card or forge the marker; the two cancellation markers are disjoint from the reschedule family (`errorForm` is never set on the reschedule path and `$rescheduleReturn` is never set on a cancellation post), asserted both ways; the whitelist is applied on BOTH failure paths - our own `failedValidation` replacement for validation, and `$request->only(['reason'])` for DomainException - so no code path can park a password or a foreign field in the session. P3's contract cannot break existing behaviour because no production code mass-assigns these models; because `$fillable` is non-empty the models stay "partially guarded", so a forged key is silently dropped rather than throwing (the safe failure mode), and the two exclusion lists are exactly the columns the service assigns under `lockForUpdate`. Honest limitations: (1) the limiter's 10/min window is a conservative operational choice, not a clinical threshold - raising it later is a one-line change in AppServiceProvider; (2) the reschedule and store FormRequests still use the framework's default `withInput()` on validation failure (out of the audited finding's scope), so a forged password posted to THOSE two endpoints is still stripped by the framework's `dontFlash` while other forged keys would be parked - the same `failedValidation` replacement can be extended on request; (3) `old('reason')`/`old('proposed_*')` remain session-global, so identical values repaint onto every eligible form of that type until the next full page load (card-scoped old input would need a per-form namespace agreed with the client first); (4) no browser click-through was performed (no browser automation package may be installed) - rate-limit headers, alert placement and textarea contents are asserted at the HTTP/markup level; (5) the pre-existing monolithic full-suite hang (documented in Phase 3D.2A) was not re-encountered because the focused gates complete in ~21s across fresh processes. Next step when approved: apply the Phase 3D.4B hardening checklist to the pre-migration plan, then proceed to the migration sprint with these contracts in place.

## DEPLA Phase 3D.4C: Local MySQL migration - appointment_change_requests (2026-10-13)

- Requirement: execute the single pending local migration that Phase 3D.2/3D.3 depends on against the LOCAL database only, behind a read-only preflight (Step 1), a verified backup and an explicitly approved six-statement dry-run gate. Hard rules for this step: local MySQL only (NEVER Railway), no rollback/refresh/fresh/reset, no destructive SQL, no .env/config/package/git changes, no unrelated code changes, no tests against MySQL, no browser QA, and STOP on any command failure instead of retrying, repairing or restoring.
- Preflight (Phase 3D.4C Step 1, read-only): resolved target from .env - APP_ENV=local, DB_CONNECTION=mysql, DB_HOST=127.0.0.1, DB_PORT=3306, DB_DATABASE=maternity_system1, zero railway references - and confirmed it against the live connection: SELECT DATABASE()=maternity_system1, host DESKTOP-POMEHAK, 10.4.32-MariaDB, datadir C:\xampp\mysql\data\ (local XAMPP, not Railway), bootstrap/cache/config.php absent so the resolved values were live. Schema history: migrations held 47 rows across batches 1-5 against 48 migration files, leaving exactly ONE pending file - database/migrations/2026_10_12_000001_create_appointment_change_requests_table.php - with no orphaned history rows, confirmed identically by migrate:status. Existing schema cross-checked: 20 tables; patients.user_id, users.must_change_password, the user profile columns and appointments (16 columns, 7 rows, 7 index names, 2 FKs) all present and matching their migrations; appointment_change_requests absent. Correction to an older note in this file: patients.previous_cs and patients.miscarriage ARE covered by 2026_08_08_000001_add_obstetric_history_to_patients_table.php (Schema::hasColumn-guarded), so they are not migration drift.
- Backup (verified, required before approval): the newest pre-existing dump (Downloads\maternity_system1 (5).sql, 2026-10-09, 281,378 bytes) held only 19 CREATE TABLE statements and NO appointments table, i.e. it predated batch 5 and was unusable - this was the Step 1 NO-GO reason. A fresh backup was then taken under separate approval and verified read-only: C:\Users\admin\Downloads\maternity_system1_pre_3D4C_20261013_1252.sql, 289,664 bytes, mysqldump exit 0, header Database=maternity_system1 / server 10.4.32; 20 CREATE TABLE + 20 DROP TABLE IF EXISTS (restorable), 15 INSERT INTO covering all 20 tables, appointments present, appointment_change_requests absent (correctly predating the pending migration), no DROP DATABASE or TRUNCATE, and every embedded row count matching the live counts (20/20). A pre-migration row-count baseline was captured for the post-run comparison and is retained for future verifications.
- Dry-run gate: `php artisan migrate --pretend` emitted exactly six statements, all additive and all targeting appointment_change_requests only - (1) CREATE TABLE with 12 columns, (2) FK appointment_id -> appointments.id ON DELETE RESTRICT, (3) FK reviewed_by -> users.id ON DELETE SET NULL, (4) index (appointment_id), (5) index (status), (6) index (appointment_id, status). An automated gate asserted statement count == 6, each statement's expected prefix, that no DROP/TRUNCATE/INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE and no ALTER on appointments, patients, users, prenatal_visits, medical_histories, birth_plans, ultrasounds, referrals, babies or pregnancy_outcomes appeared, and that no out-of-scope table was referenced. Gate PASS was required before the single real run.
- Migration executed exactly once: `php artisan migrate` -> "2026_10_12_000001_create_appointment_change_requests_table ... 151.02ms DONE", exit 0; `php artisan migrate:status` -> 48 Ran / 0 Pending with the new history row in batch 6; total tables 20 -> 21.
- Post-migration verification (read-only information_schema + COUNT checks, every assertion PASS, exit 0): appointment_change_requests exists as InnoDB / utf8mb4_unicode_ci with exactly 12 columns (id, appointment_id, type, proposed_date, proposed_time, reason, status, reviewed_by, reviewed_at, review_note, created_at, updated_at), status default 'pending', id PK auto_increment, appointment_id/type/status NOT NULL; 2 foreign keys (appointment_id -> appointments.id ON DELETE RESTRICT, reviewed_by -> users.id ON DELETE SET NULL); 5 physical indexes - the 3 declared by the migration (appointment_id, status, (appointment_id, status)), PRIMARY, plus the InnoDB-created FK-support index appointment_change_requests_reviewed_by_foreign on reviewed_by (InnoDB always creates one when a FK lands on an unindexed column; appointment_id needs none because the explicit index already covers it); table empty at 0 rows, so no data was written.
- Existing data unchanged: appointments verified at 16 columns with names identical to its migration file, 7 rows, its 7 index names and both FKs (patient_id -> patients RESTRICT, reviewed_by -> users SET NULL) unchanged, AND a fresh read-only mysqldump of the table was byte-identical (1,223 chars) to the same INSERT line in the pre-migration backup - stronger than a row-count match, which a same-count UPDATE would defeat. All 20 original tables retain their baseline counts (users 15, patients 89, appointments 7, prenatal_visits 65, medical_histories 37, birth_plans 34, ultrasounds 36, referrals 7, pregnancy_outcomes 14, babies 21, audit_logs 344, notifications 38, sessions 6, password_reset_tokens 2, plus the five empty framework tables); migrations is the ONLY table that changed, 47 -> 48.
- Rollback policy - intentionally unsupported for populated appointment tables: 2026_10_10_000001_create_appointments_table.php::down() throws a RuntimeException when appointments holds rows, so `php artisan migrate:rollback` can never drop recorded appointment request history (the same guard exists on the pregnancy_outcomes migration). With 7 persisted appointments this is now live behaviour, not just intent: a rollback attempt is refused and the table is left intact, with no rows deleted or converted. That refusal is a deliberate data-safety feature of the schema, not a defect to be worked around - recovery from any bad state is by restoring the verified pre-migration dump, never by rolling back. The new 2026_10_12 migration's own down() drops only its empty, additive change-request table, and nothing currently needs it: cancellation/reschedule provenance is clinical-adjacent audit evidence, so its table is treated as append-only rather than disposable.
- Verification honesty: preconditions were re-checked immediately before the run (project path, config cache absent, .env keys only, no railway string, live connection identity, backup present with 20 CREATE TABLE), and no database command failed at any point. Two intermediate FAIL results were bugs in the CHECKER, not schema defects - MariaDB reports bigint(20) unsigned (display width) for every bigint including pre-existing columns, returns varchar defaults quoted ('pending'), auto-creates the fifth FK-support index, and appointments has always had 7 index names (the Step 1 report's "6" was a miscount); expectations were corrected against the authoritative migration files and the re-run passed 100%. The dry-run gate likewise first reported a false zero-statement count caused by my own PowerShell extraction regex, which was fixed and re-run to a genuine 6/6 PASS before migrating. Nothing was retried against, repaired or restored in the database.
- Safety compliance: local MySQL only (127.0.0.1:3306, XAMPP datadir), Railway never contacted; no rollback/refresh/fresh/reset and no destructive SQL executed; .env untouched (mtime unchanged), no config cache created, no package or git change, no tests run against MySQL, no browser QA started; the verified backup is retained at the path above.
- Defense notes: the dry run was trustworthy because the gate compared statements against a written expectation rather than a human glance, and because the migration file itself already declared its intent (additive CREATE TABLE, appointments untouched); the byte-identical mysqldump comparison plus table-by-table baseline counts make the "nothing else changed" claim independent of memory. The fifth index is documented explicitly so nobody mistakes it for drift - it is InnoDB's FK-support index and dropping it would degrade the reviewed_by lookup; likewise the absence of a UNIQUE(appointment_id) constraint is unchanged by this migration, because the one-pending-per-appointment rule remains enforced lock-first in AppointmentManagementService. Honest limitations: (1) no browser QA has been performed on the now-backed cancellation/reschedule feature; (2) the SQLite :memory: suite has not been re-run since the schema landed locally (tests never touch MySQL by policy); (3) MySQL 8 / Railway parity is still unverified - this run was MariaDB 10.4 only, so any Railway migration remains a separate, explicitly-approved future step.

## DEPLA Phase 3E.2: Patient Portal My Medical Records - read-only portal records page (2026-10-13)

- Requirement: ship a secure, READ-ONLY "My Medical Records" page for authenticated Patient Portal users, scoped to the pregnancy row currently linked through the login's `patientAccount` relationship, with NO historical pregnancy grouping, NO schema change, NO Admin/Staff workflow change, NO risk/ML/clinical-calculation change and NO write endpoint. Explicit sprint exclusions (out of scope by instruction): ultrasounds, birth plans, delivery records, baby information, postpartum, referrals, and every risk/assessment/ML field.
- Backend: new `app/Http/Controllers/PatientMedicalRecordsController.php` with a single `index()` (reflection-asserted: `index` is the ONLY public method declared by the class). One purely additive route in `routes/web.php` immediately after `patient.profile.show` - `GET /patient/medical-records`, name `patient.medical-records.index`, carrying the exact existing portal chain `['auth','patient-portal-account','must-change-password','no-store']`. No POST/PUT/PATCH/DELETE route for medical records exists anywhere: the URI answers 405 to all four verbs and the route list contains exactly one `*medical-records*` name (both asserted). Identity is resolved ONLY through the authenticated user's own `patientAccount()->select(...)` query - never from a URL segment, query string or request body - so `/patient/medical-records/{id}` is 404 and `?patient_id=` is ignored (both asserted). The controller 403s any role that is not `patient` (the portal middleware deliberately passes clinic roles through), and `resolveOwnPatient()` repeats the Phase 2D refusal wording for an unlinked or archived record.
- Data access: three explicit SQL-level column allow-lists - `OVERVIEW_COLUMNS` (id + status/lmp/edd/gravida/para/previous_cs/miscarriage), `VISIT_COLUMNS` (id, patient_id, visit_date, gestational_age, bp_sys, bp_dia, weight, temperature, fundic_height, fetal_heart_tone, fetal_movement, next_visit_date) and `HISTORY_COLUMNS` (id, patient_id + the 18 recorded conditions + other_specify). Prenatal visits are ordered `visit_date DESC, id DESC` (id is the stable tie-breaker for equal dates, asserted) and every query runs through the `patientAccount` relation, so only the linked row's children are read; medical history is read hasOne-style. Nothing outside those lists is selected, so the excluded fields cannot be serialized even by a future template edit.
- Presentation allow-list: `index()` copies every row into plain PHP arrays (overview map, per-visit map, grouped condition map with Yes/No values) and passes ONLY those arrays to the view - the Blade template receives no Eloquent model, so there is no attribute dump to leak. Date rendering uses the existing portal convention `M d, Y` (Carbon), weight uses `App\Support\WeightFormatter::formatKg`, gestational age renders as `N.N weeks`, blood pressure as `sys/dia mmHg`, temperature as `N.N °C`. Every nullable field renders the literal "Not recorded" and no value is ever manufactured: a NULL `previous_cs`/`miscarriage` does NOT become "No" (asserted - exactly 6 "Not recorded" strings for a fully empty overview).
- UI: new `resources/views/patient/medical-records/index.blade.php` extending `patient.layout` with the three requested sections (Pregnancy Overview definition list, Prenatal Visit History as responsive cards, Medical History as grouped Yes/No chip cards plus an "Other Recorded Condition" row), page title "My Medical Records", supporting line "Records for your currently linked pregnancy. This page is read-only.", mobile-first grid/card layout, honest empty states for visits and history, and zero edit/add/delete/staff actions - the only form on the page is the layout's POST logout (form count asserted to 1). `resources/views/patient/layout.blade.php` gains a "My Medical Records" nav link between My Appointments and Notifications with its active state bound to `patient.medical-records.*`, preserving the dashboard/appointments/notifications/profile/logout entries. `resources/views/patient/dashboard.blade.php` converts the Medical Records placeholder `<article>` into an `<a>` card pointing at the new route, removes the Coming Soon badge and the "available soon" copy, and rewrites the intro pill so it no longer says medical records are unavailable - the phrase "not available yet" is deliberately retained because five existing portal tests assert it verbatim.
- Exclusions enforced and tested: `risk_level`, `risk_reasons`, `ml_prediction`, `ml_valid`, `factor_evidence`, `assessment_metadata`, `decision_source`, `rule_reasons`, `missing_records`, `urgency`, `bp_assessment`, `assessment`, `recommendation`, `treatment_plan`, `notes`, repeat-BP/staff provenance, the hypertension/diabetes/anemia visit flags, `presenting_part`/`uterine_activity`/`cervical_dilation`/`bag_of_water`, `created_at`/`updated_at` and both id columns are never selected and never rendered. A seeded visit carrying all of them plus distinctive MARKER-* values renders none of them (value AND column-name assertions), while the approved observations still render.
- Tests: new `tests/Feature/Phase3E2PatientMedicalRecordsTest.php` - 19 passed / 176 assertions with `phase3e2*` prefixed helpers, covering all 15 required cases plus two extras: guest redirect; own pregnancy overview (all labels + `M d, Y` dates); own prenatal visit history (every approved field); own medical history (groups + Yes/No chips + other_specify); another patient AND a name/birthdate-matched prior pregnancy row never appear; `?patient_id=` ignored and `/patient/medical-records/{id}` 404; unlinked 403; archived 403; admin 403; staff 403; forced-password-change redirect; fully empty states; newest-first ordering with the same-date id tie-break; risk/ML/notes/internal-field exclusion; exactly one GET+HEAD route with `index()` the only public method plus 405 on POST/PUT/PATCH/DELETE; dashboard link works and leads to the page; dashboard/profile/appointments/notifications all still 200 with every portal href present; no edit/add/delete markup and exactly one form; clinic route authorization unchanged (guest redirect, patient 403, staff 200). The existing `Phase3APatientPortalDashboardTest` placeholder case was updated IN PLACE to assert the working link and the removal of the coming-soon copy.
- Verification (isolation re-confirmed before every run: `bootstrap/cache/config.php` absent, `phpunit.xml` forces APP_ENV/DB_CONNECTION=sqlite/DB_DATABASE=:memory: with `force="true"`, `tests/TestCase.php` `ensureDatabaseIsolation` guard active): focused Phase 3E.2 file 19/19; regression chunk (1) Phase3A + ClinicRouteAuthorization + LoginRedirectSafety + Phase2C + Phase3B3 = 123 passed / 720 assertions, 0 failed; regression chunk (2) PatientProfile* + MedicalHistoryScope + Phase3B4 + Phase2A + Phase2D = 103 passed / 586 assertions with 9 failures analysed below; re-run of Phase3E2 + Phase3A after restoring routes/web.php = 49 passed / 336 assertions. `php -l` clean on the new controller, `routes/web.php` and both test files; no lint script exists in composer.json; no migration executed; no `.env`/config/package/git change; `public/hot` untouched; MySQL and Railway untouched.
- Pre-existing failures - NOT caused by this sprint, verified, and deliberately left unfixed: `PatientProfileRiskPanelTest` (8) and `MedicalHistoryScopeTest` (1) fail against `patients.show`. Evidence: `git status` shows `PatientController.php`, `resources/views/patients/show.blade.php`, `resources/views/medical_histories/*` and both failing test files are all UNMODIFIED versus HEAD; both suites were re-run after TEMPORARILY REMOVING the new route from `routes/web.php` and produced the identical 9 failures; and `patients/show.blade.php` contains zero occurrences of the strings those tests assert both at HEAD (`git show HEAD:...` count 0) and in the working tree. They are reported, not fixed, because changing the Admin/Staff clinical view is outside this sprint's scope.
- Known limitations: (1) CURRENT-PREGNANCY-ONLY BY DESIGN - `patients.user_id` is UNIQUE and `startNewPregnancy()` was not touched, so if the link still points at an older DELIVERED row that is exactly what the page shows; no name/birthdate matching is performed and the suite asserts a name-matched prior pregnancy stays invisible. (2) Ultrasounds, birth plans, delivery, babies, postpartum and referrals are not shown - each is a deliberate 3E.3 scope item, and postpartum has no data entity anywhere in the schema. (3) No risk level, assessment or recommendation text is shown, pending explicit clinic approval (H3). (4) `medical_histories` is one-per-patient by application rule only (no DB unique) - hasOne semantics render one row if a duplicate ever exists. (5) No audit row is written for a read, consistent with the rest of the portal. (6) No browser click-through was performed (no browser automation package may be installed) - layout, empty states and scoping are asserted at HTTP/markup level. (7) The pre-existing monolithic full-suite hang documented in Phase 3D.2A was avoided by running focused gates in fresh processes (about 23s total).
## DEPLA Phase 3E.3: Patient Portal My Medical Records expansion - ultrasound, birth plan, delivery and baby sections (2026-10-14)

- Requirement: extend the existing Phase 3E.2 read-only Patient Portal "My Medical Records" page with four new sections - Ultrasound Records, Birth Plan, Delivery Records and Baby Information - each scoped to the pregnancy row currently linked through the login's `patientAccount` relationship. Still ONE GET route, still NO writes, still no new file-serving endpoint, and NO cross-pregnancy history. Explicitly out of scope and untouched: postpartum, referrals, risk/ML/assessment data, `startNewPregnancy()`, `patients.user_id`, Admin/Staff clinical workflows and every database table.
- Backend: `app/Http/Controllers/PatientMedicalRecordsController.php` was EXTENDED in place (no second controller, `index()` is still the only public method, reflection-asserted). Four new SQL-level column allow-lists were added - `ULTRASOUND_COLUMNS` (10), `BIRTH_PLAN_COLUMNS` (17), `OUTCOME_COLUMNS` (4) and `BABY_COLUMNS` (10) - and four new presentation transformers (`presentUltrasound`, `presentBirthPlan`, `presentDelivery`, `presentBaby`) copy every row into plain arrays before the view is built, so the Blade still receives no Eloquent model. `OVERVIEW_COLUMNS` gained exactly one column, `patients.delivery_date` (migration 2026_03_31_174932), purely as the source for the Delivery Records section; the Pregnancy Overview presentation map and its seven rendered fields are byte-for-byte unchanged. Eager loads were added for `ultrasounds`, `birthPlan`, `pregnancyOutcome` and `babies`; `routes/web.php` changed ONLY in a comment block (22 -> 25 diff lines, no route, no middleware).
- Ultrasound Records: every scan of the linked pregnancy, ordered `scan_date DESC, id DESC` (id is the stable same-day tie-breaker, asserted). Rendered fields: Scan Date, Gestational Age from Scan (integer weeks shown to one decimal, matching the rest of this page), Estimated Fetal Weight (the migration documents GRAMS, so the value is rendered with an explicit trailing "g" and trailing zeros trimmed - 3200 g, 3150.5 g - so it can never be misread as kilograms), Fetal Heartbeat, Fetal Movement, Presentation, Amniotic Fluid and Placenta Position (the exact field labels used by the Admin/Staff ultrasound details panel). Deliberately ABSENT at the SQL level: `remarks` (staff free text), `report_file` and `report_image` (storage paths and raw filenames).
- Birth Plan: the single `hasOne` plan of the linked pregnancy, rendered as 15 patient-friendly label/value rows taken from the clinic's own birth-plan form and print view - Planned Prenatal Visits, Plan to Deliver in This Clinic, Planned Delivery Location, Transportation Arrangement, Transportation Will Cost Money, Planned Payment Method, Started Saving for Delivery, Birth Companion, Caregiver at Home, Plan to Have More Children, Number of More Children Planned, Knows a Family Planning Method, Used Family Planning Before, Family Planning Method and Family Planning Method Source. Boolean answers render as the same Yes/No chips used by Medical History. A NULL answer is omitted as a row and is NEVER converted into a "No" (asserted), and a row whose answers are all unrecorded falls back to an honest note. Deliberately ABSENT: `notes` (unapproved free-text remarks / internal staff notes).
- Delivery Records: patient-facing facts only - Pregnancy Outcome (human-readable label; `DELIVERED` maps to "Delivered" via the single-value `PregnancyOutcomeVocabulary` contract, and any other persisted value is passed through unchanged rather than silently rewritten), Delivery Date (`patients.delivery_date`, the single canonical date per the Sprint 17B migration) and Delivery Location (`PregnancyOutcomeVocabulary::deliveryLocationLabel()` - This Clinic / Another Facility / Home / Other). A record is presented ONLY when at least one of those three facts was actually recorded; a `pregnancy_outcomes` row whose fact columns are all NULL is documented as "no observed evidence yet" and therefore renders the neutral empty state, so a delivery is never implied. Nothing is inferred from the EDD, from a gap in prenatal visits or from `patients.status` (asserted). Deliberately ABSENT: `confirmation_source`, `confirmed_at`, `confirmed_by`, `follow_up_status`, `follow_up_recorded_at`, `follow_up_recorded_by` and outcome `notes`.
- Baby Information: every baby of the linked pregnancy (twins supported), ordered by `id` (birth order), labelled "Baby 1", "Baby 2", ... Rendered fields follow the clinic's existing baby views exactly: Full Name (first + middle + last composed in the controller), Sex, Date of Birth (`M d, Y`), Time of Birth (`h:i A`, e.g. 08:30 AM), Birth Weight (`WeightFormatter::formatKg` + " kg", decimal 5,2 kilograms) and Birth Length (decimal 5,1 centimetres + " cm"). Deliberately ABSENT: newborn metadata, staff fields and every other `babies` column.
- Presentation helpers added and reused across the new sections: `presentText()` (trimmed non-empty string or null), `presentYesNo()` (NULL stays unrecorded, never a manufactured "No"), `presentCount()` (integer counts as plain numbers), `presentGrams()` and `formatTime()` (`h:i A`); `formatDate()` keeps the existing portal `M d, Y` convention.
- UI: `resources/views/patient/medical-records/index.blade.php` keeps sections 1-3 byte-for-byte and adds sections 4-7 (`mr-ultrasounds-heading`, `mr-birth-plan-heading`, `mr-delivery-heading`, `mr-babies-heading`) with the same DEPLA portal card/definition-list styling, responsive 1/2/3-column grids and honest empty states: "No ultrasound records are available for this pregnancy yet.", "No birth plan has been recorded for this pregnancy yet.", "No delivery record is available for this pregnancy." (neutral - it never says a delivery did or did not happen, it says none is available) and "No baby information is available for this pregnancy yet." None of the four empty states uses the literal "Not recorded", so the Phase 3E.2 empty-state count of exactly 6 remains valid and the existing 19 tests stay green unmodified. No edit, add, delete, upload or staff action was added and the only form on the page is still the layout's POST logout (asserted).
- Privacy and authorization (unchanged contract, re-asserted): guests -> login; unlinked -> 403 with the Phase 2D wording; archived (SoftDeletes scope) -> 403; Admin -> 403; Staff -> 403; flagged -> redirect to the mandatory password change form; no patient ID from the URL, query string or request body; every new relation resolved only through the authenticated user's own `patientAccount` query, so another patient's scans/plan/outcome/babies and a name+birthdate-matched previous pregnancy both stay invisible (both asserted with distinctive markers); no write route and no patient-namespaced ultrasound/birth-plan/baby/delivery/outcome route exists (asserted); no `ultrasound.file` link, `storage/` path, `.pdf`/`.jpg` filename, download cue or file input appears in the rendered HTML (asserted); the `no-store` middleware chain is untouched.
- Tests: new `tests/Feature/Phase3E3PatientMedicalRecordsExpansionTest.php` - 23 passed / 237 assertions with `phase3e3*` prefixed helpers, covering all 19 required cases (own ultrasound; multiple ultrasounds newest first; own birth plan; own delivery record; own baby information; multiple babies; and one empty state for each of the four new sections; another patient's records never appear; historical pregnancies not exposed; staff-only notes and metadata excluded; ultrasound file paths and filenames excluded; no medical-record write or file-serving route added; Phase 3E.2 sections still render; archived and unlinked still denied; Admin and Staff still denied; forced-password-change still enforced) plus four edge cases (nullable birth-plan fields never become "No"; a NULL outcome row renders the neutral empty state while a recorded location with no outcome still shows honestly; partial ultrasound measurements format honestly; guest/clinic route authorization unchanged). Existing `Phase3E2PatientMedicalRecordsTest` (19) was NOT modified.
- Verification (isolation re-confirmed before every run: `bootstrap/cache/config.php` absent, `phpunit.xml` forces APP_ENV/DB_CONNECTION=sqlite/DB_DATABASE=:memory: with `force="true"`, `tests/TestCase.php` `ensureDatabaseIsolation` guard active; every run in a fresh process): focused Phase 3E.3 file 23/23; regression chunk (1) Phase3E2 + Phase3A + ClinicRouteAuthorization + LoginRedirectSafety + Phase2C + Phase3B3 = 142 passed / 896 assertions, 0 failed; regression chunk (2) PatientProfile* + MedicalHistoryScope + Phase3B4 + Phase2A + Phase2D = 103 passed / 586 assertions with the SAME 9 pre-existing failures documented in Phase 3E.2 (PatientProfileRiskPanelTest 8 + MedicalHistoryScopeTest 1, proven unrelated to the medical-records page and left unfixed). `php -l` clean on the controller, `routes/web.php` and both test files; no lint script exists in composer.json; no migration executed; no `.env`/config/package/git change; `public/hot` untouched; MySQL and Railway untouched.
- Known limitations: (1) CURRENT-PREGNANCY-ONLY BY DESIGN - `patients.user_id` is UNIQUE and `startNewPregnancy()` was not touched, so an account still linked to an older DELIVERED row shows that row's scans/plan/delivery/babies; no name/birthdate matching is performed and the suite asserts a matched prior pregnancy stays invisible. (2) Ultrasound images, PDF reports and downloads are deliberately deferred: no file-serving route was added for the portal and `report_file`/`report_image` are never selected, so a patient cannot view a report from this page even when one exists on file. (3) Birth plan `notes`, ultrasound `remarks` and pregnancy-outcome `notes` are free text and are never shown, so a patient will not see anything a staff member wrote in those fields. (4) The "a birth plan exists but none of its answers are recorded yet" guard is currently unreachable because `deliver_in_clinic`/`transport_cost`/`saving_started`/`plan_more_children`/`knows_fp_method`/`used_fp_before` are NOT NULL with a 0 database default (migration 2026_03_15_154906) - it is kept only as a defensive guard against a future schema change. (5) `pregnancy_outcomes` is one-per-patient by DB UNIQUE; `babies`/`ultrasounds` are hasMany and `birthPlan`/`pregnancyOutcome` are hasOne, so a hypothetical duplicate row would render one. (6) No audit row is written for a read, consistent with the rest of the portal. (7) No postpartum section - there is no postpartum data entity anywhere in the schema. (8) No browser click-through was performed (no browser automation package may be installed) - layout, empty states and scoping are asserted at HTTP/markup level.

## DEPLA Phase 3F: Patient Portal Dashboard Widgets - backend data preparation (2026-10-14)

- Requirement: prepare the two read-only presentation arrays the patient dashboard needs before the Phase 3G UI work: a `nextAppointment` array describing the patient's soonest eligible confirmed appointment, and a `maternityTracker` array summarising gestational age, trimester, due-date countdown and delivery state. No Blade changes in this sprint (Phase 3G will render them); no schema change, no new route, no write endpoint, no clinical-threshold or risk/ML change. The dashboard Blade continues to render only the four existing cards, so the five Phase 3A required strings and the forbidden-word list are preserved byte-for-byte.
- Backend: `app/Http/Controllers/PatientPortalController.php` was extended in place (no new controller, no new public method - `dashboard()` and `showMedicalRecords()` remain the only two). The controller now receives `GestationalAgeCalculator` and `PregnancyOutcomeMonitoringService` through constructor dependency injection (mirroring the existing `PrenatalVisitController`/`RiskAssessmentService` pattern; `TrimesterClassifier` is static and needs no injection). `dashboard()` computes `$clinicToday = Carbon::now(AppointmentManagementService::CLINIC_TIMEZONE)` (Asia/Manila) once and passes it, plus the authenticated user's linked Patient row, into two new private methods - `presentNextAppointment($patient, $clinicToday)` and `presentMaternityTracker($patient, $clinicToday)`. No other method changed and no existing line was rewritten.
- Next-appointment selection rules: queried ONLY through the patient's own `appointments()` relation (never a global query, never an id from the request); `status = confirmed` only (pending, cancelled, declined, completed and soft-deleted rows are excluded by the status filter, the SoftDeletes scope and the explicit `whereNull('deleted_at')` guard); the effective date is `COALESCE(confirmed_date, requested_date)` and is used for BOTH the `>= clinic-today` eligibility filter and the ordering, never `requested_date` alone; ordering is effective date ASC, then effective time `COALESCE(confirmed_time, requested_time)` ASC with a `CASE WHEN ... IS NULL THEN 1 ELSE 0` secondary sort so a date-only appointment (no recorded time) ranks AFTER any timed appointment on the same day, then `id ASC` as the deterministic tie-breaker; only the first row is selected (`->first()`); no provider/doctor field is selected or displayed (the appointments table stores none); no pending count, no next-visit-date fallback, no LMP-derived guess. When no row is eligible the method returns `null`, which the (future) Blade renders as the existing "not available yet" placeholder. The SQL uses one `select()` with an explicit column allow-list and one `orderByRaw()` per sort key - no N+1, no `SELECT *`.
- Next-appointment presentation: `appointment_type_label` (from the existing `Appointment::typeLabel()` / `TYPE_LABELS` map), `effective_date_display` (`Carbon::parse($row->effective_date)->format('l, F j, Y')`), `effective_time_display` (`substr($row->effective_time, 0, 5)` when a time exists, otherwise the literal "Time not recorded"), `status_label` (the literal "Confirmed" - the only status this array ever describes). Exactly these four keys, in this order; no id, no patient_id, no source, no decline_reason, no cancellation_reason, no created_at, no reviewed_by, no proposed_* fields, no raw Carbon objects (both dates and times are pre-formatted strings).
- Maternity-tracker rules: resolved ONLY through the authenticated user's own `patientAccount` relationship (the same inline query `dashboard()` already used - never a URL segment, query string or request body); gestational age is computed by the existing `GestationalAgeCalculator::calculate($patient->lmp?->format('Y-m-d'), $clinicToday->toDateString())` (decimal weeks, one decimal place, reversed/invalid dates return null - the service's own contract, not reimplemented); trimester is `TrimesterClassifier::label($gestationalAge)` (`<1` null, `<13` 1st, `<28` 2nd, else 3rd - thresholds taken from the existing classifier, never changed); the due-date countdown reuses `PregnancyOutcomeMonitoringService::daysUntilOrPastEdd()` verbatim and never derives an EDD from LMP or recomputes a signed day count locally. The countdown reference date is the MANILA clinic calendar date passed as a `Carbon` at midnight in the same timezone as the patient's own `edd` cast instance; this matters because `patients.edd` is a pure calendar date stored through the `date` cast, and anchoring the service's `startOfDay()->diffInDays()` at the same midnight representation keeps the day difference an exact integer instead of losing or gaining a day to the 8-hour Manila/UTC offset (an earlier draft that passed `now()` directly or a hardcoded-UTC anchor produced "179 days to go" where the calendar says 180). Strings: `N days to go` (future, `$days > 0`), "Due date is today" (`$days === 0`), "Estimated due date has passed - please contact the clinic" (`$days < 0` - deliberately not "delivered", because a passed EDD is not evidence of delivery), "Due date not recorded" (EDD is null). No progress bar, no percentage-complete, no risk level, no ML prediction, no referral state, no staff name, no confirmation metadata.
- Maternity-tracker delivery state: when `patients.status === 'DELIVERED'` the array returns a completion state - `is_delivered` true, `status_label` "Delivered", `edd_countdown_display` null (no countdown is shown once the pregnancy has ended), `weeks_along_display` null, `trimester_label` null (gestational age is meaningless after delivery and is never back-calculated from a delivery date), and `delivery_date_display` set only when `patients.delivery_date` is actually recorded (otherwise null, never "today" or the EDD). When the pregnancy is ONGOING the completion fields are false and the gestational columns are populated as above. A `pregnancy_outcomes` row was deliberately NOT consulted - `patients.status` and `patients.delivery_date` are the canonical delivery signals used everywhere else in this portal (Phase 3E.3 included), and introducing a second source would risk the two disagreeing.
- Presentation arrays (full key list, in order): `nextAppointment` -> `appointment_type_label`, `effective_date_display`, `effective_time_display`, `status_label` (or the whole value is `null`); `maternityTracker` -> `is_delivered`, `status_label`, `lmp_display` (`M d, Y` or null), `edd_display` (`M d, Y` or null), `weeks_along_display` (`number_format($gestationalAge, 1).' weeks'` or null), `trimester_label` (or null), `edd_countdown_display` (one of the four strings above, or null when delivered/missing), `delivery_date_display` (`M d, Y` or null). Every date uses the existing portal `M d, Y` convention already established by Phase 3E.2/3E.3; every nullable field is `null` when unrecorded, never a manufactured "No", "0" or today's date. Both arrays are passed to the view as plain PHP arrays - no Eloquent model is placed in the widget data, so there is no attribute dump to leak even if a future template change accidentally printed the whole variable.
- UI: NO Blade file was opened, created or modified in this sprint, per the 3F scope rule. `resources/views/patient/dashboard.blade.php`, `resources/views/patient/layout.blade.php` and `resources/views/patient/medical-records/index.blade.php` remain exactly as they were at the end of the responsive-UI/3E.5 work (their pre-existing uncommitted diffs are preserved byte-for-byte - verified by `git status` showing the same three modified files and the same untracked `public/hot` before and after this sprint). The `nextAppointment` and `maternityTracker` arrays are computed on every dashboard request and simply unused by the current template; the Phase 3G sprint will add the two card bodies that render them. Because the Blade is unchanged, the five Phase 3A required strings and every forbidden word continue to pass their existing assertions unmodified.
- Tests: new `tests/Feature/Phase3FPatientDashboardWidgetsTest.php` - 22 passed / 134 assertions with `phase3f*`-prefixed helpers (`phase3fUser`, `phase3fPatient`, `phase3fLink`, `phase3fEligible`, `phase3fAppointment`, `phase3fManilaDate`, `phase3fFreeze`, `phase3fDashboardData`), an `afterEach` hook that clears `Carbon::setTestNow()`, and two invocation paths - HTTP GETs through the real `/patient/dashboard` route for access/authorization/string-preservation cases, and direct `app(PatientPortalController::class)->dashboard($request)` calls (with `Request::create(...)` + `setUserResolver()`) for the data-shape assertions, because the Blade does not render these arrays yet and the sprint forbids touching it. Coverage: guest redirected to login; unlinked patient 403 with the Phase 2D wording; archived patient 403; Admin 403; Staff 403; flagged (must-change-password) user redirected to the password-change form; a confirmed appointment belonging to a DIFFERENT patient never appears; a pending/cancelled/declined/soft-deleted appointment is never selected; a confirmed appointment dated yesterday is never selected while one dated today is; the earliest eligible appointment wins over a later one; confirmed_date takes precedence over a later requested_date for both eligibility and ordering; a NULL confirmed_date falls back to the requested_date; same-date ties order by effective time (09:00 before 14:00, and a date-only appointment ranks after both timed ones and shows "Time not recorded"); same-schedule ties break deterministically by lower `id` (proven by giving the two rows different `appointment_type` values and observing the label flip after deleting the winner); `nextAppointment` is exactly the four allow-listed keys and never contains decline/cancellation reasons or any id; when nothing is eligible the array is `null`. Maternity tracker: an ONGOING pregnancy with LMP+EDD renders `is_delivered` false, both `M d, Y` dates, `N.N weeks`, the correct trimester label and "N days to go"; a missing LMP leaves weeks/trimester null without inventing gestational age; a missing EDD leaves the countdown as "Due date not recorded" without inventing a due date; a pregnancy with neither renders every clinical field null while `is_delivered` stays false; an EDD falling exactly on the clinic date renders "Due date is today"; an EDD in the past renders the contact-the-clinic string and does NOT mark the pregnancy delivered; a DELIVERED pregnancy returns the completion state with a null countdown and no gestational columns; a delivered pregnancy with no recorded delivery date leaves `delivery_date_display` null; the tracker array never contains a risk level, ML prediction, referral state, progress percentage or staff name (value-level assertions against seeded marker strings plus key-list assertions). The test file uses `PHPUnit\Framework\Assert::assertInstanceOf` (not `Illuminate\Support\Assertions`, which does not exist) and uses only the SQLite `:memory:` database enforced by `phpunit.xml` and the `tests/TestCase.php` isolation guard - no MySQL, no config cache, no migration.
- Verification (isolation re-confirmed before every run: `bootstrap/cache/config.php` absent, `phpunit.xml` forces APP_ENV=testing / DB_CONNECTION=sqlite / DB_DATABASE=:memory: with `force="true"`, `tests/TestCase.php` `ensureDatabaseIsolation` guard active; every run in a fresh process): focused Phase 3F file 22/22 (134 assertions); regression chunk (1) Phase3APatientPortalDashboardTest + Phase2CInitialPasswordChangeTest + ClinicRouteAuthorizationTest + LoginRedirectSafetyTest = 82 passed / 457 assertions, 0 failed; regression chunk (2) Phase3B3PatientAppointmentsTest + Phase3E2PatientMedicalRecordsTest + Phase3E3PatientMedicalRecordsExpansionTest = 83 passed / 677 assertions, 0 failed. Combined 187 passed / 0 failed (165 pre-existing + 22 new). `php -l` clean on `PatientPortalController.php` and the new test file; no lint script exists in composer.json (no pint/php-cs-fixer); no migration executed; no `.env`/config/package/git change; `public/hot` untouched; MySQL and Railway untouched; the three pre-existing modified Blade files and the uncommitted responsive-UI work were not altered.
- Pre-existing failures - NOT caused by this sprint, verified, and deliberately left unfixed: `PatientProfileRiskPanelTest` (8 failures) and `MedicalHistoryScopeTest` (1 failure) against `patients.show`, plus `ProfileAccessHeaderTest > profile update changes only the current authenticated user` (clinic-side, unrelated to the patient portal) - all documented identically in the Phase 3E.2/3E.3 entries above and re-confirmed unrelated to the dashboard data preparation, which does not touch `PatientController`, `patients.show`, medical-history scope logic or any clinic-facing view.
- Defense notes: both widgets are built exclusively from the authenticated user's own relationships (`appointments()` and `patientAccount`), never from request input, so one patient cannot read another patient's schedule or pregnancy state even by forging a query parameter (asserted with a second patient's distinctive marker values). The `COALESCE`-based effective-date logic lives entirely in one `select()`/`orderByRaw()` pair inside a private method, so there is no second implementation to drift from the first, and the same expression is used for filtering and ordering - a row can never be eligible by one date and sorted by another. The allow-listed `select()` plus the plain-array hand-off mean a future Blade edit cannot accidentally dump a column the sprint did not approve; the key-list assertions (`array_keys($next)`, `array_keys($tracker)`) will fail loudly if a future change adds one. Reusing `GestationalAgeCalculator`, `TrimesterClassifier` and `PregnancyOutcomeMonitoringService` verbatim (rather than reimplementing the arithmetic in the controller) means any future correction to GA rounding, trimester boundaries or signed-day semantics lands in one place and automatically reaches this widget - and, critically, no clinical threshold was touched in this sprint (the existing 1.0/13.0/28.0 boundaries and the existing service's day-count contract are used exactly as-is). The same-timezone midnight countdown anchor is documented inline because it is the one non-obvious line in the change: without it the Manila clinic date and the UTC-stored EDD disagree by 8 hours and a patient checking in the morning would see "179 days to go" for a due date the calendar calls 180 days out - an off-by-one that would also have flipped the "Due date is today" boundary for any patient checking between Manila 00:00 and 08:00. Honest limitations: (1) the arrays are not yet rendered - the Phase 3G sprint must add the Blade markup, and until then a patient sees the exact same four-card dashboard they saw before this sprint (the data is computed and discarded by the unused template); (2) `pregnancy_outcomes` is not consulted for the delivery state, so if a staff member ever marked a `pregnancy_outcomes` row DELIVERED without also flipping `patients.status`, the tracker would still show ONGOING until the patient row is updated - this matches every other delivery-aware surface in the portal (Phase 3E.3 included) and is the canonical signal, but it is a real coupling to document; (3) twins/multiples share one `patients` row and therefore one tracker - there is no per-fetal counter anywhere in this schema and none was invented; (4) no audit row is written for these reads, consistent with the rest of the portal; (5) no browser click-through was performed (no browser automation package may be installed) - the data-shape assertions invoke the controller directly and the HTTP assertions confirm the existing four-card markup is byte-identical, but nobody has yet seen the Phase 3G widgets on screen because they do not exist yet; (6) the pre-existing monolithic full-suite hang documented in Phase 3D.2A was avoided by running focused gates in fresh processes (about 40s total across two chunks).
## DEPLA Phase 3H.5: Admin-Verified Previous Pregnancy Linking (2026-10-14)

- Requirement: allow an authorized Admin, after verifying patient identity through the clinic's established procedure, to directly link an existing historical DELIVERED pregnancy episode to the correct Patient Portal account so it immediately appears under Patient Portal -> Pregnancy History. Explicitly NOT demographic auto-matching (no name/birthdate/contact matching decides any pairing anywhere in this phase), NOT a staff feature (Admin-only), and NOT a change to Start New Pregnancy, the current-pregnancy pointer (patients.user_id) or any clinical record. A soft-revocation mechanism (user-approved Option A) lets an Admin retract an incorrect link without deleting medical records or evidence rows.
- Backend: new `app/Services/HistoricalPregnancyLinkService.php` - sole owner of the link/revoke/restore transactions, each a single DB::transaction that (1) lockForUpdate()s the profile episode, validates the live patient-role account resolved server-side from the profile's pointer, (2) lockForUpdate()s the target episode (SoftDeletes active: archived = not found = refused), refuses non-DELIVERED / current-pointer / foreign-pointer / already-linked (active OR revoked) targets, (3) inserts the link row via direct property assignment (verified_by = admin, verified_at = now) with UniqueConstraintViolationException translated to a safe DomainException, (4) writes an ID-based AuditLog (HISTORICAL_PREGNANCY_LINKED / _REVOKED / _RESTORED) INSIDE the transaction. New `app/Http/Controllers/AdminPregnancyLinkController.php` (candidates GET, store/revoke/restore POST) + three FormRequests each re-checking role === 'admin' (LinkPreviousPregnancyRequest requires confirm=accepted + non-empty verification_method + verification_reference, both max 120). Four new admin-only routes inside the clinic group behind AdminMiddleware (the routes/web.php:371 precedent): patients.pregnancy-links.candidates/store/revoke/restore. Candidates search returns a strict 10-column allow-list (id, name, birthdate, status, lmp, edd, delivery_date, gravida, para; max 10 rows; exact episode ID or exact last-name lookup only; DELIVERED, non-archived, user_id IS NULL, no link row of ANY kind, profile episode excluded) - lookup aids for the admin's manual verification, never consumed by the service.
- Schema (new migration 2026_10_15_000001_harden_user_pregnancy_links_for_admin_linking.php - NOT executed against MySQL; SQLite :memory: only): drops the non-unique patient_id index and adds UNIQUE(patient_id) - one episode, one account, ever, as the database-level backstop that application checks alone cannot provide under concurrency; adds soft-revocation columns revoked_at / revoked_by (FK users SET NULL) / revocation_reason (250). up() loudly refuses (RuntimeException, zero changes) when the table is missing, the columns already exist, or any patient_id already holds multiple rows. down() refuses while any revoked row exists. The Phase 3H.2 (user_id, patient_id) unique pair is unchanged.
- Query-site filters (soft-revoke wiring, one line each): `PresentsPregnancyRecords::resolveVerifiedEpisodeWithRecords` and the `PatientPregnancyHistoryController::index` subquery now require `whereNull('revoked_at')` (revoked links grant no History visibility); `PregnancyContinuationService::foreignLink` guard now counts only active rows (a revoked foreign link no longer blocks continuation). `PatientAccountCreationService` conflict guard and `ensureVerifiedLink` deliberately keep counting ALL rows (including revoked): a revoked row blocks silent re-pairing and re-creation; the same pairing returns only through the audited Restore action.
- UI: `PatientController::show` passes an admin-only `$portalLinks` payload (links of the profile's portal account with patient + verifier names; no clinical relations). `resources/views/patients/show.blade.php` gains a "Previous Pregnancy Links" section after the Patient Login Account block - links table (episode #, delivery, LMP, G/P, Active/Revoked/Current badges, verifier, inline revoke/restore forms with required reason) plus an "Add Previous Pregnancy" modal (debounced fetch candidates search, explicit Select, verification-method dropdown, reference input, mandatory confirmation checkbox, amber data-minimization warning). Staff never receive or render the panel (tested).
- Security: admin-only enforced in THREE independent layers (route AdminMiddleware, FormRequest authorize, view gate); the account is resolved server-side from the profile's pointer (never request input); no demographic logic exists in service or controller; search results never include email/contact/address or any clinical child; portal remains GET-only read-only (route-scan asserted); patients.user_id is never written by this phase; pointer-link revocation is refused (use Start New Pregnancy/archive instead).
- Tests: new `tests/Feature/Phase3H5AdminPregnancyLinkingTest.php` - 32 passed / 149 assertions with `phase3h5*`-prefixed helpers, covering the full mandated matrix: admin successful link + immediate portal History visibility (index AND direct URL); audit content (admin, episode, account, method, reference); staff 403 / patient 403 / guest login-redirect for store AND candidates; duplicate pair refused (1 row kept); cross-account conflict refused; database UNIQUE(patient_id) backstop (direct second insert throws); concurrent-race simulation leaves no partial state / no audit / no pointer change; archived / ONGOING / own-current / foreign-current targets refused; missing confirm/method/reference 422s + service-level empty-evidence DomainException; revoke hides History + 404s the direct URL + keeps clinical rows, evidence row and audit; restore re-shows History + audits; pointer-link revoke refused; double-revoke and restore-active refused; missing-reason 422; profile without account 4xx; candidates data-minimization (exact key list, ineligible rows excluded, ID search, profile self-exclusion); admin-only panel present / staff-absent; revoked-foreign-link continuation regression (proceeds) + active-foreign-link regression (still refuses); migration schema guarantees; zero portal write routes.
- Two existing schema-contract tests were updated IN PLACE because this user-approved phase intentionally changed their documented contract (not to force a pass): `Phase3H2VerifiedPregnancyLinksTest` "stores the required columns..." now asserts UNIQUE(patient_id) exists and the non-unique index is gone; `Phase3H3PregnancyContinuationTest` "refuses continuation when another account holds verified-link evidence..." now asserts the conflicting second row is refused by the database itself (UniqueConstraintViolationException) and that continuation still proceeds normally on the clean linked episode - the two-owner state is no longer constructible by design. Both suites re-ran green (3H.2 30/30, 3H.3 19/19, 3H.4 25/25).
- Verification (isolation re-confirmed before every run: `bootstrap/cache/config.php` absent, `phpunit.xml` forces APP_ENV/DB_CONNECTION=sqlite/DB_DATABASE=:memory: with `force="true"`, `tests/TestCase.php` guard active; fresh processes): focused Phase 3H.5 file 32/32; regression chunk (1) Phase3H2+3H3+3H4 = 73/73; chunk (2) Phase3E2+3E3+Phase2B+2D+3A = 128/128; chunk (3) ClinicRouteAuthorization + Phase3D4B + Phase3F + PatientProfileAddressDisplay + Phase2A = PASS with only PatientViewControllerTest failing (17). Total 307 passed across this sprint's surface. `php -l` clean on all 11 touched PHP files; no lint script exists in composer.json; NO migration executed against MySQL (requires separate explicit approval, same as Phase 3H.2); no `.env`/config/package/git change; `public/hot` untouched; MySQL and Railway untouched.
- Pre-existing failures - NOT caused by this sprint, verified, and deliberately left unfixed: `PatientViewControllerTest` (17 failures on view-all-records sorting/pagination/dedup) - PatientViewController.php and its Blade are UNMODIFIED versus HEAD, reference nothing from Phase 3H.5 (no UserPregnancyLink, no revoked_at, no portalLinks), and the identical failures appear in earlier session tool-outputs predating this sprint; same report-as-is policy as PatientProfileRiskPanelTest (8), MedicalHistoryScopeTest (1) and ProfileAccessHeaderTest from Phases 3E.2-3F.
- Defense notes: the UNIQUE(patient_id) migration is the direct answer to the mandate's concurrency concern - it converts the one-episode-one-account invariant from an application convention into a database guarantee for every current and future writer, and the service additionally serializes all conforming writers on the target episode row via lockForUpdate, so a concurrent conflicting attempt either loses on the existence check or fails cleanly on the constraint (both paths tested). Soft-revocation (not hard delete) preserves the 3H.2 RESTRICT doctrine: revoked rows remain as durable evidence, permanently occupy their patient_id slot (so a revoked episode can never be silently re-homed to a different account - only the same pairing can be restored, audited), and cost exactly three one-line whereNull filters at the portal/consumption sites. Evidence is mandatory at BOTH the HTTP layer (422) and the service boundary (DomainException), so a direct service caller cannot link without it; the audit description carries method + reference text but never patient names (ID-only convention). Account resolution from the profile pointer means an admin acting on a profile can only ever link to THAT profile's login - the request's target_episode_id selects the episode, never the account.
- Known limitations: (1) the migration has NOT been executed against local MySQL `maternity_system1` or Railway - running it requires separate explicit approval and the pre-flight will loudly refuse if any duplicate patient_id rows exist (currently 0 per the SELECT-only audit); (2) Restore re-activates the SAME pairing only - re-homing a revoked episode to a different account is intentionally impossible while the row exists; (3) candidates search matches exact episode ID or exact last name only (no fuzzy search, no name+birthdate combination) - a deliberate data-minimization trade-off; (4) verification method is a fixed dropdown plus free-text reference - the clinic owns the underlying identity procedure, the system only records that it was performed; (5) the panel shows no pagination (an account holding dozens of historical links would need a follow-up); (6) no browser click-through was performed (no browser automation package may be installed) - the modal/search flow is asserted at HTTP/markup level; (7) the pre-existing monolithic full-suite hang documented in Phase 3D.2A was avoided by running focused gates in fresh processes (about 30s total across three chunks).

### Phase 3H.5 migration repair (2026-10-14, same sprint)

- Incident: the 3H.5 migration was executed against local MySQL `maternity_system1` (user-run `php artisan migrate`) and failed with `SQLSTATE[HY000] Error 1553: Cannot drop index 'user_pregnancy_links_patient_id_index': needed in a foreign key constraint`. MariaDB DDL is non-transactional, so the three revocation columns and the revoked_by FK committed before the failure, leaving a PARTIAL state: columns + FK present, old non-unique index still present, UNIQUE(patient_id) absent, migration record still Pending. Read-only inspection confirmed 5 link rows (episodes 75, 87, 90, 91, 92), 5 distinct patient_ids, 0 duplicate groups. This supersedes the original entry's "NO migration executed" note, which was accurate when written.
- Root cause: the original up() queued DROP INDEX before ADD UNIQUE inside a single Schema::table() Blueprint; Laravel compiles commands in queue order, so the patient_id foreign key from Phase 3H.2 lost its only supporting index before its replacement existed. A secondary defect: the old up() hard-refused on an existing revocation column, so it could never re-enter and finish the partial state.
- Repair (only `database/migrations/2026_10_15_000001_harden_user_pregnancy_links_for_admin_linking.php` rewritten): idempotent inventory-driven up() - all-or-none revocation-column coherence check (mixed = loud refusal, fresh = single ALTER, present = skip); revoked_by FK added only when `Schema::getForeignKeys()` shows it missing; **ADD UNIQUE(patient_id) now runs BEFORE the old index drop**, each in its own Schema::table() so cross-driver statement order is guaranteed, and the old index is dropped by its actual live name. down() mirrors the ordering rule (re-creates the non-unique index before dropping the unique one) and stays idempotent; the revoked-evidence refusal, duplicate-ownership preflight, table-exists guard, pair unique and RESTRICT FKs are unchanged. No step touches rows - ownership links and revocation evidence are preserved byte-for-byte. SQLite note: its grammar compiles foreign-key ALTERs as no-ops (FKs are creation-time there), so the FK inventory simply reports absent on SQLite; MySQL receives the real ADD CONSTRAINT.
- Verification (SQLite :memory: only, isolation re-confirmed: no config cache, phpunit.xml force=true guards active): new `tests/Feature/Phase3H5MigrationRepairTest.php` 6/6 - fresh shape + idempotent re-entry; partial-state reconstruction (columns present, unique absent, old index present) repaired with all rows preserved and second pass idempotent; mixed-column refusal; duplicate-preflight refusal leaving both rows untouched; down()/up() round-trip preserving rows; revoked-evidence rollback refusal. Regression chunk Phase3H2+3H3+3H4+3H5 = 105/105 (504 assertions). `php -l` clean. No MySQL write was performed during the repair; inspection was SELECT-only.
- Pending: real-MySQL re-run of the repaired migration (exact expected SQL on the partial state: ADD UNIQUE, then DROP INDEX, then the Pending -> Ran record) requires separate explicit approval; recommended pre-step is a targeted `mysqldump` of the table; post-run verification plan (SHOW INDEX/SHOW CREATE TABLE, row-count vs distinct-patient_id equality, admin-panel and one-link smoke test) is defined in the repair report.


## DEPLA Phase 3H.5 Retirement + Automatic Pregnancy History (2026-10-14)

- Requirement (Task A of the approved Phase 3H Cleanup): remove the Admin-Verified Previous Pregnancy Linking workflow (link / revoke / restore / candidates search, panel, modal, service, routes) and make Pregnancy History follow the pregnancy lifecycle automatically - a DELIVERED episode owned through the Phase 3H.2 user_pregnancy_links structure appears in the owning login's Pregnancy History on the next page request, with or without Start New Pregnancy, with no background job, no manual linking and no Admin action. Legacy unlinked-episode association is explicitly deferred to Task B. No demographic matching was introduced anywhere.
- Files REMOVED (whole files, zero remaining references - verified by repo-wide grep): `app/Services/HistoricalPregnancyLinkService.php`; `app/Http/Controllers/AdminPregnancyLinkController.php`; `app/Http/Requests/LinkPreviousPregnancyRequest.php`; `app/Http/Requests/RevokePregnancyLinkRequest.php`; `app/Http/Requests/RestorePregnancyLinkRequest.php`; `database/migrations/2026_10_15_000001_harden_user_pregnancy_links_for_admin_linking.php` (retired pending migration, see below); `tests/Feature/Phase3H5AdminPregnancyLinkingTest.php` (32 tests); `tests/Feature/Phase3H5MigrationRepairTest.php` (6 tests).
- Files MODIFIED: `routes/web.php` (removed the AdminPregnancyLinkController import and the four AdminMiddleware pregnancy-link routes); `app/Http/Controllers/PatientController.php::show` (removed the admin-only `$portalLinks` payload and its compact() entry); `resources/views/patients/show.blade.php` (removed the 351-line Previous Pregnancy Links section - panel, Add Previous Pregnancy modal and candidate-search JavaScript; all other profile functionality preserved); `app/Http/Controllers/Concerns/PresentsPregnancyRecords.php` and `app/Http/Controllers/PatientPregnancyHistoryController.php` and `app/Services/PregnancyContinuationService.php` (removed the three Phase 3H.5 `whereNull('revoked_at')` filters and their comments - ownership is again "an existing verified link row establishes ownership", unchanged from Phase 3H.2).
- Automatic lifecycle design (no new mechanism): `PregnancyOutcomeRecordingService` already sets status=DELIVERED + delivery_date in its transaction, and the owning link row already exists from account provisioning / continuation / the 3H.2 backfill - so the ONLY change needed was in `PatientPregnancyHistoryController`: the index query no longer excludes the pointer episode (`where id != current` removed; the canonical DELIVERED status filter alone keeps ONGOING episodes out), and show()'s scope check dropped the not-current condition (404 only when status != DELIVERED). Authorization is unchanged everywhere: strict user_pregnancy_links membership first, then delivered-only scope; pointer-only episodes, unlinked episodes, foreign episodes, archived episodes and nonexistent IDs all remain 404/omitted; My Medical Records and the dashboard are untouched; portal history stays GET-only.
- Migration retirement + partial local schema: the hardening migration was deleted from the project WITHOUT running, rolling back or cleaning anything. Because a failed migration is never recorded in the `migrations` table, deleting the file fully retires it (migrate/migrate:status forget it). Local MySQL `maternity_system1` keeps its partial columns `revoked_at`, `revoked_by` (+ its users SET NULL FK) and `revocation_reason` as inert nullable schema - no code references them anymore and no replacement migration was created (Task A rule). The UNIQUE(patient_id) index was never applied; the original non-unique patient_id index and the Phase 3H.2 (user_id, patient_id) pair-unique remain. All 5 existing ownership link rows (episodes 75, 87, 90, 91, 92) and every patient/clinical row are preserved - the cleanup touched only files and code paths. Fresh installs (SQLite tests) get the pure Phase 3H.2 shape.
- Tests (SQLite :memory: only; isolation re-confirmed before every run: no bootstrap/cache/config.php, phpunit.xml force=true APP_ENV/DB_CONNECTION/DB_DATABASE, TestCase guard active; fresh processes): NEW `tests/Feature/Phase3H5RetirementAutomaticHistoryTest.php` 13/13 (89 assertions) - ONGOING never listed; delivery recording surfaces the episode immediately with no Start New Pregnancy and no invented second episode; continuation preserves the previous DELIVERED episode in History while the new ONGOING becomes current and both link rows survive; multiple owned DELIVERED episodes listed newest-first; unlinked/cross-account/archived/ONGOING-historical boundaries; all four retired route URIs 404 for admin/staff/patient/guest with route names unregistered; patients.show no longer renders the panel for admin; History routes GET-only; GETs preserve every link row and pointer; Phase 3H.2 pair-unique + ordinary patient_id index shape confirmed. UPDATED contracts (intentional, user-approved): Phase3H2 schema test reverted to the 3H.2 shape (pair unique, non-unique patient_id index present, no unique(patient_id), revocation columns absent); Phase3H3 two-owner test reverted to construct-then-refuse (continuation refuses with "conflicting account-link history", no reassignment); Phase3H4 pointer tests inverted (current DELIVERED pointer listed + show 200; ONGOING pointer still excluded/404) plus header docblocks. Regressions: Phase3H2+3H3+3H4 = 73/73; Phase3E2+3E3+Phase2B+Phase2D+Phase3A = 128/128; ClinicRoute+Phase3D4B+Phase3F+PatientProfileAddressDisplay+Phase2A = 67/67. Total 281 green. `php -l` clean on all 9 touched PHP files.
- Pre-existing failures - NOT caused by this sprint, verified, deliberately left unfixed: `PatientViewControllerTest` (17, identical set to the pre-sprint baseline; PatientViewController.php and its Blade are unmodified by this cleanup and reference nothing from Phase 3H.5). Same report-as-is policy as PatientProfileRiskPanelTest (8), MedicalHistoryScopeTest (1) and ProfileAccessHeaderTest from Phases 3E.2-3F.
- Defense notes: automatic visibility is a READ-TIME rule change only - no writer was added, so no new failure mode can corrupt ownership; the DELIVERED status filter alone guarantees ONGOING episodes never leak into History; ownership still requires an explicit link row (provisioning/continuation/backfill), so the ~27 legacy unlinked DELIVERED episodes stay invisible by design (Task B scope); revocation columns remain on local MySQL as documented dead schema rather than requiring a new migration, per the no-replacement-migration rule; the retired migration file was removed only after repo-wide greps proved zero code dependencies. Strict restrictions honored: no MySQL write, no Railway, no migration/rollback execution, no .env/config change, no config-cache operation, no dependency install, no git stage/commit/push, no worktree, public/hot untouched.
- Known limitations / Task B: (1) legacy DELIVERED episodes without any ownership evidence still never appear in the portal - associating them requires an explicit, audited Task B design, never demographics; (2) the database-level UNIQUE(patient_id) one-episode-one-account backstop is gone with the retired migration (it never reached MySQL anyway) - remaining guards are the Phase 3H.2 pair-unique, the PatientAccountCreationService conflict check and the continuation foreign-link refusal, i.e. the accepted pre-3H.5 posture; (3) the three leftover nullable revocation columns (+ FK) on local MySQL persist until a future optional cleanup migration; (4) no browser click-through was performed (no browser automation may be installed) - all UI assertions are HTTP/markup level.

## DEPLA Task B: Automatic Legacy Pregnancy History Association (2026-10-14)

- Requirement: when staff creates a Patient Portal account from the existing Admin -> Patients -> Patient Profile -> Create Patient Account workflow, every other live DELIVERED pregnancy episode sharing the anchored episode's exact four-field identity is automatically associated with the new account inside the same transaction, so all previous delivered pregnancies appear in Pregnancy History on first login with no second staff action, no new account-creation workflow, no manual linking page and no confirmation modal. Includes a reconciliation command for accounts provisioned before this behavior existed. Scenarios covered: ONGOING anchor with previous DELIVERED episodes; DELIVERED-only account (no ONGOING required, none invented); delivery recording surfacing the owned episode (Task A lifecycle, unchanged); Start New Pregnancy preserving previous DELIVERED history (unchanged).
- Files ADDED: `app/Services/PregnancyHistoryAssociationService.php` (shared four-field matching + plan/apply ownership logic used by BOTH writers); `app/Console/Commands/ReconcilePregnancyHistoryLinks.php` (`depla:reconcile-pregnancy-history`); `tests/Feature/Phase3HTaskBAutomaticHistoryAssociationTest.php` (24 tests). Files MODIFIED: `app/Services/PatientAccountCreationService.php` (constructor DI of the association service; expansion + conflict abort inserted inside the existing provisioning transaction, between the pointer link insert and the PATIENT_ACCOUNT_CREATED audit). No route, Blade, migration, model, middleware or controller change; no schema change; no new migration.
- Four-field matching rule (approved, exact): `first_name` + `last_name` exact, `middle_name` NULL-safe, `birthdate` NULL-safe - the clinic's existing same-person convention, reusing the exact `PatientViewController::pregnanciesFor()` query shape. No fuzzy matching, no LOWER/TRIM normalization, and email/phone/address are NEVER identity keys (local data shows 2 emails shared across different identity tuples and 47 rows without email). The birthdate is compared by passing the cast Carbon straight into where() so the query grammar serializes it with the CONNECTION's date format - matching the stored value identically on SQLite ('Y-m-d H:i:s') and MySQL ('Y-m-d'), the same reason pregnanciesFor works on both drivers. Association is anchored ONLY on the explicit provisioning action (or the reconciliation command's existing pointer anchor); demographics alone never discover an account, so this is not demographic-only matching - it extends an explicitly established account-to-episode pairing within that person's own cluster.
- Automatic association during account creation (`PatientAccountCreationService::createForPatient`, inside the existing single transaction, after the pointer episode's verified link and before the account-created audit): the shared service plans the cluster (live, status exactly DELIVERED, excluding the anchor; SoftDeletes scope hides archived rows; ONGOING/REFERRED siblings are never candidates); a candidate carrying ANY foreign ownership - a patients.user_id pointer or a user_pregnancy_links row belonging to another account - aborts the WHOLE provisioning with a DomainException surfaced through the existing errors->status channel, so user, pointer, links and audit all roll back and ownership is never reassigned; same-account pairs are preserved without duplication (idempotency; unreachable for a brand-new user at creation); each newly created link writes its own `PREGNANCY_HISTORY_LINKED` AuditLog row with record IDs only (login user ID, episode ID, anchor patient ID - never names). The Phase 2B conflict guard on the pointer patient itself is unchanged.
- First-login visibility: the new links are ordinary Phase 3H.2 membership rows, so Task A's links-driven Pregnancy History lists every associated DELIVERED episode immediately (index and direct URLs) with the portal still GET-only, read-only, and no GET-time writes; the ONGOING current episode stays on My Medical Records/dashboard and never appears in History; cross-account, unlinked, archived and ONGOING episodes remain 404/omitted.
- DELIVERED-only accounts (mandate Scenario B): provisioning from a DELIVERED anchor works unchanged - the pointer episode IS listed in Pregnancy History (Task A), the matching siblings are auto-linked, no ONGOING episode is created, and My Medical Records + dashboard + Profile all render against the DELIVERED pointer (tested). EnsurePatientPortalAccount only requires a live linked row, so no middleware change was needed.
- Reconciliation command `depla:reconcile-pregnancy-history` (existing accounts provisioned before Task B): DRY-RUN by default - no write of any kind without an explicit `--apply`; output is AGGREGATE COUNTS ONLY (anchors scanned, missing links, preserved pairs, conflicting clusters refused, archived/dangling accounts skipped, links created); it reuses the same plan/apply service so matching and conflict doctrine cannot drift; conflicting clusters are refused as a whole and only counted; archived accounts are skipped and never restored; clinical records, statuses and pointers are never touched; newly created links carry verified_by = NULL (no staff verifier identity exists in a console context) plus an ID-only audit row attributed to the owning account; repeated execution is idempotent (pair-unique backstop + preserved-pair skip). The command was NEVER executed against local MySQL during implementation - only through SQLite :memory: tests; --apply was never run against any real database.
- Tests (SQLite :memory: only; isolation re-confirmed before every run: no bootstrap/cache/config.php, phpunit.xml force=true APP_ENV/DB_CONNECTION/DB_DATABASE, TestCase guard active; fresh processes): NEW Phase3HTaskBAutomaticHistoryAssociationTest 24/24 (194 assertions) covering all 22 mandated cases plus NULL-birthdate matching, reconciliation cluster refusal, archived-account skip and ID-only audit content. One fixture defect was caught and fixed during the sprint (the email-only distractor accidentally shared the full identity tuple and was correctly linked - the fixture was corrected to share only the email; production code was untouched). Regressions: Phase2B = 32/32; Phase2C+2D = 60/60; Phase3H2+3H3+3H4 = 73/73; Phase3H5Retirement+3E2+3A = 62/62; Phase3E3+3F = 45/45. Total 296 green across this sprint's surface. `php -l` clean on the three new/modified PHP files.
- Safety compliance: no local MySQL write of any kind (no INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE, no migration, not even a command dry-run against MySQL); no Railway access or deployment; no schema change or migration; no .env/config/database.php modification; no optimize:clear or config-cache operation; no dependency install; no git stage/commit/push/reset/stash/clean/branch switch; no worktree; public/hot untouched; no unrelated UI change; no patient-identifying data printed by the command or written to new audit descriptions.
- Defense notes: the shared PregnancyHistoryAssociationService is the SINGLE implementation of both the match and the conflict doctrine, so account creation and reconciliation cannot drift (the alternative - two inline copies - was rejected for exactly that reason); passing the cast Carbon into where() mirrors pregnanciesFor byte-for-byte and was the fix for the one bug found in testing (toDateString() never matched SQLite's stored 'Y-m-d H:i:s' format, which surfaced as 18 failures and is documented here because the failure mode - NULL-safe pairs matching while non-NULL pairs silently miss - is precisely the kind of quiet under-association that must never reach production); whole-cluster refusal on any foreign ownership follows the continuation service's "never reassign, refuse loudly" doctrine rather than per-row skipping, so a mixed cluster always surfaces for human review; the mandate's two clauses - "automatically associate reliably established episodes" and "no demographic-only matching" - are reconciled by anchoring: the staff's explicit provisioning of ONE specific episode is the ownership evidence, and the four-field rule only extends that pairing within the person's own already-established identity cluster (the same rule the clinic's staff History panel has always used), never inventing a new account-to-person pairing.
- Known limitations: (1) accounts provisioned BEFORE Task B are invisible to creation-time expansion - only the reconciliation command (or a future provisioning event on the same person) can reach them, which is why the command exists and why its --apply must be run by a reviewed, backed-up operator (never executed in this sprint); (2) women whose entire history is delivered-only with no account anchor remain invisible until they return and an account is provisioned - association can only follow an explicit anchor, by design; (3) two different accounts CAN still be provisioned for the same four-field person on different episodes (pre-existing capability; the mandate scopes conflict refusal to candidates within one provisioning event, and mixed-pointer clusters currently number 0 locally); (4) matching remains exact-string, so a staff typo in a new episode's name fragments that episode from the cluster (safe failure: under-association, nothing leaks); (5) audit_logs.user_id is NOT NULL, so reconciliation attributes its per-link audit rows to the owning portal account (the affected party) rather than an invented staff actor - documented here as the deliberate console-context choice; (6) no browser click-through was performed (no browser automation may be installed) - all UI assertions are HTTP/markup level; (7) the reconciliation command's output deliberately omits even episode IDs per the counts-only mandate, so an operator investigating a refused cluster needs a separate read-only query rather than the command's own output.


## DEPLA Phase 1.5: Email-Only Patient Portal Account Setup (2026-10-15)

- Requirement (user-approved mandate): replace staff-entered initial password in Patient Portal account creation with email-only provisioning plus a strictly single-use, 24-hour, patient-chosen-password setup link. Admin/staff permissions unchanged. Ordinary Forgot Password keeps its 60-minute expiry. No plaintext password is ever returned, logged or emailed. No schema change, no migration.
